# vigia — Specification

Status: **v0, 2026-07-30.** Source of truth. Code is written against this document. See `CLAUDE.md` § Method for the drift rule.

## 0. How to read this document

Source of truth means: **what must hold now**. It does not mean every sentence is beyond question. The difference has cost real features more than once, so it is written down here where every reader passes.

- **A ruling is its reason plus a date.** The reason, not the sentence, is the authority. Before citing a ruling to refuse or constrain something, re-read the reason and check that it is still a fact. A citation without that check is only an opinion.
- **A refusal that names an absence expires fastest.** *"No API reports that"*, *"the takeover does not enable X"*, *"no font carries it"*. Dependencies add things, terminals add things, and nobody re-reads a refusal when they do. Two features here were declined for a year on absences that had stopped being true (§11.2 B10 is one). One more was declined on a fact about fonts that was quoted as a fact about rendering (§5.3, corrected by [#318](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/318)). Check the world, not the memory of it. Read the dependency's source in the registry cache, and search the web.
- **An invariant is load-bearing, and whether it reaches the case in front of you is a fresh question every time.** Quote the row's own words. I1's budget is *0 wakeups while idle*. Nothing a reader's hand is doing is idle.
- **A budget cited as a reason arrives with its current headroom** or it is not an argument. "This costs a wake" means nothing beside "2.4ms of a 16ms frame".
- **Research rows start from the world and then diff against this file**, not the other way round. Reading the rulings first anchors the survey to what was already decided, which is how a document that records yesterday's ceiling becomes tomorrow's.
- **When a reason collapses, the question reopens.** It does not get a fresh reason for the same conclusion ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123) is the case that taught this).
- **A ruling can be withdrawn, and `REVOCATIONS.md` is where that is said.** If a ruling is deleted silently, the next reader cannot tell a reversal from an accident. So a withdrawal keeps the old text verbatim and names who ended it.

---

## 1. Problem

Running a full git TUI in a pane beside an AI coding agent, to watch changes as they land, is the wrong tool. A multi-panel git client spends the pane on branches, commits, stashes and status. In a pane already halved against the agent, every panel is a few lines tall and names truncate.

What is wanted: **the live diff, fullscreen, auto-updating, scrollable, mouse-driven, and beautiful.** Nothing else.

## 2. Product class — the load-bearing decision

`vigia` is a **monitor**, not a reviewer. That single distinction generates every budget below, and the budgets are the product.

| | Reviewer | **Monitor (`vigia`)** |
|---|---|---|
| How it starts | Launched per review | Already open |
| Interaction | The point | Rare or never |
| Correct when untouched | No | **Required** |
| Runtime | Minutes | **Days** |
| Latency budget | Seconds | **A frame** |

`btop` is the reference for what monitor-class feels like: you read state from shape and colour, glance away, glance back, and never configure anything.

Design rationale beyond this section is recorded outside the codebase. Call `recall` when a decision needs it.

## 3. Invariants

Tests refer to these invariants by number. Each invariant has a test that fails when it is violated. An invariant without such a test does not count.

Budgets are **absolute**. Each one is set on its own terms, not relative to another tool.

| # | Invariant | Budget | How it is proven |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I1** | Redraw is **event-driven**, never driven by a timer that runs on its own. No filesystem event and no git index change means no work. See the I1 paragraph below. | **0 wakeups** while the history window is empty, the state of a monitor left open overnight. While the window holds a sample: **at most one wake per `HISTORY_SAMPLE`**, and at most `HISTORY_SAMPLES` wakes after any burst, because the window drains. While a file that moved is inside the settle margin: **one wake**, at the margin's end after the last move | CPU sampled over a 60s idle window, **entered with the history window already empty**. Assert no render calls. Each clock also has a structural gate that asserts the loop's wait has no timer when nothing is held: `nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` for the loop, `an_empty_window_and_nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` for the ageing clock, and `a_settled_worktree_and_nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` for the settle clock |
| **I2a** | **Re-diffing is incremental.** The frame path never re-diffs a file that did not change. | Re-diff cost is proportional to what changed, **not** to worktree size | Assert the re-diff count and byte count for a single-line edit, across **two** fixtures that differ only in changed-file count. One fixture cannot prove it: see §7 |
| **I2b** | **Re-highlighting is incremental.** Only changed hunks are re-parsed. | Re-parse cost is proportional to edit size, **not** to file size | Assert the re-parse count and byte count for a single-line edit, across **two** fixtures that differ only in file size. One fixture cannot prove it, for the same reason as I2a: see §7 |
| **I3** | **Resources stay flat over days.** RSS, file handles and temp files do not grow without bound. | **RSS drift < 5%** over 24h. **Zero** temp files retained | A soak drives synthetic edits through the whole pipeline and samples RSS a fixed number of times across the window. It runs on a schedule, not per commit. The scheduled window is shorter than 24h for a reason outside this project: see [`RULINGS.md`](RULINGS.md) and §7 |
| **I4** | **Streams, never buffers.** A frame **builds** only what it draws. Diffs, highlighting and reads of file *content* follow the window, never the worktree. See the I4 paragraph below for three exceptions. | **First paint < 100ms** on a 100k-line diff. **Counting the height < 16ms** on the same fixture | `criterion`, gated in CI. The counting bound is in `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs`. `what_a_row_exact_scrollbar_would_cost` records the measurement the third exception was admitted on |
| **I5** | **Correct with zero interaction.** The pane follows the newest change and scrolls to it, untouched. | — | Scripted edit sequence, snapshot of the frame, no input given |
| **I6** | **Legible at 40 columns.** No horizontal overflow, and no label truncated until it is useless. | — | Snapshots at 40, 80 and 120 columns. Structural gates in `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs` sweep every width from 1 to 120: no row over-occupies, no hint is cut in half, and every label that lost characters says so |
| **I7** | Startup to first paint is imperceptible. **The first frame draws the diff without colour.** No frame ever parses under a grammar that has not been compiled. | **< 50ms** to first paint, and **< 16ms** for the frame that meets a new grammar | `crates/vigia/tests/first_paint.rs` for the first paint, and `crates/vigia-core/tests/warm.rs` for the grammar rule. See the I7 paragraphs below |
| **I8** | The terminal is restored on **every exit the process can observe**. See the I8 paragraph below. | — | The takeover order and its exact inverse, the partial-failure unwinding, a panic-hook test, escape sequences checked against DEC's own numbers, and a two-process gate that signals a child and reads back what its guard wrote |
| **I9** | Steady-state frame time holds 60fps under continuous edits. | **< 16ms** p99 | Gated over the **frame path**, not the primitives: a settled frame, one line rewritten before each frame, every file materialised. The caller-side gate **paints as well as collects**, for the reason §7 gives. `criterion` tracks the same shape |
| **I10** | **Glanceability history is bounded.** Churn history has a fixed window and a fixed cap on tracked paths. A path that ages out of the window is dropped entirely. | **≤ 256 paths**, **≤ 120s**, whatever the session changed | Structural, in `crates/vigia-core/tests/history.rs`. A fixture records **10,000** distinct paths and asserts that the store sits *at* the cap and that eviction ran. A window of silence empties it. The soak asserts the same over the real process, and **refuses to assert** in a window that reached neither rule. It is not in `tests/budgets.rs`, whose gates are all ratios or durations. This one is a count |

A regression past any budget **fails the build.**

**I1 clocks.** A clock may run only while the thing that armed it is still on screen and still changing. It must meet three conditions, and nothing more is required. It does not start on its own. It does not outlive the thing that armed it. The idle path has no timer, by construction. Hold-to-repeat on the scrollbar's step buttons (§11.1) was the first clock to meet these conditions. It is an **instance of this rule, not an exception to it**. Any other clock bounded the same way is already allowed and needs no further ruling. **A sample inside the history window is one such clock.** This was ruled 2026-08-22 by [#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243). The window ages and empties `HISTORY_WINDOW` after the last write, and its clock stops then.

**I4 exceptions.** The first is the diff's **height**. It is counted for every changed file once per tick, without materialising any content. The second is a note on a file that is in both the staged and the unstaged run. The run it lands in is decided from both diffs, so the note builds a diff the window does not draw. The third is the set of facts a named row of §11.1's position list draws. That list covers a whole run the window never shows. It is **bounded because it is a frame kept for as long as the box is open**. So the walk is incremental, on the machinery I2a holds the pane's own frame to, not a fresh read of the worktree each wake. The third exception is admitted on its measurement and nothing else: a fresh frame costs **20.21ms** over two hundred changed files, against I9's 16ms, and a kept frame costs **5.93ms**. *Ruled 2026-09-11, session* ([#508](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/508)).

**I7 grammars.** A grammar's patterns compile on first use, at 74-362ms per grammar. A cold screenful costs **124 to 695ms** at the scale a frame pays it. These are Windows figures taken under `codegen-units = 1`. §6 records that setting as overstating what ships by roughly 6x on Windows and by nothing on Linux. That cost belongs on the frame after the first, so the first frame shows the diff, uncoloured, not a blank alternate screen. The same rule holds later in the session. A file in a language new to this process draws plain. The warmer compiles its grammar off the frame, and the wake that ends the warm is the redraw that colours the file. Without this rule, the cost lands mid-session on a frame nobody asked for ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)). This rule now does the work that §7's cold-path exclusion used to do.

**I7 tests.** `first_paint.rs` measures the **shell's** first paint, not the core's. It asserts that the body is full and that nothing was parsed, and only then checks the clock. `criterion` does **not** track this shape: `benches/engine.rs`'s `open_and_first_paint` builds no `Highlighter`, so it is the core-only measurement that §10 corrects. `warm.rs` has two tiers. The first is a wall clock against 16ms, seen failing at 93.73ms. The second is a structural pair that shows the cause: a cold grammar parses **zero** lines and draws plain, and the same hunk is coloured once a warm lands.

**I8 exits.** The covered exits are the quit key (Ctrl-C included), an error return, a panic under `panic = "abort"`, and an externally delivered termination. These are `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` or `SIGHUP` on Unix, and a `CTRL_C`, `CTRL_BREAK` or `CTRL_CLOSE` console event on Windows. **`SIGKILL` and `TerminateProcess` (`taskkill /F`) are outside it on both platforms**, because neither runs any code the process owns. They are the only exits that *no design* can reach. Four more cases are excluded by **choice**, not by the platform. `SIGQUIT` is excluded because whoever sends it wants its default dump. `CTRL_LOGOFF_EVENT` and `CTRL_SHUTDOWN_EVENT` are excluded because they reach a service, not a console application. **The second ask** takes the default disposition and restores nothing, because a process that was told once and is still running must stay killable. The **first** ask exits the way the quit key does: exit **0** and no message. §11.1 describes both asks.

> [!NOTE]
> **The measurement histories behind these rows are in [`RULINGS.md`](RULINGS.md)**
>
> - **I1** has a cost its row does not measure. `crossterm`'s mouse bundle sets `?1003h`, so pointer motion over the pane wakes the loop, and that cannot be reclaimed portably. **A batch that asks for nothing, moves no pointer mark and meets no deadline paints nothing**, so the wake is all it costs ([#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154)).
> - **I4** was **narrowed 2026-08-01**. A height is hunk boundaries and line counts, not content. That is the row's first exception, and it is why #49's refusal of a repository-wide total still holds for sums over content. The height walk became **incremental 2026-08-04** (#101). Since 2026-09-04, a file that moved inside the settle margin keeps its height until it settles. It is then read once, on a wake at the margin's end ([#412](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/412), [#425](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/425)). The numbers and three rejected alternatives are in the ledger.
> - **I2** is two numbers because re-diffing (`gix`, Phase 1) and re-highlighting (`syntect`, Phase 2) have different dependencies and phases. The 18.58ms-vs-3.27ms measurement that forced the split is in the ledger.
> - **I8** no longer names `SIGINT` for Ctrl-C, because raw mode makes Ctrl-C a key event, never a signal. The externally delivered `SIGINT` is **covered since 2026-08-08** ([#24](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/24)). It was taken for both tier-1 platforms in one issue, because the single-task version was Unix-only and [#16](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/16) had already rejected a guarantee that means different things on different targets. Measurement made that affordable: neither half adds a crate to any graph. The row's exclusions now read the same on both platforms.
> - **I3**'s scheduled soak runs 30 minutes a week on Linux over the 20 x 200 fixture. The fixture settles in about thirty seconds, so the window measures the plateau. *Ruled 2026-09-29, reader.* The full 24h runs by `workflow_dispatch` or locally. The sample count is fixed, so the statistic is computed the same way at any window.

## 4. Scope

**In:** working-tree diff (unstaged by default), where in the history the pane stands (§11.1), event-driven refresh, follow mode, scroll (keyboard + mouse wheel), syntax highlighting, per-file churn visualisation, a pinned file list above the diff (§11.1), a note on a diff line carried to the agent (§11.2 B21), responsive layout, theming.

**Out of v1, deliberately:** staging, committing, rebasing, AI features, remote operations. `ROADMAP.md` carries a reason for each, and the first three are reviewer-class: each costs an invariant if built.

**Deferred, not rejected:** multi-worktree view (several agent sessions at once, the strongest differentiator after glanceability, and the most btop-shaped), and Jujutsu and Sapling support.

## 5. The differentiator: glanceability

`btop`'s real achievement is that state is readable from **shape and colour** without reading text. `vigia`'s translation:

- per-file **churn sparkline**: change density over time
- a **heat strip** locating change within the file
- live **+/− counters**
- a **visual pulse** on what just changed

This is what makes it a monitor rather than a narrow diff view, and it is where design effort goes once the invariants hold.

### 5.1 What the README mockup commits to

`assets/preview.svg` is the most detailed design artifact in the project, it is **public**, and until now it was specified nowhere. **A picture in a public README is a specification whether or not it is written down.** This one implied a retained time series, a recency gradient, two status readouts and a keybinding, none of which appeared in the spec, the roadmap, or any issue.

Elements the four bullets above do **not** cover are marked **(unspecified)**. They are in the picture, so they are commitments. Every row below is now ruled and implemented. §11.1 carries what the shell does and this table carries what the picture asked for.

| Element in the mockup | What it needs |
|---|---|
| Header: `vigia · 3 changed` … `watching` | A **mode word**, so there is a set of modes, and a count. **Ruled: the mode set is two** (`watching` and `not watching`) ([#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)). These are the only two states that a shell that wakes on writes can tell apart, and anything finer is a duration. **The count sits on the left with the worktree name and reads `3 changed`, and nothing is ever drawn beside it** ([#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67)), **amended 2026-09-10 by [#507](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/507): §11.1's position token can come between them.** The ruling was written against the mode word on the count's *right*, which is untouched, and the branch has always been able to sit on its left. What the amendment buys is that a count drawn without the token saying what it is a count *of* reads as the working tree's, which is then the one thing it is not. *Ruled 2026-09-10, reader, asked which of the two gave way.* That is a layout ruling rather than a rewording. `watching · 3 files` reads as a verb with an object, naming a curated set that does not exist. Once separated, the count has nothing beside it for a participle to govern. **`changed` rather than `files` is the load-bearing half**, because this repository has more than three files in it. So the count must name the change rather than the things, and **`N binary` rides beside it** where the run holds files with no lines to give. **The right-hand side is the run's changed-line total, and the mode word draws where there is none** ([#472](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/472)). *Ruled 2026-09-09, reader.* Left counts files, right counts lines. See §11.1 for the ladder. |
| Per-file **sparkline** | A **retained time series per file**: samples of churn over a window, bucketed. This is the only unbounded state in the design and the one that I3 forbids growing. So the **window** and the **path cap** are part of the invariant. That is I10, a 120-second window capped at 256 paths, evicted by window and by least-recently-changed. **How finely the window is divided sits underneath I10 rather than inside it**, and it has moved three times. The store keeps 120 one-second samples per path. The drawn bucket count is a **width rung** of 24, 12 or 6 ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)), so a drawn bucket is ten seconds on every pane the picture is measured at, and a wider pane buys more of them. The measurement found that a finer *period* buys nothing and a finer *column count* buys the shape. Twelve buckets of five seconds draw fewer distinct heights than twelve of ten, while twenty-four of five draw more than either. **An empty bucket draws a track** ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)), so a file with no history draws a full one. The store is fed from the watch, so a file already modified when `vigia` started has no tick behind it. That is the *ordinary* first frame rather than an edge. The track is `_`, deliberately not the ramp's `▁`. A sparkline's channel is **height**, so if the ramp's floor were spent on "no data", one write and no writes would get the same shape. **A sample weighs the bytes a write moved, and a height is measured against `scale_of`** ([#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)). Counting files instead let a worktree where one file is saved repeatedly make itself the peak and draw every active column at full height. **The ramp is cyan, three stops** ([#196](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/196)). The picture ramps across five greens. It is right about the ramp and wrong about the hue, because green already means addition two rows down. See §11.1. |
| Per-file **heat strip** | Hunk line-ranges projected onto buckets across the file's length. So it needs the file's **total line count**, not only its diff. That count is free, see §5.2. **Twelve buckets at the picture's own width, from a source resolution of twenty-four** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)). §11.1 carries the rung ladder. An empty bucket is a **dark track** rather than a gap, so a drawn strip is always its full width and a reader can see how much of the file is untouched. The one exception is a file with no line diff at all. It draws no strip, and §11.1 carries why that is the opposite of the sparkline's rule. **A mixed bucket is yellow**, because every alternative paints it as pure, and separating addition from removal by position is the strip's whole job. **Intensity is three steps at a wide palette and two at sixteen colours**. `ansi` spells this out in its own fields rather than leaving the depth ladder to collapse by accident. **The twelve are drawn as twelve** ([#196](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/196)). The picture draws separate rects, and the shell drew `█` twelve times. On a cell grid that is one continuous bar, so two adjacent slices in one band read as a single wide one. *Where in this file the work is* is exactly the question that needs counting. |
| Per-file `+42 −7` | Covered. Per-file counters are free, because a file must be diffed to be drawn (§10). |
| A **dimmed row** (`Cargo.toml` renders fainter than the rows above it) | A **recency gradient**: rows fade as their last change ages. **Ruled, session; three rungs of one ladder**: pulse, live and cold, read from I10's store. Sixteen foreground-only colours have three intensities to spend, so the gradient is a ramp of three rather than a fade. **A wider palette does not bring a fourth**, because the rung count belongs to `Recency` in `vigia-core`, whose `cold` means *untracked* rather than *old*. So a better palette draws the same three rungs in better colours. A real fade needs the store to emit an age fraction, which is a core change with an I10 budget on it. See §11.1. |
| `●` on the diff header | The pulse, drawn as a **persisting mark**, not a flash. **What it persists for was corrected 2026-08-26** ([#345](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/345)), reported from the pane. It stays on the newest burst's last write until another write arrives, or until I10's own bound retires the track. Ageing the churn window gave the recency a second term on a complaint about a settled row's *brightness*, and the mark went with the ink because one value answered both questions. It is a second predicate over the same store now. The ladder answers *how brightly* and this answers *which file last*. It is deliberately **not** a duration, because a wall-clock decay cannot be drawn without the timer that I1 forbids. It marks the newest burst's last write, the file follow moves to, and the rest of that burst keep the ladder's top rung. *Ruled 2026-09-29, session* ([#362](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/362)). **The `just changed` label is gone, from the picture as well as the shell**. **A label is not free on a monitor**: text has to be *read*, and §5 puts the whole differentiator in what can be taken from shape and colour without reading. Fourteen columns of the label sat in the widest rung, so below sixty-five columns the picture and the shell disagreed about what the pulse even was. |
| Diff body: **syntax highlighted content**, five classes deep (`kw`, `fnn`, `typ`, `var`, `con`) over a default foreground | I2b, and a **class set** rather than a palette: what the picture commits to is which distinctions are worth a colour, not which colour each one gets. The engine emits meanings and the shell colours them. See §11.1. |
| A **tinted row** with a coloured left bar on every added and removed line | A per-row **background**. It is what separates a changed line from a context line in the picture, because the text is highlighted identically on both. It does the same work as the dimmed row: it is how the eye finds what moved without reading. The wash is painted across the whole row, gutter and trailing blanks included, so it reads as a band rather than as a highlight behind some text. It is **not drawable below 24-bit**, where a quantised background is a solid block, and a block behind highlighted code destroys the colours on it. **The left bar is the pane's leading cell, background only, and it costs no column** ([#218](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/218)). It is one cell of the blank margin that [#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)'s ladder keeps, so it is a **width rung**: drawn from forty-three columns up, absent below. **It reaches `ansi` and every depth, where the wash reaches neither**, and that asymmetry is the ruling. A wash must be a **tint**, which cannot be chosen without knowing the background. A bar is a **saturated block on a blank column**, which needs no such knowledge. It was refused before on *the bar has no terminal equivalent that does not spend a whole column*. That was true while the pane drew from column zero. It was false once #119 gave the pane a margin, and nobody re-read it. |
| Status bar `0.8ms frame` | Instrumenting the render path and drawing the result. This is self-referential: measuring and painting the number costs frame time that I9 gates. **Ruled, session; the p99 of the last 128 completed frames, nearest-rank, sampled every frame** ([#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41)). The **statistic is p99 because I9 is**, and a readout reporting a median would be silent about exactly the frames the budget exists to bound. **128 because §7 already says why not 30**: at 30 a nearest-rank p99 is just the maximum, and at 128 it is rank 127, so one cold outlier is excluded and two are not. **A frame is the whole turn of the loop**: the wake, the drain, every `Frame::advance` in the batch, `View::collect` and the paint. That is §7's whole-frame rule applied to a readout. The number drawn is necessarily the previous frames', because a frame cannot include its own paint in what that paint draws. |
| Status bar `11MB` | A live RSS readout. I3 samples RSS in a **soak test**, never on screen. Reading it per frame is a syscall on some platforms. **Ruled, session; once per painted frame, on all three tier-1 targets, drawn in `MiB`** ([#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41)). **The read is a syscall everywhere it ships**, which is not what the soak's own helper suggested. `soak.rs` spawns `tasklist` on Windows, which is **42.8ms median** against the 16ms I9 budget. What makes it affordable on screen is that both platforms have an in-process answer through a crate that `gix` **already** puts in the graph: `libc::proc_pidinfo` on macOS and `windows-sys`' `GetProcessMemoryInfo` on Windows, against `/proc/self/status` on Linux. **`MiB` rather than the mockup's `MB`**, because I3's soak quotes this quantity in MiB throughout, and with the mockup's unit a reader who compares screen against soak would read the 4.9% difference as drift. `assets/preview.svg` was corrected to match. **And the number is "as of the last change", not "now"**. This shell wakes only on a filesystem event, so a pane left open on an idle tree shows the RSS from whenever the last write landed. That is consistent rather than broken: the diff on screen is *also* as of the last change. |
| Status bar `follow ▶` | A follow-state indicator, which presumes the mode exists. **Landed with I5**, on the footer rather than a third chrome line. I6 later gives it a line of its own, above the hints, at the widths where one line cannot hold both. See §11.1. |
| Key hints `q quit · f follow · ↑↓ scroll` | A hint bar, and it **constrains I6**: roughly thirty columns of it must degrade legibly at forty. **Ruled 2026-07-30, session: it does not degrade by shortening.** The footer takes a second line instead. Only below the width where a whole line holds the bar does it drop hints, `JK files` first and then `jk scroll`. |

Three of these are corrections rather than gaps:

1. **`f` toggles follow.** The mockup shows a dedicated key and a state indicator. That is the answer to §11.2 B1, published before the question was asked.
2. **The dimmed row and the pulse are one mechanism**, not two: both are recency rendered as intensity. If they were specified separately, they would produce two decay clocks that disagree on screen.
3. **The table above is a list of elements and was silent about the container**. That let B4 sit `(proposed)` for two phases while the picture and the code drew different screens. The file list is a **region**, pinned above the diff ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66)). Every row in this table is unchanged by that. What changed is where three of them are drawn and for how long they stay there.

#### The departure ledger

A departure is a decision somebody made for the shell against the picture. **One is open: the header draws the worktree name where the picture draws `vigia`.** The rest are closed. How each closed is the record worth keeping, because §5.3's law is that a published artifact answering an open question *is* the answer and does not outrank a decision made against an invariant.

- **The element split across the two regions**: closed 2026-08-17 by correcting the **picture**. It gave the diff's heading no kind letter, no counters, no heat strip and no sparkline, so each region drew a different subset. That is coherent only while the pinned region shows every changed file, and an automatic unbounded changed set means it cannot. If a file were to scroll out of a capped region, it would take its counters with it, and nowhere would be left to read them. Both regions draw the same row through the same `Painter::file_row`, which also leaves one degradation ladder to gate rather than two.
- **The right-hand order**: closed 2026-08-17 by redrawing the **picture**. `Painter::file_row` allocates right to left by priority, so the drawn order is pulse, heat strip, sparkline, counters. Allocating by priority is what lets the ladder drop the least valuable element first. The picture places every element where the binary places it, on the ground that a specification nobody can check against the thing it specifies is not one. §5.3's rule that the mockup is corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws is what keeps that true.
- **The columnar row**: closed 2026-08-02 towards the **picture**. Every file row in `assets/preview.svg` puts the same element at the same `x`, so three sparklines read as one small-multiples chart and three heat strips as a comparison. That is the whole of what a file *list* is for. `Painter::file_row` right-packed instead, so every element's position was a function of the widths of the ones to its right and no two rows agreed. §11.1 carries the rule and what it costs.
- **The sigil's standoff**: closed 2026-08-15 towards the **picture**, by its own coordinates rather than by argument. The sigil sits at `x=72`, one cell of `~8.1px` ends it at `80.1`, and every content origin is `x=88` ([#164](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/164)). A reader watching the pane found it.

Three things that look like departures and are not:

- **The staged run and its mark** ([#313](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/313)). The toggle is off by default, so the mockup and the shipped default pane are the same screen. It is a departure only if the picture draws one thing while an unpressed pane draws another.
- **The left rail** ([#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252), [#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295)). `assets/preview.svg` is a **109-column** render, below the rail's 139. Since B14 made the rail a gesture, the stacked layout is what ships at every width until a reader asks otherwise. `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_grows_with_the_pane_and_keeps_the_pictured_complement` asserts the glance complement against a rung read off that render rather than restated.
- **A picture that is simply behind.** Two were found in one pass. The status bar drew a hint bar that [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80) already replaced. The state cell drew the follow marker without the `N/M` position that `state_rungs` has drawn since the pinned region existed. **A departure is a decision somebody made. This is a picture nobody opened since the ruling that changed what it needed to say.** §5.3's *"corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws"* is the rule that was missed. Missing it is silent, which is why the audit that found these was of the artifact rather than of any gate.

**Two claims about the picture in this section have themselves been wrong, both found by opening the file** ([#156](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/156) is that gap, open). One said the mockup gives the summary rows no kind letter, but the mockup has drawn `M` at `x=32` since its first commit. Nothing downstream was able to tell, because a claim about a picture is checked by opening the picture, and the sentence read like a description of one.

**And the picture states one colour rule nobody had read off it** ([#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157)). It draws `+42` in `.grn` and `-7` in `.red` on its first two rows and `-0` in **`.faint`** on its third. So the rule is *value-dependent*: a half takes its diff colour where it has something to say, and the row's dim grey where it does not. §5.3 derives the same answer without the picture, since green and red are lent only where they restate the same fact and a `-0` restates none. The reading it protects is the point. On a worktree an agent is only adding to, red drawn unconditionally would put red on every row, and *is anything being removed* would have no answer left on screen. The two halves are still drawn or dropped **together**, because `+42` with no `-7` beside it reads as a total rather than as half a pair. Only their ink is per half. What is deliberately not settled is which grey.

### 5.2 Where the mockup pulls against the invariants

§5 says design effort goes here "once the invariants hold", and [#10](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/10) repeats it: *"depends on the invariants holding first."* **That framing is wrong, and correcting it is the most consequential thing in this section.** At least two elements need retained state that the frame path does not produce. That state belongs in `vigia-core`, not in the shell. The elements do not sit on top of the invariants. They **move** them.

**The sparkline needs precisely what eviction throws away.** `FrameStats.evicted` exists so the cached-diff map stays "bounded by the current diff rather than by everything ever edited". That is how I3 is argued today. A churn sparkline is change density *over time*, so it has to survive a file settling. A sparkline that empties the moment a file stops changing shows nothing worth glancing at, and *"what was hot thirty seconds ago"* is the entire question it answers. Glanceability history therefore cannot live in the evicting map, and it must not be unbounded either.

> [!NOTE]
> **I10 earned its row on 2026-07-31**
>
> It was written here as a **proposal**, deliberately kept out of the §3 table. That table is for invariants with a failing test, and this proposal had none. It now has a budget (256 paths, 120 seconds), a fixture that drives ten thousand paths through it, and a soak assertion over the real process, so it has moved up. [#38](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/38).
>
> It cost two things that this section did not predict. First, the store had to be fed from the **watch** rather than from the frame path. So `Tick` now carries every file a burst wrote instead of only the last. The list is capped at the same 256, so a bulk operation cannot make a tick expensive before the store gets a chance to bound it. Second, the decay that the mockup asks for turned out to be an I1 question rather than a rendering one: see §11.1.
>
> What it got right is the sentence that everything else followed from. **The data structure was the decision and the drawing was the easy part.**

**The heat strip needs a whole-file property.** To locate change within a file, the strip needs that file's **total line count**. The frame path is built to avoid exactly that: pure revalidation reads **0 bytes** (§10), the number I2a is written against. If the count is measured naively, for every changed file on every frame, it reintroduces the read that I2a removed. The count can be cached per `(path, blob id)`, because a file's length cannot change without its content changing. It is then paid once per version rather than once per frame. **That caching is not an optimisation. Without it the heat strip breaks I2a.**

> [!NOTE]
> **The cache above was never needed, and the reason is worth more than the prediction**
>
> **Corrected 2026-07-31 by building it.** [#39](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/39). The paragraph above is right that a whole-file read would break I2a. It is wrong that a new cache is what avoids the read. `hunk::compute` interns **both sides** to diff them at all. So the line count of the working-tree side is already computed on every diff, and the code was throwing it away. It is now a field of `FileDiff`, which means it is cached and invalidated with the diff itself.
>
> That is **stricter** than the `(path, blob id)` key proposed here, not merely cheaper. A blob id names the index side, and a working-tree edit does not touch it. Had the cache been keyed that way, it would have served a stale length for exactly the file that a reader is watching being written. `tests/frame.rs` gates the difference with an edit that keeps the byte length of a file and changes its line count.
>
> The general shape, and this is the third time this project has hit it: **the expensive-looking property was a by-product of work already being done.** I5 found the follow target already resolved by the gitignore filter. I10 found the paths of the burst already resolved by the same filter. This one found the line count already interned by the differ. Look for the by-product before you design the cache.

**Two status readouts measure the thing they run inside.** `0.8ms frame` means instrumenting the render path and drawing the result. The cost of the readout falls inside the budget that it reports, and I9 gates that budget at 16ms p99. `11MB` is a live RSS number. I3 samples RSS in a **soak test** precisely because reading it is a syscall on some platforms rather than free per frame. Both are honest to show. Neither is free to show. The spec said nothing about either.

**Consequence for sequencing:** Phase 3 is not a rendering phase. Its two headline elements each need a core-side change with an invariant attached. They belong in the same conversation as I2a and I3, not strictly after them.

**Confirmed by building the first one.** [#38](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/38) landed the sparkline, the recency gradient and the pulse. The diff was mostly `vigia-core`: a new bounded store, a change to what a `Tick` carries, a new invariant with its own budget, and a soak assertion. The drawing was a bucket ladder and a three-step ramp. This section predicted that split and it was right.

### 5.3 The design language


The principles that the individual rulings keep deriving, stated once so that a new question starts here instead of re-arguing an old answer. Each names where it already bit. The laws themselves live where they always did (§5.1 for the picture, §11.1 for behaviour) and are not restated.


**The picture binds the elements. The snapshots are what ships. The distance between them is the work.** There is one canonical mockup. It is corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws. It is never redrawn ahead of a ruling, because a picture that answers an open question *is* the answer and must not arrive by ambush (B4). It is never left behind a ruling either, because that is drift. But the mockup is rendered in pixels and a cell grid can only approximate it. What the mockup specifies is the **element set and each element's promises**, never glyph-for-glyph fidelity. Suppose every promised element is present and the pane still reads far from the picture. The gap is then not a defect in either artifact. It is the design backlog, itemised: spacing, colour weight, composition, density. The snapshot suite carries the rendered truth at every width and state, which one picture cannot.


**Furniture runs full-bleed. Text is inset.** The mockup draws washes and rules to the pane edge and glyphs one to three cells in. The two roles must not swap. A wash that stops short reads as a misaligned highlight, and text at column 0 reads as squeezed. *Ruled 2026-08-10, session* ([#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)). §11.1 carries the ladder and the floor that keeps it clear of I6's forty columns. The build added one point: a margin has to be laddered as a **total** rather than per side, because stepping both sides on one column hands a widening pane a narrower row. Chrome is the third role and never grows for a transient thing.


**Furniture spans the region that drew it, not the screen.** The law above says a wash runs full-bleed. This law says *full-bleed to what*. Until 2026-08-23 the two readings were the same sentence, because every region spanned the pane. [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) put the list beside the diff. The reading that had never had to be chosen became the only one that draws, because a wash that reached the pane's edge from a region two thirds of the way across the pane would paint over the rail. `render` kept the seam open for three phases on purpose, rather than collapsing two numbers that happened to be equal.


**An act answers in one frame, and a change lands still.** **Anything the reader does lands whole, immediately**: a click, a drag, a digit, a toggle. Easing a response tells a reader that the tool is still working, and this tool's claim is that it already finished. **A change the agent writes is not drawn arriving.** An agent that wrote line after line kept the diff blinking, and the pulse and the heat already say where writes landed. *Ruled 2026-09-28, reader.* **The footer's own line is outside this**, *ruled 2026-09-04, reader*. Every message on it arrives and leaves, a receipt for the reader's own act included, and each of the three does so in its own way. The line is chrome rather than content. A message that appeared between two frames was reported from the pane as reading like a glitch.

**What licenses it is I1's clock rule, not an exception to it.** The old ruling argued that animation needs a clock and that a clock is the timer I1 forbids. `RULINGS.md` had already corrected that reasoning for the step-button repeat, and the correction had not been carried across. I1's measure is **0 wakeups while idle**, and a clock that cannot start on its own does not touch it. So an effect is allowed on exactly the three conditions that I1 already states: **an event that had already woken the loop arms it, it must not outlive what armed it, and it must not re-arm itself.** It holds I9's 16ms like any other frame. A quiet tree still costs nothing, which is the whole of what I1 protects. The same reasoning retired the pulse decay and the frozen-clock header words: **anything drawn as *elapsing* lies the moment nothing wakes.**


**Acknowledgment before action, priced honestly.** Every act that the shell accepts answers on the same frame: a click lands, a drag tracks, a digit teleports. Extending that to *intent* (hover) was ruled on rather than accreted ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123), §11.2 B10). It was declined, then reversed 2026-08-16. §11.1 carries the behaviour and the clearing ladder.


**And prefer the claim that has no tense.** *"`TAKEOVER` does not request focus reporting today"* was true when written and false when the build merged. The correction to *"requests it today"* was the same shape pointed the other way. *"`?1004h` is one step `TAKEOVER` can take"* was true before the build and after it. A sentence about what the code does **now** belongs in §11.1, which a build already has to update.


**The principle the reversal produced**: a mark about an **input device** is licensed inside a body region on a surface that the pointer of the reader can act on, and it must be the quietest thing still visible in that region. The quietness is not decoration. It is what pays for a mark that can go stale: a glance has to reach the worktree first and the pointer never.


**The general shape**: a feel improvement that costs an invariant a sentence gets a decision issue. One that costs nothing gets a row.


**Colour is spent by role, and the diff's is inviolable.** Green and red mean added and removed. They must never collapse at any rung that has colour (the one absolute of the depth ladder). They are loaned out only where they restate the same fact: the follow marker of the footer, the counters. **A loan is per statement rather than per element**, which is what "the same fact" means when read strictly. A `-0` on a file row is the element of the counters and is not the fact, so it keeps the dim grey while the `+2` beside it takes green ([#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157)). Cyan marks structure and instrument. Intensity is the recency ladder and nothing else: dim is a *meaning*, not a style. **A new element gets a colour only by taking a role, never by being distinct.**


**A pointer mark is the one case that is not durable** ([#189](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/189)), so it can take a weight of its own. It is not a claim about the worktree, and it is gone the moment the pointer moves. **It takes the pointer's own colour**, `Theme::bar_hover`'s, so one reading means *the pointer is here* on a step button, a thumb and a listed path alike. **It underlines**, which nothing else in these palettes does. That modifier was always the channel that carries the meaning, and the brightness never did the work. On `ansi`, the foreground of a hovered path equals that of `Theme::path_cold` outright, because sixteen names hold nothing between colour 8 and `Gray`. The mark sits **under** all three recency weights, not above them. A hover can go stale and a recency cannot. A mark that outranks every claim about the worktree is read first and is the one most likely to be wrong. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::a_hovered_row_reads_as_the_pointer_and_never_as_recency` and `::the_pointer_reads_the_same_colour_wherever_it_rests` are what fail if either stops being true.


**And a second bounded exception, the durable one**: the row that the caret marks draws its **path bold**, on top of whatever recency gave it. That is not a third meaning for intensity. It is the second channel of the caret: `▸` and the weight are one statement said twice. `Painter::list` resolves both from one predicate, so they name the same row or neither is drawn. The tie is `affords_caret`. Below seventeen columns the caret is dropped and the weight goes with it, because a bold row with nothing pointing at it would be a weight that a reader cannot attribute. The weight shares `BOLD` with the pulse rung. That is not a collision, because both readings carry a glyph of their own, and glyphs are what survives `NO_COLOR`. **Why the region needed it**: every other durable distinction there is carried by weight, and the one row that a reader most needs to find carried a glyph alone. The two exceptions are orthogonal rather than ranked, so a hovered current row reads as both. Colour and underline are the pointer, and weight is the file that the diff is inside. Ranking them would take one of those facts off the screen.


**Green is loaned a third time, to the staged mark** ([#313](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/313)). *Staged* is a different fact from *added*, so this is not the same-fact loan above. It is taken on evidence rather than on room: **`git status` paints a staged path green and an unstaged one red**. So a reader who has used git already knows this mark, and any other colour chosen here would be a second vocabulary for a distinction that git already spells. Inheriting one beats inventing one, which is why `SHEET_CLOSE` and the glyphs of the scrollbar are what they are.


**What keeps the diff's own green unambiguous is *where* the mark is drawn, and that is the bound on the loan.** It inks the kind letter, on file rows only. So it is never on a content row and never beside a `+` sigil or a `+42` counter. A reader who has not asked for the staged run sees no green in the list at all. **The ladder is untouched and so is the channel of the pointer.** The mark takes no intensity, so recency still owns weight. It underlines nothing, so hover still owns that. **What it does not do is survive `NO_COLOR`** ([#316](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/316)). There the run separators carry the fact alone. That is the price of the column, paid only by a reader who has both asked for the staged run and turned colour off. `render.rs::the_mark_takes_the_staged_colour_and_never_the_diffs_green` is what fails if the two greens are collapsed into one theme key. They are the same hue today and they are different roles.


**The column is what a glyph cost.** The mark was a `│` in a gutter column of its own until 2026-08-25. It had to be taken on *every* row of *both* runs, because a mark that appeared per row would slide the paths beside it one cell sideways at each run boundary. So the pane spent a column of the filename, its most contested element, everywhere at once, to mark the rows of one run. Colour says the same thing for nothing. **A background block is refused by the depth ladder**: the quantiser of §5.1 drops `bg` at every rung below `Truecolor`, and `Ansi16` is the default. A mark drawn as a background block would be invisible for most readers rather than merely plainer. Foreground survives every rung that has colour at all.


**An overlay was asked for first and was checked before it was refused.** `U+20D2 COMBINING LONG VERTICAL LINE OVERLAY` composes correctly. `ratatui` segments by extended grapheme cluster, a `Cell` holds a string rather than a `char`, and `"M\u{20d2}"` measures one grapheme of width one, so using it would move no row arithmetic. `Cell::merge_symbol` is not a route either. It is box drawing only, and `MergeStrategy::Exact` falls back to `Replace` on a non-box base, and that fallback would eat the letter. The overlay was refused on **font coverage**, and **that half was corrected on 2026-08-25** ([#318](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/318)). Every terminal in the 2026 matrix resolves a glyph that its configured font lacks from the other fonts of the system by default. So the same per-terminal table that decides the rungs of `Depth` and `Glyphs` can decide this one. **What that does not reopen is the shipped mark**: ink needs no vocabulary at all and costs nothing anywhere. What it reopens is the vocabulary itself ([#324](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/324)). Overlays, sextants and octants are per-terminal capabilities now rather than per-font gambles.


**A union is what makes any of this necessary, and a swap would have needed none of it.** Showing one comparison at a time spends no channel at all, because every row on screen is the same kind and the header can say which. It was the shape that this was first planned as, and the reader ruled against it. The reader's ground was that neither view alone answers *what has the agent done*. The second run costs a column of every path and one accent. What it buys is a pane that never goes blank while there is uncommitted work in the tree.


**Shape carries signal. Words are read.** The five glance elements are shape-and-colour precisely so the eye takes them without reading (§5). That is why the pulse lost its label and why a hint is dropped whole rather than abbreviated: half a word is a *reading* task on a surface built to avoid one. Text on screen is either content, a name, or advice, and each has its own degradation law.


**Richness is the reward of space.** The invariants price wakes, writes and clocks. They price **ink at zero**: borders, dense graphs and colour ramps all draw on frames that already happen. So a monitor can be the best-looking thing in the terminal while staying the cheapest. The ladder therefore runs in both directions. Below I6's floor the pane is spartan because every cell is contested. Upward, each rung *earns* more of the cockpit. What stays refused is only what the invariants actually name: easing (a clock), decoration that must be configured (B6), and any element whose information is not already retained. **An earlier draft refused borders and meters on taste. That was the dial of the author, not of the spec.** The correction is recorded so the next reader knows which kind of rule this is.

## 6. Architecture

Cargo workspace, two crates:

- **`vigia-core`**: library. Git (`gix`), diff modelling, incremental highlighting (`syntect`), filesystem events (`notify`), the watch and coalesce engine, the frame path. **No terminal I/O, no ratatui.** Every invariant except I6 and I8 is testable here, headlessly.
- **`vigia`**: the `ratatui` + `crossterm` shell: input, layout, theming. Thin by design, so the TUI stays swappable and the engine stays provable. It is a library with a five-line binary on top rather than a binary alone. This is because §7 makes the snapshot suite the proof for I5 and I6, and a test cannot import a `main.rs`.

The split is a dependency decision, not a hedge. The TUI renders whatever the core produces, so the core has to work first or the TUI is built on sand.

### Dependencies, and what each one costs

**`CLAUDE.md`'s rule is that a dependency reaches the spec before it reaches a manifest**, so every direct one is named below with what it buys and what it adds to the graph. **The cost of a dependency is a fact about the lock file and is one command away.** `windows-sys` was assessed from memory as *"a new crate in the graph"*, and the Windows memory readout was filed as deferred work ([#56](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/56)) on the strength of that assessment. A sentence in this section had said otherwise since Phase 1.

**`notify`** is named because I1 requires filesystem events rather than a timer, and each platform delivers them differently: `inotify` on Linux, `FSEvents` on macOS, `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on Windows. It is the standard Rust abstraction over those three and it keeps the binary pure Rust. Verified against all three tier-1 targets: no `cc`, `cmake` or `bindgen` in any graph. The `-sys` crates it pulls are FFI declarations against facilities that the OS already ships, so they compile no C.

**`gix`'s `revision` feature** is named for the position of §11.1. It provides `Repository::merge_base`, which resolves the point where a branch left the one it tracks. It also provides `rev_walk`, the lazy commit iterator that the list behind the token pages against. It is a **feature rather than a crate**. `gix-revision` is already compiled beneath `status`, so turning the API on adds **zero crates and no `cc`**. This is measured at 213 either way over `cargo tree -e normal`, not over the lock. The laziness of the walk is a fact rather than a promise: a probe stopped it after three commits without draining it. That is what lets a history longer than a screen cost a screenful.

**`fancy-regex`** is named for the `hide` pattern of §11.2 B6. It is the engine that this binary already carries rather than a second one. `syntect`'s `regex-fancy` below puts it in the normal graph, so declaring it directly adds **zero crates and zero features**. This is measured over `cargo tree -e normal` rather than over `Cargo.lock`. That distinction is the whole reason it is this crate and not `regex`, which looks equally free and is not. `regex` reaches the lock only as a dev-dependency of `criterion`, so taking it would add a crate to the shipped binary for a job that the graph can already do. A pattern with no backreference and no look-around wraps `regex-automata` and matches in linear time. A pattern with either runs a backtracking VM under a 1,000,000-step limit. The VM returns an error rather than hanging, and a pattern that errors hides nothing. MIT.

**`tachyonfx`** is named for the motions of the pane: the note box, the rows of a note, the messages of the footer and the word of the menu. It sits under the `ratatui` organisation rather than beside it, which is the provenance that this stack already accepts for the shell itself. **It schedules nothing**, which is the whole reason I1 is untouched. `Shader::process` takes the elapsed duration as an argument and `done` reports completion, so the clock stays this program's and stays bounded by the tick that armed it. Measured rather than assessed, on this section's own rule: it adds **`bon` and `micromath`**, plus `bon-macros` and `prettyplease` as build-time proc macros. It adds nothing else, because `compact_str` and `unicode-width` are already in the graph beneath `ratatui-core` and `thiserror` is already beneath `gix`. The `dsl` feature is taken and adds **`anpa`**, which has no dependencies of its own and no build script. It is MIT and Apache, with an MSRV of 1.82 under the 1.89 of this workspace. `tachyonfx` has no `cc` and no `-sys`, and it is MIT. **Its MSRV is unstated.** Nothing here gates that, and it is recorded rather than found later. **The motions of the pane are written in that DSL**: a motion is a source and the bindings that its names take, the crate composes and times what it builds, and `Timed` retires an effect on the length that the effect reports. It takes 6.3us to register the compilers and 12.7us to compile a source. Both are paid when a motion is armed, which is a press or a wake and never a frame. A source that will not compile draws nothing and the surface under it stands, because this workspace aborts on a panic. Every source ships gated by a suite that compiles it. *Ruled 2026-09-07, reader.*

**`ratatui-textarea`** is named for the note box of §11.2 B21. It provides the text model, the caret, the editing keys and undo. The shell draws the cells itself, so the box wraps by the rule of the note rows and the widget half of the crate goes unused. It is under the `ratatui` organisation, MIT, MSRV 1.86. `default-features = false` with `crossterm` alone leaves `search` and `regex` behind. **Measured 2026-09-06 over the build graph, which is not the lock file: it adds six crates on Windows and seven on musl.** These are the editor itself and `time` with `time-core`, `deranged`, `num-conv`, `powerfmt` and, on unix, `num_threads`. The cause is that it declares `ratatui-widgets` with the defaults of that crate, `calendar` among them, and feature unification is additive. The `time` tree that the `ratatui` pin of the shell itself had kept out by taking no widgets arrives through the editor, and nothing on this side can send it back. Every one but the editor itself was already in `Cargo.lock`, as an optional dependency that nothing enabled. That is what B21's *one crate* counted, and it is why that number was true of the lock and false of the binary. None of them has a `build.rs` or a `cc`, and the `no C toolchain` job holds that.

**`rustix`** is named for exactly one act: the startup background query ([#325](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/325), §11.2 B18). Before the event reader exists, the shell asks the terminal for its background colour (OSC 11), and for `system` its palette too, and waits a short time at most for the answer. Waiting on a tty without burning a thread needs one `poll`. It was already in this workspace's graph beneath `crossterm` and `notify`. The alternative, hand-rolled `libc` FFI, is the same dependency with less checking. Unix only. Windows sends no query.

**`ureq`, `rustls-graviola` and `serde_json`** are named for the startup update check (§11.1), the only request that this program makes, one per process. `ureq` blocks, so the check is a thread rather than a runtime. `serde_json` reads one field out of the answer. `serde` and `memchr` are already here beneath `syntect`, so that crate costs two crates. Measured, not assessed, and over the **build graph** rather than the lock: **190 unique crates to 208 on musl and 191 to 209 on Windows**, with no `cc`, `cmake` or `bindgen` on any of the four targets of §9. `Cargo.lock` carries `ring` and `cc` once each, behind an optional `rustls-webpki` feature that nothing enables, and `cargo tree -i ring` reaches neither. `gzip` adds no crate, since `flate2` and `miniz_oxide` arrive with the grammar dump. It takes the transfer from 71,166 bytes to 10,002.

**The provider is where the pure-Rust rule bites, and one combination survives it.** `rustls` defaults to `aws-lc-rs`, which is `cmake`. The `rustls` feature of `ureq` itself is `ring`, which is `cc`. `rustls-no-provider` with [`rustls-graviola`](https://github.com/ctz/graviola/) compiles nothing but Rust. It is written by the author of `rustls` under the same repository as `graviola`, which is the provenance that this stack already accepts for `tachyonfx`. The alternative, `rustls-rustcrypto`, is alpha and carries an advisory. **Two of the defaults of `ureq` are wrong for one fixed endpoint**, and they are set rather than inherited. First, it follows ten redirects with `https_only` off, so a redirect can carry the check to cleartext. Second, its body cap counts *compressed* bytes, so a small gzip answer can decompress without bound.

**And it costs two guards, both about a crash rather than a feature.** `graviola` **asserts** on six x86_64 CPU features (`aes`, `pclmulqdq`, `bmi1`, `adx`, `avx`, `avx2`) and four on aarch64. Its own source puts these at processors after roughly 2013. Under `panic = "abort"` that assert kills the monitor rather than the request, so the same features are tested through `std::arch` first. It is also a `compile_error!` elsewhere, so both crates are **target-conditional**. An armv7 or riscv64 `cargo install` still compiles, and the check is not in it. **Its MSRV is 1.89**, which is why the MSRV of this workspace is the same.

**`libc` on macOS and `windows-sys` on Windows** are named for the memory readout of §5.1. Both are **target-conditional dependencies of `vigia` alone**. Both are `unsafe` FFI declarations against facilities that the OS already ships. **Neither adds a crate to any graph**: `gix` already puts `libc` into the Apple graph through `gix-hash` → `sha1-checked` → `cpufeatures`, and `windows-sys` 0.61 into the Windows graph through `gix-sec`. What each buys is one syscall in place of a **subprocess**. `soak.rs` reaches the same numbers by spawning `ps` and `tasklist`. That is affordable 288 times across an hour and not sixty times a second, since `tasklist` alone is 42.8ms median against a 16ms frame. Linux needs neither, since `/proc/self/status` is a file read.

**Both are `dev-dependencies` of both crates as well, for the CPU clock of the budget gates** ([#212](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/212)). The absolute tier of §7 attributes a wall-clock overshoot to the host or to us by reading thread CPU time beside it. `std` exposes no CPU clock on any platform, and this time Linux needs one too: `clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` on every unix, `GetThreadTimes` on Windows. **The distinction that keeps it cheap is `dev`.** A dev-dependency is absent from the graph of the published crate and from the shipped binary. The purity gate walks `-e normal,build`, so the no-C-toolchain rule of §6 is not even in play.

**`criterion` and `insta`** are dev-dependencies for §7: the benches and the snapshot suite. Neither reaches the shipped binary. `package.rs::dependencies_in_spec` fails when a direct dependency is not named in this section.

**`signal-hook` on Unix and `windows-sys`'s `Win32_System_Console` on Windows** are named for the externally delivered signal of I8. They are one decision rather than two. `std` has no signal API and the mechanism differs by platform. The alternative to naming both is the platform-asymmetric guarantee that [#16](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/16) already rejected as worse than one stated uniformly. Both are target-conditional dependencies of `vigia` alone, and **neither adds a crate to any graph**. `signal-hook` is already present at 0.3.18 on Linux and Apple, pulled by `crossterm` through `ratatui`. `Win32_System_Console` is a feature of a crate that `vigia` already declares. Measured 2026-08-08, unique crates before and after: 183 Linux, 181 Apple, 185 Windows, unchanged.

**`signal-hook` is the one dependency here where pure Rust had to be read rather than assumed.** It ships a `build.rs`, which is the shape that §6 exists to catch. The file is `fn main() {}` for every feature but `extended-siginfo-raw`. `cc` is an *optional* build-dependency behind that one feature, and nothing enables it. The `no C toolchain` CI job keeps that true.

### Highlighting: the engine choice and what it defers

**`syntect` is fast enough incrementally to hold I2b, so tree-sitter stays out on speed as well as on toolchain.** One screenful of Rust highlights in **1.53ms** against I9's 16ms. A real shell frame under continuous edits takes **10.52ms p99**, against **6.97ms** for the core path before highlighting existed. What is not affordable is parsing a hunk *whole*. The 1006-line hunk that the fixture produces costs **60.97ms**, 3.8x over budget and paid on every frame under I9's own shape. That is why parsing forward only as far as the screen asked is load-bearing rather than an optimisation.

**`regex-fancy` is what keeps `syntect` pure Rust, and its price is a compile deferred to first use.** The default engine of `syntect` is `regex-onig`, which is oniguruma, which is C. If the defaults were taken, `cc` would enter the graph on its own and cost musl-static and Windows tier-1. What was never priced is *when* the pure-Rust engine does its work. `syntect` holds each pattern in a `once_cell::sync::OnceCell` and hands it to `fancy_regex` on first use. So the **first parse under a grammar** costs **74–362ms**, where loading all seventy-five costs 318µs.

**And it is per pattern rather than per grammar**, which is the part that decides what can be done about it. Warming on a small sample leaves a *different* file of the same language still paying. Over a 2.5KB synthetic sibling, `.js` is 80.49ms above floor, `.html` 40.10ms and `.cpp` 37.55ms. So **"this grammar is warm" is not a property that exists**, and anything built on one would act on a false claim ([#51](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/51)).

**The cliff is a compile rather than a parse, which is what the flatness in content size says.** Measured at screenful scale, twenty-four lines, a fresh `SyntaxSet` per case:

| | cold | after the warmer read one real 64KB sibling | floor |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.rs` | 123.98ms | **2.40ms** | 2.40ms |
| `.md` | 694.75ms | 89.77ms | 90.65ms |
| `.toml` | 15.26ms | 0.43ms | 0.40ms |

A 594-byte Markdown screenful costs **631.46ms cold and 0.97ms warm**, a 650x penalty on half a kilobyte. A 16.8KB one costs 439.26ms cold and 117.00ms warm.

> **Every figure in this table was measured on Windows under `codegen-units = 1`, and it overstates what ships.** [#261](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/261) found that at that setting the MSVC target compiles `fancy-regex` roughly 6x slower than at any other. So these costs are inflated by a factor that this repository chose and did not know it was paying. The profile now sets 2, and the same worst screenful parses in **8.81ms on Linux**. The penalty is also **Windows-specific**: on Linux, `codegen-units` 1, 2 and 16 sit within 3% of each other on every fixture. So the cliff is a property of the codegen of one target rather than of the engine or the profile. macOS has never been measured. **A reader who takes a number from this section is advised to ask which of the four targets of §9 it came from.** The figures are left rather than rewritten because re-measuring them honestly needs the platform they were taken on, and a number retyped across platforms is worse than a number with its provenance attached.

**So the exact claim is the negative one, and that is what the frame path acts on** ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)). A grammar that nothing has ever built a `ParseState` for has every pattern uncompiled. This is knowable rather than estimated. `syntect` exposes no way to fill a `OnceCell` but a parse, so the three places in `vigia-core` that parse are the whole population: the frame path, the warmer over a file, and the warmer over a block that an answer quoted, whose grammar no file here can name. **A frame therefore never parses under a grammar that the warmer has not run over. It draws that file or block plain, says which grammar it wanted, and the warm that follows wakes the loop to colour it.** Reaching every pattern directly was checked and is **not available**. `SyntaxReference::contexts` is private, the fields of `ContextId` are private, and `Regex::try_compile` compiles a throwaway rather than filling the cell of the set itself. Parsing real text is the only way in. That is why the warmer reads `WARM_BYTES` of a file rather than a fixture, and why the demand of a quoted block carries its own lines: warmed on any other text, that same block still costs 9.42ms against 93µs on its own.

**Warming past what the reader is about to meet is refused on memory.** Compiling ten grammars moves RSS from **6.73 MiB to 64.73 MiB**, Rust alone +12.43 MiB and Markdown +19 to +35 MiB. The budget of I3 is drift rather than a plateau, so that is a bad trade rather than a breach. If more grammars were warmed, a monitor left open for days would carry the compiled form of languages that the session never opens. The exception is the **three** grammars that the repository itself leads with, counted from the index, because the agent in the other pane is near-certain to write the language that the repository is mostly made of. **Counted per grammar rather than per extension**, and the difference is not cosmetic. The index can only be tallied by extension, since §6 puts `syntect` on the other side of `worktree.rs`. A language spelled two ways (`.yml` and `.yaml`, `.md` and `.markdown`) is then counted twice, at exactly the point where the counts decide who wins.

### The grammar set

**Which languages the dump covers is a decision, and until 2026-08-19 it was an inheritance.** `Highlighter::new` loaded `syntect`'s bundled dump: 75 syntaxes, a Sublime Text 3 era snapshot chosen by nobody. Probed against what a 2026 reader opens, 36 formats drew plain. TypeScript was first among them, and `Cargo.toml` drew plain in a tool written in Rust. *Ruled 2026-08-18, reader* ([#235](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/235)): **`vigia` covers every modern language, with no tiering.** The bar for a format is that a grammar exists that this stack can carry.

**The mechanism is a curated dump embedded in `vigia-core`, not per-grammar submodules.** [`two-face`](https://codeberg.org/CosmicHarper/two-face) (MIT OR Apache-2.0) packages `bat`'s curated grammar set as a `syntect`-ready dump with a **`fancy`-vetted variant**: every definition it ships compiles under `fancy-regex`, this crate's engine. That vetting is required, not a convenience. `syntect` compiles each pattern lazily behind an `expect` and the workspace builds with `panic = "abort"`. So **a grammar carrying a pattern the shipped engine cannot compile is a process kill on the first file that reaches it**. With such a grammar, a single `.ps1` in the diff would take the monitor down. `bat` never meets this because its default engine is oniguruma. Grammars `bat` does not carry are vendored as pinned `.sublime-syntax` files under `assets/syntaxes/` with provenance headers, **and each upstream's licence text is committed beside them** at `assets/syntaxes/licences/<repo>.LICENSE`, because MIT and Apache-2.0 both ask for the notice in every copy of a substantial portion, and the dump is a copy. A header that names a licence is not the licence.

**No shipped grammar is allowed to carry a pattern that fails to compile**, because of the abort just named. `two-face`'s `fancy` build guarantees it for `bat`'s set by excluding what cannot comply. `crates/vigia-core/tests/coverage.rs` gates every locally vendored grammar. It force-compiles the patterns **and the `first_line_match`** under the shipped engine, because a lazy compile is a gate that passes whatever nothing happened to reach. Three more gates live beside it. One compiles every first-line regex in the **whole** dump, since the vetting in `two-face` covers match patterns, and a first-line regex is exactly the kind nothing reaches until an extensionless file turns up. One ties the committed sources to the hash table in `NOTICE.md` and to the roster of the dump, because the two were otherwise checked separately, and a source edited without a rerun of `xtask` passed both. One parses a snippet per covered format and asserts the spread of classes it reaches, because a grammar that loads and draws plain is what the second half of #235 was about.

**An ambiguous extension resolves by a written rule, not by registration accident.**

| Extension | Resolves to | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `.h` | Objective-C | ObjC's grammar is a C superset, so C headers colour fully and only C++-only constructs draw plain. It is also the dialect of the reader who filed #235. `bat` rules the other way (`*.h` → C++, their #877). That is recorded here so that flipping this row is one edit and an informed one |
| `.m` | Objective-C | Same population argument over MATLAB, and the same one-row flip if it proves wrong |
| `.v` | V | Verilog is in the dump too, so the collision is real. The ruling's whole subject is the modern-language set, and V is the one it covers |
| `.sass` | Sass | It resolved to **Ruby Haml**, which is wrong. The pin gate in `tests/coverage.rs` makes a quiet return to Haml a red build |
| `.jsx` | the TSX grammar | TSX is a superset of JSX, and the Babel grammar is `fancy`-excluded |
| `.ts` | TypeScript, unless the first line opens `<?xml` (then XML) | A `.ts` whose first line is an XML declaration is a Qt translation file |

**Resolution has five steps rather than two.** First the table above. Then the whole file name, with a retry that adds a leading dot (so `.gitignore` can match a grammar that registers `gitignore`). Then the extension. Then the nearest-grammar approximations below. Then `find_syntax_by_first_line` when the first line of the file is known, which is what gives an extensionless shebang script a language at all. The whole name comes **before** the extension because it is the more specific claim. The CMake grammar registers `CMakeLists.txt` whole, and the old extension-first order handed it to Plain Text, so it drew plain for as long as highlighting has existed. The first line travels on `FileDiff`, captured from bytes the diff already holds, so no read is added anywhere (I4). The warmer deliberately does not use it. Its per-grammar cap is checked before the read, and reordering that to sniff content first would spend the I/O the cap exists to save.

**What the ruling costs, measured 2026-08-19, release.** The release binary moves from **5,492,224 to 6,102,528 bytes** (+11%) carrying 217 syntaxes against 75. The embedded dump is 970,915 bytes. Loading it at startup takes **0.674ms** best of 20 (318µs before). The dump is **uncompressed by measurement rather than by default**: the compressed form of the same set loads at **9.67ms**, a 14x, against 90KB saved in a binary whose size budget has an order of magnitude more room than its startup budget. First paint over the 100-file, 100k-line fixture is **15.15ms against I7's 50ms**. RSS is untouched by the count, because it follows the grammars that *compile*. A grammar nobody opens costs its bytes in the dump and nothing else.

**Four ruled formats have no grammar this stack can carry, and each is a recorded gap with a nearest-grammar approximation rather than a silent plain.** **Astro** and **Bicep** ship ST4 `version: 2` grammars built on `extends:` inheritance, which `syntect` does not implement. So `.astro` draws as HTML and `.bicep` as JavaScript, the grammars their upstreams extend. **Mojo** has no `.sublime-syntax` anywhere and is a Python superset, so `.mojo` and `.🔥` draw as Python. The only real grammar for **MDX** carries no licence to vendor under, and MDX is a Markdown superset, so `.mdx` draws as Markdown. **Carbon** has no grammar in any format and gets nothing. The approximation step runs **after** ordinary resolution fails, so the day a real grammar lands in the dump it wins without the table changing. **PowerShell is covered, not a gap**: `two-face` excludes it under `fancy` for one subroutine-call regex, and the vendored copy patches that one pattern structurally, the capture being unused.

### Coalescing, and the frame path's memory

**Coalescing stays ours.** `notify` has a companion debouncer crate. Taking it would move coalesce policy out of `vigia-core`, which is the one place I1 is testable. `notify` supplies raw events and nothing else.

**There are two coalescings, and only the first is that one.** `vigia-core` coalesces **events**: which filesystem writes count as one change. That is I1's. The shell coalesces **paints**: how many frames one burst of wakes is worth. That is I9's. It cannot live in the core, because a paint belongs to the shell and because one of the four wake sources is the terminal. It decides nothing about which events are real. It costs I1 nothing, because draining a queue that is already there is a `try_recv` and never a wait. An idle shell still blocks on one `recv` and still does no work ([#45](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/45)).

A coalesced tick also **names the file whose write landed last in it**, which is what I5 follows. The path is a by-product of the gitignore filter, which already resolves every event against the worktree root, so carrying it costs no syscall. This is the second thing that taking the debouncer crate would lose.

**The frame path keeps its diffs between frames.** I2a forbids re-diffing a file that did not change. So the core holds the diff of the previous frame per path and revalidates it instead of recomputing. Three things that cost no file read decide validity: the index blob the change names, the kind of change, and a `stat` of the working-tree file. Content is never hashed to decide this, because hashing is the read I2a exists to avoid.

A `stat` on its own is not proof. The gap is the one git calls *racily clean*: `stat` cannot tell apart two writes of the same length inside a single modification-time granule.
