**The working-tree side is normalised the way git's clean filter would normalise it**, and the diff is taken against that, not against the bytes on disk. Git does the same. On a checkout with `core.autocrlf=true`, the CRLF on disk becomes the LF the blob holds before anything is compared. Without this, every line of every such file differed from its stored form: a 21-line edit was drawn as **+905 −885** on the installed default of one of three tier-1 targets ([#65](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/65)). *Ruled 2026-08-01, session, from the reader's report.*

**The rules that filter applies are re-read once per frame, not once per process.** The agent in the other pane is free to write `.gitattributes` and `core.autocrlf`. Resolving them at startup would draw a stale answer indefinitely, which is I5 failing silently over a window that I3 measures in days. `Frame::advance` drops the filter and the next working-tree read rebuilds it. So staleness is bounded to one frame, and a frame that reuses every diff rebuilds nothing. `crates/vigia-core/tests/normalise.rs` gates this through `Frame::advance`, not through two bare `Worktree::diff` calls, because nothing in the latter marks a frame boundary.

**`core.safecrlf` is not consulted, because it guards writing.** It makes `git add` refuse a file whose conversion would not round-trip, and `git diff` ignores it. `vigia` never writes. When it was inherited, any file with mixed line endings failed the frame, on that frame and on every frame after, because the file stays mixed.

**What decides that a change is binary is `.gitattributes` first, then the bytes.** An unset `diff` attribute, which `binary` unsets, draws the file as binary. A set `diff` diffs it as text whatever its bytes. With neither, a NUL in the first 8000 bytes of either side makes it binary. That is git's order. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session* ([#68](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/68)).

**External filter drivers are not run, because §6 rules them out.** `gitattributes` can point the clean filter at an external program. Running it means a process per file per frame, and §6 chose an in-process diff over a `git diff` subprocess per tick. `eol`, `working-tree-encoding` and `ident` are pure Rust and do apply. The cost is recorded: under LFS the worktree holds content while the blob holds a pointer, so an LFS-tracked text file diffs as a rewrite ([#69](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/69)).

**A file that differs only in its line endings reports no change, and stays listed.** Dropping it from the list would need its content during `Frame::advance`. That is a read per changed file on the path I4 governs, and I4 forbids it. A header count that disagrees with the body under it is also the contradiction that B3's empty state exists to remove. Both halves match git: `git status` lists such a file and `git diff` reports nothing for it.

**An empty pane says which comparison it is empty *for*, and where the work went.** B3's line reads `no unstaged changes` in those words because the comparison is against the index ([#40](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/40)). When the other run has changes, the line carries its count, so `no unstaged changes · 3 staged` tells a clean tree from a fully staged one. Without the count, an agent that stages its own work empties the pane and the line is true of both. With both runs drawn and nothing in either, it reads `no staged or unstaged changes`. **A `hide` pattern takes the row instead.** It reads `12 hidden`, with the other run's count still beside it, since the count of §11.1 would otherwise be contradicted one line below itself. It costs one walk of the other comparison, on the one frame that computes no diff, and it is the cheaper walk because the staged one touches no file. **Off the live pane, the words come from the token** wherever the token reads as a clause. At the branch point, an empty run reads `no changes since main`, because `no unstaged changes` there describes a comparison this frame did not make. `only` is the exception and reads `nothing in this commit`, because the id is on the header directly above and `no changes only a1b2c3` is not a sentence. Either way, the body's one row states the body's own fact. *Ruled 2026-09-10, session.*

**The body has three regions by default: a pinned file list, a rule, and the scrolling diff.** The list is a map of the diff, and it yields to the diff. Its depth is capped at a share of the pane ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)), not at a count. §5.3 says *richness is the reward of space*, so the map grows with the pane and never at the expense of the diff. The cap steps by one row of list per four rows of pane. *Ruled 2026-08-17, session.*

**A sample weighs the bytes a write moved, not the fact that it happened.** `Track::bump` used to count files written. A worktree where one file is saved repeatedly then held exactly one sample, so the window was its own peak. Heights are scaled against that peak, so every active column drew at full height. The element showed *when* a write happened and never *how much*. **The shell takes the size, not the store.** It makes one `symlink_metadata` call per changed path per wake. `vigia-core` has no working directory, and a syscall at the call site keeps its cost inside the frame that the budgets gate. The status walk has already paid for that syscall, and taking the size from there instead is [#233](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/233). *Ruled 2026-08-18, session, from a live pane ([#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)).*

**The scale is not the mean either.** A mean does not reliably find the ordinary write. Agent work is heavy tailed: a test run that rewrites thousands of bytes sits in the same window as the 180-byte edits around it. The outlier that should saturate was setting the denominator it was measured against. **A value more than ten times the median of the non-empty values is left out of the mean.** The rule lives in `vigia-core` beside the store, not in the drawer, because it is about the signal and not the picture. Every rung of the sparkline ladder divides by a figure taken under it. *Ruled 2026-08-22, session, from a live pane ([#256](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/256)).*

**A level's width shows when a write happened, not how much.** Without this rule, rounding ends a level and the drawn width reports the size of the write: a floor-weight write draws a single mark where a large one draws a shape. The cut is three times the kernel's constant. The unit is a 256th of a byte. The bands are `[12, 18]` and `[256, 4096]`. *Ruled 2026-08-29, session ([#348](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/348)).*

**The window ages while it holds a sample, and stops when it is empty.** `History::roll` used to run only from the tick path, so a quiet worktree left the last burst pinned in place. That is worse than stale. The window's axis is time, so a frozen window keeps its newest sample at the right edge, and a burst from ninety seconds ago draws as *just now*. `Recency` froze with it, so a file that went quiet kept its pulse. The pane was then incorrect with zero interaction, which breaks I5. *Ruled 2026-08-22, reader, asked for from use ([#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243)).*

**This clock is an instance of I1's licence, not an exception to it.** The reason is the one `RULINGS.md` records for the first clock: every timer the spec refuses runs while nothing is happening. This one runs only while a burst is still decaying. The window empties `HISTORY_WINDOW` after the last write, so when nothing is happening the wait is untimed again, by construction. The licence gains one thing: a change in the worktree can arm a clock, not only a reader's gesture. A clock could already outlive what armed it by a bounded time: `SCROLL_LINGER` keeps the direction arrows lit for 220ms.

**The period is `HISTORY_SAMPLE`, one second, and it is derived, not picked.** It is the store's own quantum, so it is the finest interval at which any drawn cell can change. A faster period redraws identical pixels. A coarser period derived from the drawn cell is declined on measurement. An ageing wake costs **165µs** in release at the path cap, against I9's 16ms, so a width-derived period could save at most 1% of one frame budget. A cap restricts a feature, so it needs the same bar as a refusal. Ageing costs a paint, not a tick, which keeps that number small. It is not a filesystem event, so it does not walk status. An ordinary tick measures **529µs** on the same fixture. At most `HISTORY_SAMPLES` wakes follow any burst, so ageing one burst to nothing costs about **19.8ms of CPU spread over two minutes**.

**The height is recounted when a moved file settles, not at the next event.** While the agent writes, the tick after each file settles recounts it. When the agent goes quiet, no tick comes. The total then stayed where it was until the next event or until the reader scrolled, and #412 reported that jump. The frame now reports when the last height it is waiting on settles. `Shell::patience` folds that deadline in beside the ageing clock, and the loop advances and paints once on it. That is one wake per burst, at the end of the margin after the last print that moved. Nothing is armed while no print is inside the margin, so this stays within I1's licence. It costs a tick, not a paint, because the walk asks each waiting file again: **529µs** on the ageing fixture against I9's 16ms, plus the reads that #412 priced on the settled tick. The deadline is the last print's, not the first's, so a sweep whose prints spread over a hundred milliseconds is one walk, not a cascade. A walk that fails disarms the deadline. Otherwise the loop would fold the spent deadline to a zero wait, then advance, fail and fold it again, flat out. *Ruled 2026-09-04, session, as an instance of I1's licence ([#425](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/425)).*

**The sparklines age with the pulse by construction.** One store and one roll drive both, and the pulse moves with them. That meets the coherence requirement of [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234) through the mechanism, not by keeping two elements in step.

**The pulse's life is set by the sample grid. This is ruled, not a side effect.** `Recency::Pulse` means that a path was named by the newest burst *and* has ink in the newest `PULSE_SAMPLES` samples. So the mark expires at a sample boundary. It does not last until something else is written. Its life is `[HISTORY_SAMPLE, PULSE_SAMPLES x HISTORY_SAMPLE]`, closed at both ends. **A separate duration for the pulse is refused.** It would need a second clock per track, running on wall time beside an element that runs on the sample grid. The two would disagree about how long ago *now* was, and [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234) forbids exactly that. A mark that means *there is ink in the newest cells* is bounded by those cells. **The frame a burst causes always keeps its pulse**, by construction. One turn of the loop takes one instant, and both the tick and the roll read it, so a sample boundary cannot fall between a write and the paint it triggered. *Ruled 2026-08-18, session; amended 2026-08-25, reader, from a live pane ([#313](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/313)).*

**`?` draws the gestures sheet over the pane, and each further `?` advances it until it closes.** This is §11.2 B12, where the rejected branch is kept. This section says what the shell does. The sheet is a centred, bordered table of every gesture the pane binds. It is composited over cells the regions have already drawn, and it has a close control in its own title bar. **It moves nothing**, and that is why it was chosen: no rect gets smaller and no region gives up a row. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_sheet_moves_no_content` gates this.


**`m` draws the config menu, which edits the config file.** See §11.2 B22. Each view toggle the pane has gets one row. The name is in the chrome's ink. The state is `on` in the diff's `added` colour or `off` in its `removed` colour, right-aligned in three columns that do not move. `follow` is one of the rows, but the file does not accept it as a key until the second build of B22, so the rows and the file's keys are one list only from then on. The menu sits where the sheet sits: in the pane less its header and footer, never in the body's split, so no toggle on it moves it. `menu.rs::no_toggle_the_menu_draws_moves_the_box_it_is_drawn_in` gates this. **Only one overlay is drawn at a time**: `m` over the sheet replaces the sheet, and `?` over the menu replaces the menu. **The menu is the pane's second bounded mode**, and a narrow one. `↑` and `↓` move the caret, `Space` and `Enter` flip the row, and `Esc` closes the menu. Every other key keeps its meaning, so `r` flips the rail from inside the menu. When the pane is too short to draw the box, the box takes no key but `Esc`. That is why it is a mode and not a state. `▸` marks the caret's row, and a pointer marks the row it rests on, both in the ink a listed path uses. The whole row is the click target. The keys are drawn on the box's bottom edge, `↑↓ move · Space toggles · Esc closes`, on the note box's ladder. **A pane that is too short scrolls the menu and does not page it.** The window follows the caret, and the title bar counts the rows it hides. The menu is as wide as its widest legend needs and no wider, which puts the names and their states at the two ends. Its inset narrows by one at the floor. A flipped cell coalesces over the length of a receipt, and that is the menu's only motion. **`Save settings` sits under its own rule and is off until it is turned on.** While it is on, every flip is written to the file at once, because a write on exit is lost in the deaths that I8 excludes. Only the lines it owns are replaced, so comments, `hide` and the reader's spacing survive. A refused write is one alert, and it turns remembering off. `Reset to defaults` sits under it, drawn as an action, with no state cell. **A row names its setting, not what the setting does**: sentence case, at most three words. *Ruled 2026-09-28, reader ([#541](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/541)).* *§11.2 B22, ruled 2026-09-11, reader. The session ruled its six open questions.*

**The sheet opens with what the pane *is*, above the table of keys**: `working tree's diff, live · it follows on its own`, or `working tree's diff, live` under forty-nine columns of text. A reader ran the pane for days with `? keys` on the footer and could not say what it was for. The sheet answers *what can I press*, and only someone who already knows the answer asks that. The pane itself is unchanged: a permanent line on the empty state would buy discoverability with the screen this tool shows most of the time. *Ruled 2026-09-09, reader, from the pane, with both drawn ([#492](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/492)).*

**A rung short of room gives up the prose line first.** A rung that dropped a gesture for width never draws the line, and that includes the eleven gestures of the mouse group. A rung draws the line only while the whole table still fits beside it, so no sheet pages to make room for a sentence. When the line costs a rung a row, that rung is offered twice, first with the line and then without it. A paged rung weighs the line alone. So a pane gives up the line, not the shape it had. The line costs what the rung makes it cost. The roomy rung pays **nothing**, because the line goes into the blank row it already keeps under its title bar. The two-column rung pays **one** row, because its heading is a rule, and a rule separates the way a blank row does. A one-column rung pays **two**, because it has neither. **The line is not a gesture**, so the page counter's ordinals do not count it, as with `Line::Heading`. *Shape ruled 2026-09-09, session.*

**The sheet is not a mode, which keeps it outside B4's refusal.** No key changes meaning while it is up: `q` still quits, `j` still scrolls the diff behind it, and a digit still jumps. **`?` keeps exactly one meaning, *the sheet*, and B13 is the cost of that.** On a pane where the whole table does not fit, the next press shows the next page, and the press on the last page closes the sheet. On a pane whose sheet is one page, `?` is the toggle it always was. **The close control is its own action, not the key's.** `?` means *the sheet* and advances it. The control means *close* and closes from any page, and a pointer has no `?` to fall back on. **`Esc` closes the sheet through a ladder, not through a second meaning.** It puts away the sheet if one is up, otherwise a drag in progress, otherwise the program. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::escape_puts_the_sheet_away_before_it_puts_the_program_away` holds the sheet's rung against the program's rung in one gate, because a key that both dismisses and quits needs a fixed order. *Ruled 2026-09-03, reader, restating what [#341](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/pull/341) shipped on 2026-08-26 ([#391](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/391)).* A click on the control closes the sheet. Anything else that lands on the sheet does nothing at all. Letting it through would let a click seek a scrollbar the reader cannot see, or a wheel scroll a diff the sheet covers. **This covers the press as well as the action.** `Regions::step_at` and `grab_at` answer geometry questions without going through `action_for`. Both are guarded inside the method, not at their call sites, so every caller is correct by construction. *Ruled 2026-08-17, session; the press half 2026-08-25 ([#298](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/298)).*

**The sheet is opaque.** Every cell it covers is **reset** before it is styled, so the sheet is a hole punched in the diff, not a tint over it. `Cell::set_style` in `ratatui` merges into the style already in the cell, and the chrome's own style has no background. Styling alone would leave the `added_row` and `removed_row` washes underneath as green and red bands under the table. The gate injects a theme that washes its rows and does not use the shipped palette, because `Theme::default` sets no row background. A gate written against it would have passed on the screen that was reported as striped. **Closing the sheet restores every cell exactly**, symbol and style. That is the other half of *it moves nothing*, and it is gated.

**The close control uses two of B10's three rungs**: the chrome's weight at rest, and `bar_hover` under the pointer. With one weight, a reader cannot tell whether the glyph is a control at all. A pointer resting anywhere else on the sheet marks **nothing**, because the sheet swallows what lands on it, and a mark on something underneath would suggest otherwise.

**Two rungs, not the three of the step buttons,** ruled 2026-08-25 ([#298](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/298)). It was found by asking what puts a cell into `Chrome::pressed`, not by reading either. **The reason is the third rung's purpose.** `bar_active` shows a gesture *in progress* on an element that is still on screen. A step button needs it: pressing *up* at the top of a diff moves no row, so the lit cell is the only answer. The close control **dismisses on the press**, so the sheet is gone before the next paint, and no frame can draw both. Building the rung would mean firing on `Up` instead. That adds dismissal latency to the sheet's only pointer escape, and adds a press-here, release-elsewhere case to rule on, for a weight visible for milliseconds on an element that then disappears. It is declined for that reason, not for cost, which is nil either way. **The general ladder is unchanged**: *"a click is always brighter than a hover"* below is about the bar, which keeps all three rungs. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::nothing_can_press_the_close_control` is the gate. It says in its own words that it is **defence, not behaviour**: it cannot be made to go red, because it asserts that nothing produces a state.

**The sheet is retained state, not a one-frame draw.** The pane wakes on filesystem events, and a write from the agent redraws the frame underneath the sheet. A sheet that lived for one frame would be dismissed at random by someone else's build. B12 named this as the constraint most likely to be missed, and `a_write_under_the_sheet_does_not_dismiss_it` holds it.

**The sheet degrades on two axes and trades between them on one rung. Both floors live in the layout, not in the painter.** The ladder has four rungs, and their order is the contract. First is the roomy rung ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)). Next is every row in one column. Next is the two-column rung ([#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220)), which spends width to buy height. Last is the **paged** rung ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286)), which draws as many rows as the body holds and puts the rest on the next page. Width picks one spelling, wide or tight, for the whole table. A table whose rows each chose their own width is not a table, and a truncated verb has no identifying half to keep. **The height axis no longer drops anything.** It splits into pages, which is B13. **Below the floor, `?` still toggles and nothing is drawn**, just as `r` behaves on a pane too narrow for a rail, so no gesture is swallowed by a sheet nobody can see. The floors belong to `Body`, not to the drawer, for the reason of [#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158), which was already paid for once.

**Width still drops gestures, in the same order as before.** A pane too narrow for the whole table at the tight spelling gives up the mouse group, then the keyboard rows in drop order, keeping `a`, `f` and `?` last. The gestures a reader cannot guess outlive the reflexive ones, which is the hint bar's rule one surface over. `q` goes first because the hint bar spells `q quit` on every frame. Measured over every width from 20 to 144: **38 columns and up reach the whole table**. The ranges 35 to 37, 32 to 34 and 30 to 31 each reach a smaller set. Below 30 no sheet is drawn. **These boundaries do not move when a row joins or leaves the table**, because no row here has ever moved a width. They are re-derived from a swept pane, not incremented. The sizes of the sets are not restated here: a count beside a sentence that says a gate holds it goes stale at the next gesture, and this one did. The promise is stated at forty columns, because forty is I6's width. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::every_gesture_is_reachable_at_forty_columns_and_up` is that promise as a gate. It walks `?` from the first page to the close on every pane in the sweep. `the_display_order_is_the_readers_and_the_narrow_floor_keeps_the_unguessable` pins the three narrow sets by name, not by count.

**The page counter sits in the title bar, so the omission is never silent.** It reads ` a-b of 26 ` between the title and its rule. It is drawn only when the page is not the whole table, so no screen that fits everything today gains a character. It costs no content row. B12 chose an overlay for that property, and a notice that took a row would spend it on the gestures it was reporting missing. The ordinals are positions **within what this pane can reach**, not within the whole table. A set dropped for width is not contiguous in display order, because `q` goes first and `q` is at the bottom of the table. So a last page that stops short of the total shows how much this pane reaches. True positions with gaps would read as a rendering fault, not as a count. The total is `KEYBOARD.len() + MOUSE.len()`, not a literal, because a counter naming a total the tables no longer hold would be wrong, and no gate over the drawn output would catch it.

**Every rung reserves the widest spelling of the counter, whether it draws the counter or not. That reservation turned a guard into a rung.** The floor is the title, plus ` 18-20 of 21 `, plus a border, a space and two columns of gap at each end: **thirty columns**. The reservation is for the widest *spelling* of the counter, so a table that grows leaves it where it was. The reservation makes the counter **fit**. The title bar's rule runs for `width - title - counter - 5` through a saturating subtraction, so on a rung too narrow for the counter the rule silently goes to zero and the ordinals run into the close control. The reservation is **not** what keeps the box the same size between pages. `sheet_fields` measures over the whole row set, and every page of a pane shares that set, so the width does not depend on the page anyway. The deepest dropping rungs are raised to it. A pane under thirty draws no sheet at all, not a handful of the sixteen gestures with nothing saying so. B13 states that trade openly. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_floor_is_a_rung_now_and_the_narrowest_sheets_are_the_sizes_the_ruling_states` holds the three numbers: 30 narrowest anywhere, 38 narrowest at forty and up (I6's floor), 71 narrowest two-column.

**The roomy rung heads the ladder: the same one column, with its sections headed and space around them.** The key cells start four columns in, the verbs eight columns after the key field, and the headings two columns in, so they stand back from their rows. A blank row sits above and below every section. **The headings are plain text, not rules.** This is the one difference from every other rung, and it follows from the space: a rule under a blank row is a second separator doing the first one's job. The sections are `moving` (`j k`, `Space`, `d u`), `files` (`g`, `n p`, the digits, `J K`), `view` (`f`, `r`, `s`, `a`, `w`), `notes` (`c` and the box's `Enter` and `Esc`), `mouse` (the whole mouse table) and `leaving` (`?`, `q`), in that order. **`leaving` sits at the bottom**, because quitting is not what a reader reads first. The five keyboard runs tile the table exactly once, in order. A `const` block beside them asserts this, not a test, because bad bounds are an index panic on the frame path, and a build that will not compile beats a suite that goes red. The mouse group sits fourth and stays last wherever the groups are stacked, because a rung without headings cannot put two unlabelled keyboard rows after a labelled mouse group. **The roomy rung always takes the wide spelling.** This is derived, not chosen: space is surplus, and this rung is the wide one-column sheet plus six columns of gap and six of inset. **Being first makes it additive.** It needs strictly more of both axes than the full one-column rung, so a pane that takes it would already have drawn every gesture, and a pane that does not is unchanged. The roomy-rung gates in `tests/sheet.rs` hold its figures. They are not restated here, because a number here goes stale at the next gesture. *Ruled 2026-08-24, reader, from his own mock ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)).*

**The widths come from the shipped drawer, not from the mock.** The fields are derived from the content of the rows a rung draws, and the trailing inset matches the leading one, as at every other rung. The mock decides the **space**, and every number it gives for that is kept.

**Display order and drop order are two separate orders. They were one until #285.** `KEYBOARD` is the order a reader reads. `DROP_ORDER` is the order in which the height ladder gives rows up. The second is a ranking, not a direction through the first. Before #285 they were the same by construction, because the ladder dropped a prefix of the one array, so *the rows a reader meets first* and *the rows the ladder gives up first* could not disagree. They are different claims: a reader meets `j k` first, and the ladder gives up `q` first. Keeping them as one made the sections impossible to build. **This is a trap, and the failure is silent.** If you reorder the one array for a new section, the keep-set is re-ranked without warning. The pane that then loses a gesture is one of the two or three shortest on the whole ladder, and nothing else on screen changes. With the reader's order in place, dropping from the top would have kept `q`, which the hint bar names on every frame, and dropped `f` and `a`, which this section names as the ones a reader cannot guess. The drop sequence itself is the same as before the reorder. The `sheet_tables` module of `crates/vigia/src/render.rs` holds both ends on the tables, and `the_display_order_is_the_readers_and_the_narrow_floor_keeps_the_unguessable` holds them on a screen. These are two separate claims: a drawer free to walk the table backwards satisfies every assertion over the constants.

**One column while one column fits, and two columns where the pane is short and wide** ([#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220)). The problem pane is any pane shorter than twenty-four rows. The one reported was 120 by 20: the sheet drew eleven of sixteen gestures and silently dropped the whole mouse group, while sixty of the hundred and twenty columns held nothing. **The two-column shape splits on the group boundary. It is not two columns of key-and-verb pairs.** It is the one two-column arrangement that does not read as two unrelated tables, because the sheet already drew a `mouse` heading at exactly that seam. The groups were labelled before they were columns, and both carry a label now, on one rule. Its size follows from giving each column its own widest cells, not the widest cells of either. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_two_column_rung_is_the_size_the_ruling_states` holds the size. It is not restated here, because every added gesture moves the height. *Ruled 2026-08-24, session.*

**The two-column rung sits below the full one-column rung and above every dropping rung, which makes it additive.** A pane where one column already fits never reaches it, so nothing that draws every row today changes. It picks its own spelling, not the pane-wide one. The pane-wide spelling was chosen against a one-column sheet. Choosing per rung lets an eighty-column pane take the tight two-column rung instead of falling past it and dropping the whole mouse group. **It arrives at 73 columns**: `margin_of` returns 2 from 44 to 78, so a 73-column pane leaves 71 columns of room, and the tight two-column rung is exactly 71 wide. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_two_column_rung_arrives_at_the_width_the_ruling_states` walks 70 to 84 one column at a time, so that number cannot drift. `::the_widening_ladder_is_monotone_and_never_leaves_the_body` holds the claim that growing a pane on either axis never takes a gesture away. It walks every width from 40 to 144 against every height from 6 to 38. Those bounds are the claim's bounds: 40 is I6's floor, and 144 is above every width the rung arrives at, so a pane outside them is untested, not covered. **It snaps** in both directions, per §5.3's *snap, never ease*. A pane that is short **and** narrow gets no column trading. The height axis pages and does not drop, so a pane loses only what its width cannot spell, and the counter says how much that is. *Ruled 2026-08-24, session; the arrival width corrected 2026-08-25 ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286)).*

**The two regions are a layout ruling, not a navigation ruling.** B4 was written as *"one continuous scroll, list as map"*. That answered *navigable?* and settled *is it a region at all?* underneath it. `assets/preview.svg` draws the other answer, and drew it before the question was asked: `src/engine/watch.rs` appears **twice**, once in a block of summary rows and once as the diff heading, with a rule between them. One stream never does that. §5.1's rule, that a published artifact that answers an open question **is** the answer, applies to layout exactly as it applied to `f`. **The rule between the regions stays bare** (§11.2 B11), because of what a heading *is*, not where the reader scrolled. A heading is a `Painter::file_row` that carries the glance elements through the drawer both regions share. Folding it into a border line would strip those elements. *Ruled 2026-08-01, session ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66)).*

**The footer has a rule over it, and both rules run edge to edge.** Every other boundary on this screen is drawn. The bottom bar had none, so chrome sat directly under content with nothing marking it. The footer rule is the same `─` the list uses, on a reserved row. **Full bleed is the visible half.** §5.3 rules that furniture runs to both edges, and a rule that stops short reads as a box someone forgot to close. So both rules are drawn from the pane's rect, not from an inset rect. **It yields on a short pane**, like the list rule. A rule that cost the diff its last row would announce a region without separating one. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::both_rules_reach_both_edges_of_the_pane` gates the pair, including the two-line footer, where a mark placed a fixed row up would land inside the bar. *Ruled 2026-08-18, reader-reported.*

**The split keeps the differentiator on screen.** Four of the five glance elements ride a `Row::File` heading. In one stream they are visible only while that heading is. Scrolling two hundred lines into a file takes every file's glance signal off the screen at once. §2 buys *readable at a glance*, and a glance surface that survives only at scroll position zero is gone whenever anyone is reading.

**The list is still not navigable.** That half of B4 stands. The list has no selection, no focus and no second mode. `▸` marks the file the diff is inside. It is not a cursor, and no key changes meaning based on where anything is. The region is a **map**. "List as map" asked for that, and a stream of headings could not supply it.

**The caret's row draws its path bold. The weight is a second channel for the caret, not a second mark.** Every other lasting distinction in this region is carried by weight, yet the row a reader most needs to find carried only a glyph. `Painter::list` resolves the glyph and the weight from **one** predicate, so both name the same row or neither is drawn. That ties the weight to `affords_caret`: below seventeen columns the caret is dropped, and a bold row with nothing pointing at it would be a weight nobody can explain. The bold says which file the diff is *already* in, and no key moves it. It shares `BOLD` with the pulse rung of the recency ladder. The two stay distinct because each has its own glyph, `●` and `▸`, and the glyphs also survive `NO_COLOR`. Both regions draw through one `Painter::file_row`. The stream's call site passes `false`, so a heading for the file the caret marks stays plain. *Ruled 2026-08-16, session ([#193](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/193)).* **The weight is the theme key `path_current`, patched onto the style the row already took**, so a theme can add a modifier or a colour without restating the rung. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session ([#195](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/195)).*

**The list's height is the changed-file count, capped at a quarter of the pane and never below six, plus the rule.** Three changed files draw three rows, exactly as the picture shows. A formatter that touches two hundred files draws the cap and scrolls. On the 24-row pane this tool is built for, the cap is six, which leaves fourteen rows of diff. A 50-row pane draws twelve. The clamp order is: one row per changed file, then the cap, then whatever leaves the diff `MIN_BODY`. **The share is the rule, and six was its instance** ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)). Six was first recorded as an absolute and justified by arithmetic about one pane. Six is a quarter of twenty-four, so the number was a proportion stated against a single height. A 50-row pane that kept six drew empty space where the map could be. A glance reads the region's share of the screen, not its row count. At a quarter, the map never dominates, and the diff keeps three quarters at every height.

**Each rung adds one row of list per four rows of pane. The step size is load bearing, not rounding.** The diff gets what the list leaves. A cap that gained two rows for one row of pane would take a row off the diff on a pane that had just grown. That is the "a bigger container holds less" failure the margin ladder exists to avoid. Stepping by one keeps the rows left after the list non-decreasing. At a rung boundary, the row the pane gains goes to the map, and the other three go to the diff.

**The cap is read from the pane, never from the body.** The inset ladder and the caret's floor make the same ruling on the other axis. The body is the pane less its header and a footer. The footer's height depends on the changed-file count, so a cap read from the body would deepen the list when a seventh file appears. The height is a function of **pane geometry, follow state and changed-file count**. That is exactly the set `Footer::plan` takes, and none of them is transient. So the region cannot jog the diff the way a notice or a passing failure can. It does move when the footer does, since `f` on a forty-column pane takes a second footer line. That is the reader's own keypress changing the shape of the screen.

**The list uses the stream's order**, which is `Frame::files()`, which is status order. That makes it a map, not a second opinion: the caret's place in the list and the thumb's place on the diff's scrollbar describe the same position. Ordering by diff size was rejected on I4, because it needs every changed file's diff. Ordering by recency from `History` is free, but it was rejected too: it decouples the list from the stream, so the caret no longer matches the scroll position and the map stops being one.

**A list row draws exactly what a stream heading draws**, through the same `Painter::file_row` and the same degradation ladder. `assets/preview.svg` draws the same row in both regions, as §5.1's departure ledger records. A split fails once the list scrolls, because a file scrolled out of the region would lose its counters entirely.

**A region's file rows draw their glance elements in columns** ([#77](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/77)). If an element is placed from a budget that already lost the elements outside it, its position depends on their widths on that row. The counts cell is the widest variable thing there. Three sparklines that do not share a left edge do not read as one small-multiples chart, and the list exists to give that reading. So the ladder runs **once per region**, and every row draws into the slots it produced. The order is counts, then the note mark, then the pulse, then heat, then sparkline. **A row with nothing for a slot keeps the column open**, so the alignment holds on every row. Under right-packing, each absence let that row's remaining elements slide into the gap, out of line with the rows around it. A missing sparkline now draws a **track**, not a blank. A missing heat strip still draws a blank, and the heat strip paragraph further down says why that difference is ruled. **The counts cell is two columns, not one field**: `+N` right-anchored in its own column and `-M` in its own, one space apart, as the picture draws it. A single field aligns only the end it is anchored to and leaves the other ragged. An eye running down three files' additions would then get the same non-comparison this ruling removes. **Each half is drawn in its own diff colour, and a half with nothing to say is drawn in the row's dim grey**: `+42` green, `-7` red, `-0` grey ([#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157)). That is §5.3's rule, not the picture's. Green and red are used only where they restate the same fact, and a zero restates none. Otherwise a worktree an agent is only adding to would carry red on every row, saying nothing. **Presence is shared and ink is per half.** This is the one place the two rules pull apart. The pair is drawn or dropped together. A renderer that greyed a whole cell because one half is zero would apply the wrong rule.

**The slots come from the pane, never from the rows.** Sizing the counts field to the widest count among the rows about to be drawn is stable *within* a window and moves *between* windows. Scrolling until a file with `+1500 -1500` enters the window widens the field by six columns and slides every heat strip and sparkline on every row. Intermittent movement is worse than a wrong layout, because nothing on screen explains it. So every slot is a constant or a rung of the pane's width. Measuring over every changed file would also be stable. It is forbidden for [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s reason: it needs every changed file's diff, which makes the frame's cost grow with the diff, against I4.

**Each region plans its own slots. From forty-three columns up, the two plans agree.** They are allowed to differ, and they did: the list was inset by the caret, two columns. So the list's status sigil sat two columns right of the same sigil on a diff heading, and every element after it inherited the offset ([#173](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/173)). **The caret takes one column in the pane's own margin**, so the list charges the row only what the margin cannot lend. That is nothing at or above forty-three, and one column below it, where the ladder has nothing left to lend. The regions still plan separately, because `Painter::file_row` must stay one drawer that knows nothing about which region called it. Neither plan reads a row, so a heading scrolling past cannot move the pinned region above it.

**Below forty-three, the list pays one column. That is decided, not tolerated.** Extending the margin ladder down, so the caret always has a blank cell to stand in, was refused for [#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)'s own reason. That floor protects I6's forty-column pane, and paying it here would spend a column of code at exactly forty. Dropping the caret below forty-three would buy alignment at every width. It would cost the narrow pane the one mark that says which file the diff is inside, which trades information for tidiness at the width that can least afford it. Keeping the old two columns made the gap between caret and sigil appear at forty-three, vanish, and reappear at seventy-nine as the pane widened. So the caret narrows to one column, takes the margin where there is one, and charges the row a single column where there is not.

**The caret is the one glyph allowed on the pane's own edge.** This is a stated exception to the inset ladder, not a hole in it. Reading *"flush against the pane's left edge"* literally stops the gap between caret and sigil from growing with the pane. The marker sits at column zero at every width, and what follows it is whatever margin the ladder bought. §5.3 already rules that the picture binds the element set and each element's promises, not glyph-for-glyph fidelity. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_pane_insets_its_text_at_every_rung` puts the exception inside the assertion: it strips the caret and measures everything after it against the margin, so a list row that lost its inset *and* drew a caret still fails.

**The width that drops the caret is computed from the row's own floor, not kept as a constant.** What the caret costs a row depends on the width. A fixed sum would be wrong above the ladder's floor, and wrong in the other direction below it. `affords_caret` asks whether the row still clears `ROW_FLOOR` after the bar, the inset and the caret's own gutter, using the same expression the drawing plans against. It works out to seventeen columns, far below the forty I6 is named for. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_caret_threshold_is_the_row_floor_it_claims` asserts the comparison, not the pixels, in both directions.

**The body opens with one blank row under the header, and only while there is a list.** The header states a fact about the whole worktree, and the list is a map of it. They are different classes of thing. The boundary *below* the list already spends a whole rule saying so, and this boundary had nothing ([#174](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/174)). It is a blank, not a rule, for two reasons. The request was separation, not a second boundary mark. And one glyph drawing two boundaries of visibly different weight says less than no mark at all. A body with no map has nothing to separate from the header, so the row goes back to the diff. The changed-file count, the notice and follow mode cannot move it. It costs one diff row of about fifteen on a 24-row pane. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_body_opens_with_one_blank_row_under_the_header` holds the rule.

**Every slot is reserved whether or not anything can fill it.** That includes the sparkline slot in a region where no file has a history yet, which at launch is every file. Reserving only what some drawn row can fill would move every column on the tick the first file is written, which is exactly when someone is looking at the screen. The price was a row that was mostly blank until the first write, and [#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78) refunds it. The reserved sparkline slot now draws a track instead of a blank, so the slot that looked pointless now says *no churn in the window*. Reserving the column made saying so free.

**A region is planned against its own width less a scrollbar column, whether or not the bar is drawn.** Beside a rail, a region's width and the pane's width are different numbers. A seventy-column rail planned against a two-hundred-column pane would lay out rows for a screen it does not have ([#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252)). The *inset's rung* still comes from the pane. A bar appears when `scrollable` finds that the region cannot show everything it holds, so whether a bar is drawn depends on the **contents**. Without the reserved column, a seventh changed file, or a diff one row taller than the screen, would narrow the region by two columns and re-plan every row in it. At some widths that slides the elements sideways, and at others it takes the counts cell off every row. Always reserving the column prevents that. The problem survived the first fix only because a scrollbar does not look like content. **The cost is the sparkline between 40 and 41 columns**, on top of the 36 to 39 the counts cell took. With the inset's column and the mark's column, the sparkline needs 47. At exactly the width I6 is named for, a pane shows the heat strip and the counts and no sparkline. The alternative is not *a sparkline at forty* but *a sparkline at forty until a seventh file changes*. An intermittent view is worse than a smaller one, because nothing on screen explains it. **A row's band runs under the scrollbar's own column, and the bar draws over it.** `BAR_WIDTH` is `reserved(1)`: a bar column and a blank one in front of it. A wash that stops before either leaves a notch of pane background down the height of the diff. *Reported from a real pane.* The reserve itself is about glyph adjacency and is unchanged. It exists so a full-block thumb never sits flush against a count and reads as `-6#`. A blank cell whose background matches the band is still a blank cell. **`Painter::bar_cell` assigns the style instead of merging it**, so the band keeps the background and the bar owns the symbol, the foreground and the modifier. Merging fails in a way only configuration can reach. `Cell::set_style` inserts `add_modifier` unconditionally, and `VIGIA_THEME` accepts a trailing modifier word on every key `Theme::KEYS` names. So `removed_row = on #45222a reverse` would put `REVERSED` on the thumb and swap the two colours every contrast gate exists to prove. Assigning makes the guarantee structural, instead of relying on every palette leaving that field unset. **The track colour moves with the band.** `bar_track` at `#57606a` falls to 1.88:1 on an added row, against a 2.0:1 floor. It becomes `#656c76` on dark and `#7d8590` on light. The light value is `spark_track`'s own, so that palette invents nothing. `heat_track` is deliberately unchanged, since the heat strip never draws on a washed row. Five gates hold this, and the last three cover what the first two cannot see by construction: `render.rs::a_wash_runs_under_the_scrollbar_column`, `render.rs::every_row_of_the_bar_carries_its_own_rows_background` over every row at both bar shapes, `render.rs::a_row_washs_modifier_never_reaches_the_scrollbar`, `palette.rs::a_bar_track_is_visible_on_every_row_it_crosses`, and `palette.rs::every_bar_style_says_what_bar_cell_reads` on all three palettes including `ansi`. *Ruled 2026-08-17, reader-reported; finished 2026-08-18 ([#214](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/214), [#239](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/239)).*

**The pane keeps a margin from its own edge, on its own ladder: one cell a side from 44 columns, two from 80, and none below 43.** `assets/preview.svg` draws its furniture to the window edge and its glyphs one to three cells inside it. The shell drew everything from column 0, which under §5.1's law made that an undocumented departure from the picture, not a choice. The floor is I6's, not a rounding: the sparkline has been bought and sold twice over two columns at exactly forty. **The margin is a ladder of the *total* width, split between the two sides. This is a ruling, not arithmetic.** A per-side ladder steps both sides at the same column and spends two columns for the one a widening pane just gained, so the content width goes *down* on a wider pane when it should go up. Taking both sides at once grew the footer to two rows at 44 and at 80, spending a body row on a **wider** pane. It also moved the header's worktree name from marked back to whole as the pane *narrowed* across 80. So the total grows by one per rung, and the odd column goes to the left side. That keeps the pane square at every width except 43 and 79. The left goes first because a reader sees it as the edge of the page. *Ruled 2026-08-10, session ([#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)).*

**The inset is charged to a region on its left side only, which makes it affordable.** Every row stops two columns short of the pane's right edge for the bar, drawn or not, so `planning_width` already spends the columns a right-hand margin would want. A row loses its rung's width in path once, never twice, and at 80 the pane's two margins come out equal. This holds only while no rung's **right half** exceeds the bar's reserve. `the_inset_never_outgrows_the_scrollbars_reserve` fails if one ever does, because a wider half would bring back the trailing reserve that a later paragraph refuses.

**The diff's rows are drawn across the width they were laid out for: the region's planning width.** A heading, a hunk header, a note and a content line all stop where `planning_width` stops, so every row lines up with the heading above it at every width. Charging the pane's trailing margin *as well* puts a content line left of its own heading wherever the diff is long, which lets the contents decide the layout. Charging it *instead* separates the drawn width from the `Body::diff_width` the same text is wrapped at: by two columns below forty-four and by one up to seventy-nine, on any pane that is not scrolling. `a_diff_outgrowing_its_pane_does_not_move_the_content_rows_edge` gates the first case and `the_width_a_row_wraps_at_is_the_width_it_is_drawn_across` the second. *Ruled 2026-09-09, session ([#474](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/474)).*

**`r` puts the pinned list beside the diff as a left rail, from a hundred and thirty-nine columns up.** Below 139 it changes nothing drawn and eats no gesture. The request is **kept**, not cleared, so a pane that is narrowed and widened again gets its rail back without being asked again. `Chrome::rail` holds the request, and `Body::rail` holds what the pane can give. *Layout ruled 2026-08-23 ([#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252)); the gesture is §11.2 B14, ruled 2026-08-24 ([#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295)), and until then the rail arrived on its own.*

**There is one arrival width, derived at the block rung.** Both regions read the layout table, so splitting a pane costs each half the width the whole had. A split costs **no rung** only where both halves, and the undivided pane one column narrower, sit on the same plateau. The settled ladder's plateau ends at a pane of 138. The only other plateau is the table's top rung, which would need a pane of 338. So the rail is offered from **139**: the first width at which the stacked list, without a rail, would leave the settled ladder and spend its new columns on a twenty-four slice heat strip. Since B14 the rail is offered, not automatic. The derivation decides where the gesture can be honoured. It does not decide whether the gesture exists. **The split costs the diff's content column. For a reader who asked for the rail, that is the feature, not a defect.** B14 exists for that qualifier, and it is why the rail stopped arriving on its own. At 138 the diff plans against 134 columns, and at 139 against 63. A rail at any width narrows the diff, because its columns can come from nowhere else. The choice was between narrowing the diff here and halving the heat strip in both regions at a later width. Where the rail arrives, the two are within a few columns of each other, 72 against 67, and the diff is the wider from 145 up.

**The rail is 72 columns until the pane reaches 219, and a third of the pane after that.** The floor is built from what it must hold: the scrollbar's reserve, the pane's leading inset, the kind letter every file row starts with, twice `MIN_PATH_WIDTH` of path, and the settled glance cluster the picture draws. The rail is twice the floor, not the floor, because at exactly twelve columns a path shrinks to its bare tail. A rail whose paths lose their directory gives up half of its purpose. The share is the smaller part of the pane on purpose. Below 219 every column the pane gains goes to the code. Above it the rail earns width the way §5.3 says richness is earned.

**The derivation is for the block rung. That is a limit, not a caveat.** `Columns::plan` takes the glyph rung, and a dense cell draws two buckets per column, so the same layout table is reached at different widths. On a terminal with braille or octants, the stacked ladder climbs at a pane of **119** and is past the settled rung by 133. Crossing into the rail then costs twenty-four heat slices for twelve in both regions. No arrival width is loss-free at every rung. That is arithmetic, not a choice, because the braille plateau that would allow one is empty. [#284](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/284) is the row for an arrival width that knows its rung.

**Below a 417-column pane, the rail draws the glance elements the picture shows. At 417 it climbs.** The table's share clamp needs 135 planning columns for the next rung up, and the rail's third of the pane reaches that at exactly 417. The boundary is stated as a number because the sentence it replaced, *"does not climb at any pane anyone runs"*, is one no sweep can fail. Below it, twelve slices and twelve buckets is exactly what `assets/preview.svg` draws, so §5.1's picture binds the rail as well as the strip. At 417 the rail earns more. `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_grows_with_the_pane_and_keeps_the_pictured_complement` sweeps past the boundary and pins it in both directions.

**Beside a rail, the list's height cap does not apply.** [#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160) made the cap a share of the pane because the list's rows come out of the diff's rows. In this layout they do not, so the region takes every row the body has: a forty-row pane draws thirty-six changed files where the stacked layout draws ten. Each visible row still costs one `Frame::diff` and no more, which is the I4 claim `tests/reads.rs` owns. The deeper region was measured against I9, not assumed: a frame on a 200x40 pane drawing 36 pinned rows is **0.71ms p50 and 0.79ms p99 against the 16ms budget**.

**`s` pins the diff to the file the caret is on, and the walk stops there.** It lets a reader keep one position, in the file, instead of two. Scrolling stops at that file's two ends and does not carry on into the file below. The scrollbar measures the file. The pinned file changes with `n`, `p`, a digit, a click on a listed file, or a follow. The pin is off by default at every width and height. It is `f`'s sibling: follow decides *which* file the pane goes to on its own, and the pin decides how much of the tree the reader's own scrolling reaches once there. *§11.2 B16, ruled 2026-08-25, reader, from the pane ([#297](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/297)).* **It is not a mode**, so B4's refusal does not reach it, as with B12's sheet and B14's rail. No key changes meaning while it is on. `j` and `k` scroll a row, `Space` pages, and the digits jump, all within one file's rows. **`g` and `G` keep one meaning.** Both always meant *the ends of what you can scroll to*, and the pin sets what that is. `G` reaches a real last **row** only while pinned, because the pinned file is already diffed. Without the pin, the last row of the whole diff needs every file's height added up. I4 forbids that read, so unpinned `G` goes to the last file's *heading*.

**The position decides which file is pinned. Nothing new decides it.** Every gesture that moved the viewport between files still moves it. **Follow is one of them, on purpose.** `f` and an automatic follow are explicit requests to be moved, and the pin only limits what the reader's own scrolling reaches. So pinning is not a manual scroll, and it does not disengage follow.

**The pin has no readout of its own. This is ruled.** Follow has `follow ▶` because its state is otherwise invisible: a pane that is following and one that is not draw the same screen until something changes. A pin is visible. The diff's scrollbar is scaled to one file instead of the changeset, so its thumb is larger. Only one heading is on screen, and scrolling stops at the file's end. **A pane too short for a bar is the one where the pin cannot be seen.** That is the case to reopen on, and no footer rung is spent on it now. The footer's `N/M` stays the changeset's, because it is the reader's place in the tree and the pin does not move it.

**The heading of the pinned file draws as the block's first row, and it is not sticky.** It carries the pulse, the heat strip, the sparkline and the counts, so it is kept and not dropped as redundant. A sticky heading would cost the diff a permanent row. After the heading scrolls past, the caret and the bold path still name the pinned file. **The last row of a pinned file is content.** This is the rule for the bottom of the diff, applied to one file. The blank that closes a file's block separates it from the file below, and a pinned frame draws no file below. `crates/vigia/tests/single.rs` is the gate file.

**The pin adds nothing to the frame path.** One of I4's exceptions is the diff's **height**, counted for every changed file once per tick. A pinned bar needs none of it: it only needs the pinned file's span, which the walk records on its way past. The pin skipped the walk entirely until the header's total arrived. A total is a fact about the run, not about the pane, so the walk is owed in every mode, and pinning no longer saves it. The pin must never buy a **second** walk, per frame instead of per tick. `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs::a_pinned_frame_counts_no_height_it_has_already_counted` checks this by redrawing and expecting zero. *Amended 2026-09-09, session.*

**`o` draws the file list and nothing under it.** The pane shows counters, heat strips, sparklines and the recency gradient, with no diff to read. This state answers *is it working*, not *what did it write*, which is a different question asked at a different moment. It is off by default at every width and height. *Ruled 2026-09-10, reader, from the pane ([#493](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/493)).* **It is not a mode**, for B16's reason: no key changes meaning while it is on. `J`, `K`, `n`, `p` and the digits move the caret and the list's window as before. `s` does nothing and is not refused, because the walk a pin bounds does not run in this state.

**The list runs to the bottom of the body, and the quarter-pane cap does not apply.** That cap is a share of the pane *because the map may not grow at the diff's expense* ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)). This state has no diff region, so the reason does not reach it. The region is the body less its lead row, and the entries are the changed files: thirty rows on a fifty-row pane, where every other state draws twelve. Rows the entries do not reach draw blank. The region keeps them and does not shrink to fit, because a body that reports fewer rows than the pane gave it no longer describes the pane. *Ruled 2026-09-10, session.*

**`o` wins over `r`, and gives way to an empty worktree.** A list beside a diff needs a diff, so `Body::split_rows` takes this branch before the rail's. `Chrome::rail` still carries the request, so B14 is unchanged. B3's empty sentence is drawn in the diff's region, so this branch has the rail's own `files > 0` guard. A worktree with nothing in it keeps a region to say so in.

**`o` costs one `Frame::diff` per visible list row and no diff-body walk.** That is this region's existing bound, not a new one. It is not the cheapest state: a deep list is more diffs than a shallow list over a short window. It does not pay for the walk that builds rows and highlights them. The height walk is owed here as everywhere, for B16's reason above, and `Frame::churn` folds the header's total out of it whatever the body's shape. `crates/vigia/tests/overview.rs` is the gate file.

**Toggling the pin settles the position; it does not restore it.** From a screen inside one file, pinning and unpinning returns the same screen. From a screen that straddles two files it cannot. The pin rests the pinned file's last row on the bottom, as a pager does, and that rewrites the position. The promise is that every pair of presses after the first returns the same screen. So the key is a toggle, and it does not walk the view up the file one press at a time.

**Beside the rail, the horizontal rule is gone and no vertical rule replaces it.** B11 rules that the rule between the regions stays bare. `Body::split` makes the rule and the list coextensive, so there is no boundary for a horizontal rule to sit on. No vertical rule was added. The rail's scrollbar reserve and the diff's leading inset already leave the boundary blank, and a rule that carries no fact is chrome announcing a region, which B11 already refuses.

**The rail layout needs three rulings the stacked layout did not.** The **wash** follows the region, not the screen, which §5.3 states as a law. The **margin** keeps its rung from the pane and is charged to each region's leading edge, so the pane stands back from both its edges and the boundary between the regions gets the same inset. The **caret** is afforded from the region, not the pane, because the question is whether *the row* still names its file after the marker takes its column. A 210-column pane says nothing about a 70-column rail. The caret still stands on the pane's own leading column, which in this layout is the rail's.

**The counts cell is two fixed halves of five columns**, `+9999` and `-9999`. Past that, a count takes a shorter form instead of more digits: `+10k`, `+999k`, `+1M`, `+4G`. Five columns is the narrowest width at which that abbreviation is **total**. A narrower rung drew `+0k` for a 250-line change, because two characters have no truthful form for it and the search fell through to the thousands unit. The fix cost the sparkline between 36 and 39 columns. The status bar's frame and memory cells already follow the same rule with `>1s` and `>1GiB`. **The halves do not narrow.** They are the only thing on the row that does not. **The header's total uses the same anchors**, and grows left where a half outgrows its cell. *Ruled 2026-09-09, reader.*

**A rename's label is a rung, not a token to be cut.** A row draws `new ← old` whole, or it draws the new path alone. It never elides the pair. Every other label loses its *head* when it does not fit, because a path's tail identifies the file. That does not hold for a pair. Cutting the head of `new ← old` leaves a tail that belongs to `old`. The row would then name the file the rename came **from**, not the file it is about, and nothing on screen would say so.

**The layouts are written out as a table, not allocated element by element.** The table makes narrowing monotone. Greedy allocation in priority order oscillates for a *shared* layout. Each element takes the widest rung it can afford and starves the elements after it, so a row can lose its sparkline at one width and get it back two columns later. Each row of the table gives up exactly one thing against the row above it and gains nothing, so the ladder is monotone by construction. **Narrowing drops, in order: the sparkline's resolution, the strip's resolution, the sparkline, the strip, the pulse, the note mark, and the counts last.** The counts go last because they are the row's content, not a signal drawn beside it. The note mark outlives the pulse because nothing else on a narrow pane says that a file holds a conversation. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_glance_columns_collapse_in_one_order` asserts the monotonicity as a rule. It also pins the walk with the width each state begins at, because a rule alone cannot see a boundary that moved.

**A file row draws the state of its notes in a slot of its own, and the strip tints the slice they sit in** ([#471](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/471)). `✎` is a note still waiting for the agent's answer. `↳` is a note the agent answered and nobody resolved. `✓` is a note that was resolved, while its departure draws. The worst state wins. A reply outranks a waiting note, because a reply asks the *reader* and a waiting note asks the agent. **The glyph carries the state, not the ink.** `Depth::None` drops every colour, and colour alone would leave three identical cells. `↳` already opens the agent's answer. `note_mark`, `note_mark_reply`, `note_mark_resolved` and `heat_note` are keys of their own, on the same rule as the staged mark: one hue today, four roles. **The strip shows presence, and only for an unresolved note.** It keeps `■`. A different glyph would make the slice misstate its magnitude, and a third meaning in a nine-value ramp stops a busy slice from reading as busy. The tint is placed by the note's **stored** line. An old-side line is first walked to its working-tree position, because the strip is projected onto the working-tree length. `has_heat` gives a file with no line diff no strip. The mark covers that case, which is why both exist. The mark never counts, so the mark and the footer differ by the adrift notes, which have no row. *Ruled 2026-09-08, reader, from use.*

**The table answers two questions.** Below the widest layout that shipped, the question is *what survives* as the pane narrows, and the twelve-column path floor decides it. Above that layout, the question is *what a wide pane is worth spending*. There "does it fit" is the wrong test, because a fixed-sum table takes a rung the moment it fits. The widest heat rung fits inside **71** columns, which is narrower than the 109-column render that `assets/preview.svg` is measured from. A rung chosen on the fit test alone would have made the published picture false while every gate stayed green. **So a rung above the settled ladder is taken only when the glance elements fit inside two columns in five of the row.** That puts the 24-slice strip at **139** columns and a 48-slice strip at **199**. Those are the two widths [#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161) asked for, reached by a rule derived from neither. The clamp is floored at the settled ladder's widest layout, so **no boundary that shipped can move**, whatever the share is set to. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_widest_strip_waits_until_the_path_keeps_the_row` derives the boundary from the layout's own slots, not from a failure.

**The two elements alternate down the table**, the sparkline's resolution and then the strip's, all the way down ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)). The twenty-four source buckets halve to twelve and then six.

**Every width in the paragraphs above is the block rung's.** This qualifies them. It does not correct them. They were measured when there was only one rung, and they are still exact for a terminal drawing eighth-blocks. At a dense rung the same table is reached earlier, because a slot costs `spark_cells` of its bucket count, not the count itself. So the statement that forty columns show no sparkline is true of blocks and false of braille. The *ladder* does not change. Only the price of one of its slots moves.

**There is no trailing reserve beyond the scrollbar column.** The picture leaves about a sixth of the row blank on its right. That blank is an artifact of a fixed canvas at a width where nothing degrades. It is not a promise, and I6 forbids spending it at forty columns. The two columns a row stops short of the pane's edge belong to the scrollbar, and the row pays for them whether or not a bar is drawn. The pane's inset is a *leading* margin. The inset costs a glance row one column per rung instead of two because this ruling stands and the trailing columns stay the scrollbar's.

**A visible list row costs one `Frame::diff` and no more.** That keeps the region inside I4. The bound is the region's own height, never the changed-file count, so the cost follows the window the same way the body's does. `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs` gates it beside a rail and under a stacked list. **The cost of that row depends on which side of the settle margin the file is on.** A settled file costs a `stat` and a cache hit that reads **zero bytes**. That is I2a at work, and it is the state a monitor is in for almost all of its life. A file inside the margin costs a real read, because `Frame::diff` cannot prove that a file written in the last two seconds is unchanged, and re-reads it by design. So a six-row region over a tree that is being written costs six reads a frame, not six `stat`s.

**The two regions hand entries to each other instead of asking twice.** The file an agent just wrote is always covered by the margin, always the file the diff is inside, and always in the window while the list follows. Asking the frame for it a second time read and diffed it again on every frame, in exactly the situation this tool exists for: **258,790 bytes where 36,970 will do**, over twenty files of five hundred lines. `View::collect`'s walk records the entries it builds, and `take_list` draws from those. The diff already followed the same one-pass rule. `a_screen_a_single_file_fills_reads_that_single_file` gates it as a relation: the region computes one fewer diff than it asks for. A relation survives a change to the region's height.

**The record is taken whether or not the heading fits.** A walk that built a file's entry only on the row where it drew the heading would leave the file out of the record whenever the viewport sat *inside* it. The list would then ask the frame for it again. So on a pane that has a list, the entry is built for every file the walk reaches, and drawn for the files whose heading fits. On a pane too short for a list there is no `take_list` to serve, and the undrawn entry is not built. `a_pane_with_no_list_builds_no_entry_it_cannot_draw` gates that through `View::recorded`. That counter exists because nothing else can see the difference: building an entry reads nothing, and no `FrameStats` figure moves. The cost is one `entry_of` for at most one extra file per walk, against a `Frame::diff` that is a whole-file read inside the margin. So the file being written saves a read, and a settled tree trades a syscall for a walk over lines already in memory. *[#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257).*

**The list follows the diff, and `J`/`K` move the list without moving anything else.** The list slides on its own to keep the caret visible, so the region is correct untouched, as I5 requires. The modifier is Shift because `Ctrl-J` is LF and `Ctrl-C`/`Ctrl-D` already quit. Terminal emulators and macOS Option intercept Alt. `G` already shows that case matters here. A plain letter also reaches terminals that never report a modified arrow. **`J` takes the window over, and anything that moves the diff hands it back.** This is the list's own version of follow mode. A manual scroll takes the *diff* off following and puts the *list* back on it, in the same line of the same function. Without the takeover, `J` would do nothing. Without the handback, a list the reader browsed once would never agree with the diff again. **The caret marks the current file whenever the window still shows it**, and is absent once a browsed window moves off it. The caret means *the diff is in this file*. That stays true while the reader browses, so hiding it would withhold a fact the reader can act on.

**Scrolling the list does not disengage follow mode.** This is a ruling, not an oversight. Follow is a claim about the **diff** viewport. Moving a window over the list says nothing about what the diff should show. The monitor behaviour is to browse the changed set while the diff keeps following what the agent writes. If one disengaged the other, the two would fight. `Action::is_manual_scroll` is an exhaustive match, so a new action has to answer this question instead of inheriting a default.

**Both regions carry a scrollbar, and both are exact.** The list's thumb spans the visible window over the changed-file count, and both numbers are free. The diff's thumb spans the screen's rows over the diff's **total** rows, and sits at the rows above the screen. Every other scrollbar means the same.

**A bar tall enough for one has a step button at each end, and one click is one step.** The buttons are taken out of the track, so the ladder is a floor, not a preference. `STEP_FLOOR` is two buttons plus the shortest track that can still show more than one position: four rows. It is written as that sum, not as `4`, so the threshold cannot keep an old total while every gate over the drawn output stays green. Below the floor, the column is byte for byte what it drew before there were buttons. That matters because `MIN_BODY` is 2 and a diff region can legitimately be two rows tall. The floor is per region, not per pane, and comes from the region's own height. So one drawer serves a six-row list and a thirty-row diff without a special case. *Ruled 2026-08-15, session ([#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166)).* **Holding a step button repeats it, and this program may own that clock.** The repeat has to be built, because **no mouse protocol reports a button that is still down.** `crossterm` delivers `Down`, `Up`, `Drag` and `Moved`, and a drag needs motion. So a held button arrives as nothing at all until the pointer moves or the button is released. Repeat is therefore a timer. It is allowed because **I1's budget is *0 wakeups while idle*, measured over a sixty-second idle window, and a held mouse button is not idle.** Every other timer this spec refuses would run while nothing is happening, which is the state that measure protects. This one exists only between a press on a step button and its release. The finger bounds it, and it cannot outlive the finger. `Held::wait` enforces this: with nothing held it returns `None`, and the loop blocks on an **untimed** receive. `nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` fails if that changes. I1's first sentence gains a qualifier: *never a fixed timer* means never one that runs unbidden, not never one a reader is holding down. *Ruled 2026-08-15, session.*

**I1 states this as a general rule, not as a licence for this one control.** A licence for one instance would leave the next instance to be argued again, even with the same argument. One is already visible: a press held on the **track**, not on a button, is the page-repeat every desktop scrollbar has. It is a clock bounded by a gesture, and it is not a step button. `Held` repeats whatever action it is armed with. `Action::repeated` is an exhaustive match so that a later held control inherits the mechanism. I1 states the three conditions, and this control applies them.

**The first repeat comes after 500ms, then one every 50ms. The numbers are taken from toolkits, not chosen.** Qt's `QScrollBar` presses a control with `initialDelay = 500` and repeats at `50`. GTK exposes the same pair, with the repeat defaulting to 50ms. Two toolkits use one shape, so the cadence matches any desktop scrollbar. The delay keeps a single click a single step. **The rate does not accelerate**, and this is ruled. A step button is for placing the viewport precisely, and acceleration overshoots. For travel there is the wheel, `j`/`k`, `d`/`u`, `n`/`p`, the digits, `g`, `G` and the thumb.

**A late repeat folds into one action, not a backlog, and this is a correctness claim.** `Action::repeated` multiplies a relative step by the number of whole intervals that actually elapsed. A tick that arrives four intervals late applies `Scroll(5)` through one `apply` and one paint, not five of each. So the rate depends on time, not on paint speed. A slow pane scrolls the same rows per second as a fast one, with coarser steps, and it never keeps moving after the finger lifts. The deadline advances by the steps taken, not to *now plus one interval*. So a tick handled late cannot push the next one later and slow the rate under load. **The mechanism is general, not specific to the scrollbar.** Every action states how it combines with itself. The actions that return themselves unchanged are ruled that way: a held page is the reader's own key repeat, and `Top`, `Bottom` and `File` name a destination that does not add up. An action added later stops that match compiling until it answers.

**A hold ends in five ways.** First, a release of any button. Second, any key press: a hand on the keyboard is not holding a mouse, and `q` must not be answered by a program that is still stepping. Third, a **pointer move**. `MouseEventKind::Moved` is motion with no button down, so it proves a release, and it catches an `Up` that never arrived. Fourth, a drag off the button. A twitch inside the same cell keeps the hold. Fifth, **the window losing focus**, which I1's second condition requires: without it the repeat keeps stepping and repainting, on a clock, in a pane nobody is looking at. A desktop scrollbar resumes when the pointer comes back. This one does not, because resuming needs a suspended state that `input.rs` has nowhere to keep. **A drag onto a button does nothing.** The reader grabbed the thumb and pulled past the end of the track. Acting on it would need both stepping and clamping to know that the drag *began* on a button.

**A gesture in progress is lit, in the same way in three places.** A held step button, a thumb being dragged, and the arrows while keys scroll all take `Theme::bar_active`. That style is brighter than the mark's resting style, not a different hue, because the element keeps its meaning and only its state changes. This matters most where nothing else responds: pressing *up* at the top of a diff moves no row, and without the lit cell the control looks like decoration.
