It costs no row, no rect and no wake, because the frames it changes are frames the gesture was already painting. **The arrows answering the keyboard are the one lit thing nobody is touching.** They earn it because the arrows exist to show *which way*. `j` lights the down arrow and `k` the up one. Magnitude is not direction, so a half page lights what a single row lights. **Each region lights its own bar.** The two regions move different things and answer different keys, so the mark shows the region as well as the direction. `j`/`k`/`d`/`u`/`Space`/`g`/`G` and the file steps light the diff's bar. `J`/`K` light the list's. A jump or a drag lights neither.

**The keyboard's lit arrow expires. The other two do not.** A release ends a hold. A key burst just stops and sends nothing, so without an expiry the last arrow of a burst would stay lit on an idle tree and describe the past. `SCROLL_LINGER` is 220ms: long enough to bridge the gap between two key repeats, and short enough that a reader who stopped does not see it. It is the one number here that is a judgement of feel, not a measurement, and it is stated as one. The burst that armed the clock bounds it, and it fires once, so it meets I1's three conditions like the repeat does. `input::patience` asks every clock at once, so *is there a timer at all* has one answer and one gate.

**Hover gets no expiry because it does not need one. I1 does not refuse it either.** A linger armed by a motion event would meet I1's three conditions exactly as `SCROLL_LINGER` does. It could not start on its own, it would fire once, and the idle path would stay untimed by construction. Citing I1 against it would be the citation without a check that B10 was reversed for. It is refused for what it **does**: an expiry puts the mark out while a reader rests on a row, and resting on a row is the one case hover exists for. **This is stated as one rule because B10's decline was read as a rule against every pointer-derived mark.** Under that reading the lit step button would be a mistake. It is not, because B10 never refused that. `input::hover_after` is the rule, with nothing added.

**A drag keeps the bar it started on, wherever the pointer goes.** `action_for` asks what is under the pointer. That is the right question for a press and the wrong one once a hand is moving, and a hand does not stay inside a one-column target. So a press on a track takes hold of that region. Every motion after it resolves against that region alone, with the row **clamped**. Pulling above the track holds the first window, and pulling below it holds the last. Every scrollbar does this. A press on a step *button* grabs nothing, because that gesture is already a step. *Ruled 2026-08-16, reader-reported.* **A button is chrome, drawn in the bar's own dim style, not the thumb's.** Its shape tells it apart from the track, which has the same weight, so the thumb stays the only lit thing on the column. The glyphs are `▲` and `▼`, which are outside CP437. They stay because **every** candidate arrow is outside it too (`▴ ▾ ↑ ↓` alike, and CP437's own two triangles sit at control-code positions), and a direction is the one thing this shape has to say. On such a console the loss is two cells, on a column that otherwise survives whole. §10's Windows bullet carries the list, and [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159) owns the ladder. This ruling does not add a ladder inside the scrollbar.

**The diff's bar is exact.** A bar that estimates the whole from the current file's height vanishes on a short file, balloons on a long one, and never reaches the bottom, because the trailing files are rarely the height of the one being read. I4 forbids diffing every changed file for a total, so the total comes from the heights the walk already has. Diffing every file is unaffordable, but a total does not need it. A height is hunk boundaries and line counts. A `FileDiff` is those plus an owned `String` per drawn line. Counting instead of building took the reference fixture from **442.71ms to 8.76ms**, against `git diff --numstat`'s 46ms for the same work. §3's I4 carries the narrowing that allows the walk. *Reported from use within the hour.*

**Keys.**

| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| `q`, `Esc`, `Ctrl-C`, `Ctrl-D` | quit |
| `j`, `↓` | scroll down one row |
| `k`, `↑` | scroll up one row |
| `Space`, `PgDn` | page down |
| `PgUp` | page up |
| `d` | half a page down |
| `u` | half a page up |
| `g`, `Home` | first changed file, or the pinned file's first row (B16) |
| `G`, `End` | last changed file, or the pinned file's last screenful (B16) |
| `n`, `→` | next changed file |
| `p`, `←` | previous changed file |
| `1`–`6` | put the diff at the Nth listed file |
| `J`, `Shift-↓` | scroll the file list down one row |
| `K`, `Shift-↑` | scroll the file list up one row |
| `f` | engage follow mode, or disengage it |
| `r` | put the pinned list beside the diff, or back above it |
| `s` | show one file, or the whole diff |
| `a` | draw the staged run beside the unstaged one, or stop drawing it (B17) |
| `b` | stand the pane at the branch point, or back at the live one |
| `w` | wrap a long line onto the row below, or clip it (B19) |
| `c` | draw the note rows, or stop drawing them (B21) |
| `?` | draw the gestures sheet, advance it a page, or stop drawing it |
| click a content row's gutter | open the note box under that line, with the note already there if one is (B21) |
| click a note's left side | take that note back (B21) |
| `Enter`, `Esc`, in the note box | send the note, or close the box and send nothing. `Alt-Enter` is a newline, and every other key is the box's |
| mouse wheel | scroll the region under the pointer |
| drag either scrollbar | move that region, until the button comes up, wherever the pointer goes |
| click either scrollbar's end | step that region by one |
| click a listed file | put the diff at that file |
| terminal resize | redraw, no state change |

**`d` and `u` move half a page, with no overlap row.** They are `less`'s own bindings. The follow-mode paragraph below already claims `less +F` semantics, so the precedent is internal as well as cultural. A whole page steps `height - 1`, so one row survives the seam and the reader keeps their place. A half page already leaves half the screen in place. Taking a row off it would pay twice for one anchor and put `d` and `u` out of step with each other. Both directions round down, so the pair is an exact inverse on an odd body as well as an even one. **`Ctrl-D` and `Ctrl-U` are refused on purpose.** `Ctrl-D` is one of four ways out, and turning a way out into a scroll is a surprise this map avoids everywhere else. `D` and `U` are unbound for the same reason as `F`: they sit one row above a pair where case tells the two apart.

**The half page has no hint, and any hint added to the bar is paid for out of the readouts.** The hints and the readouts share the footer's one row. A wider bar moves the column where the frame time starts and the column where the pair starts. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::a_wider_hint_bar_cannot_quietly_push_the_readouts_out` pins both boundaries. So a new hint must justify what it spends, and the gate moves to the widths it measures, never around them. This refusal costs little: `Space`, `PgDn` and `PgUp` have never been hinted either.

**The file is a unit of movement, and it was the only one missing.** Rows are `j`/`k`, screens are `Space` and `d`, and the two ends are `g` and `G`. None of them is the unit the pinned list draws, the header counts and the reader thinks in. `n` and `p` step one changed file, and so do `→` and `←` (§11.2 **B15**). Vertical keys move inside the diff and horizontal keys move between files, so the four arrows form one set. `1` to `6` put the diff at the Nth row the list is drawing. **All of these keys land on the file's heading**, as a click on a listed file does. None of them costs a diff: a heading is row zero, so nothing asks how tall anything is, and I4 never sees it. **A follow jump lands differently.** These keys name a *file*, the unit the list draws and the header counts. I5 promises the *change*. Every gesture that `Action::is_manual_scroll` counts as a manual scroll cancels a pending landing, so these keys never inherit a landing that follow armed. **B4 is untouched.** All three move the *diff*, as a click does, and nothing is remembered.

**`p` inside a file goes to the previous file, never to the top of the current one.** At either end of the changed set, the step moves nothing. The pager habit of going to the top of this section first is refused, for two reasons. One key would mean two things depending on where the viewport happened to be. And `g` already reaches a top. Clamping the file index is also refused. It would make `n` at the last file jump *backwards* and `p` at the first jump forwards, so a key would undo what its own letter says. **Follow mode still disengages at both ends**, even where nothing moved. On this map, follow yields to what the reader meant to do, not to whether the arithmetic landed somewhere new.

**The digits address the window, not the changed set.** `3` means the third row the list is drawing, so after browsing with `J` it names a different file than before. Whatever is visible can be named. Two bounds make that true. The first is the **drawn rows**. A digit past them is the ordinary case, and only a keystroke can reach it: the region bounds a pointer, and nothing bounds a keyboard. The second is the **file count**. It looks redundant, but it guards against a screen that is **out of date**. The row count comes from the last frame drawn. Between that paint and the next keystroke the changed set can shrink (`git reset --hard`, a branch switch, or the agent reverting its own work). Without the file-count bound, `6` would still pass the window bound. **A mutation settled which bound does the work.** Deleting the file-count bound left every other gate in this file's suite green, and `crates/vigia/tests/list.rs::a_digit_after_the_diff_shrank_names_no_file` now holds it. Both bounds refuse silently, with no bell and no error. `0` and `7` to `9` are not bound at all, which is different on purpose. An unbound key is no action. A bound key that names an undrawn row would be a jump that lands nowhere and still spends the reader's follow mode.

**The digits stop at six because six is the deepest list *every* pane draws**, so `3` means the same thing at every height. A key that worked only above some pane height would be the intermittent affordance this section refuses, one region over, for the caret's own column. The extra rows of a taller pane are reached with `J`/`K`, `n`/`p` and clicking. The region bounds a click, not the key grammar, so a click reaches every drawn row at every height. `crates/vigia/tests/list.rs::clicking_a_listed_file_sends_the_diff_to_it` runs at 24 rows and at 50 for that reason, and `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::the_digits_cover_the_settled_cap_and_stop_there` is the other half. **None of the three has a hint, for [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121)'s reason.** The eighty-column footer is exactly full, so any fifth hint is paid for out of the frame-time and memory cells. `Space`, `PgDn`, `PgUp`, `d` and `u` are an established family of unhinted keys. A numbered list could serve as the affordance, but **the list draws no numbers**. `rows__a_real_repository_draws.snap` draws a caret column and then the kind letter, with no rank on any row. So the digits are the first binding with no drawn affordance at all. The thumb, the list and every hinted key each have one. Whether to draw the rank is ruled below. [#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147) owns the rung question underneath.

**The list does not draw the rank the digits address.** The region is a map, and a rank would make it a menu. B4 keeps the list not navigable: selection implies focus, focus implies a mode, and modes are reviewer-class. The caret is a fact about *state*: the diff is in this file. A rank is a fact about the *keymap*: pressing this goes there. Drawn, it would be the first thing in the region that is an instruction rather than content or location, on every row, all the time. Numbered rows are the file-manager idiom, and a file manager is a surface you operate. `btop`, which §2 names as this tool's class, numbers nothing. **A full footer is a reason not to add a fifth hint, not a reason to put the hint somewhere cheaper.** §5.3 puts advice in the footer, and [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121) measured that bar exactly full at eighty columns. Drawing the digit on a content row moves advice into a region this spec does not meter, and every later binding that cannot afford a hint would claim the same. **A rank is also read.** It sits in the leftmost cell, first in the eye's path and last in what it says about the worktree. That is the §5 glance law that retired the pulse's label ([#99](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/99)). The digits are the fourth way to a file, after `j`/`k`, `n`/`p` and a click, so a reader who never finds them loses a shortcut, not a capability. The cost is that `README.md` is the only place anyone meets them, as with the pager keys. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_caret_column_draws_a_mark_and_never_a_rank` fails if the gutter ever gains a rank. *Ruled 2026-08-14, session ([#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149)).*

**The case to reopen on is not discoverability.** The digits address the **drawn window**, so which digits are live depends on pane height and changed-file count, and nothing on screen says so. A drawn rank would show that where a hint cannot. It still loses, for two reasons. A digit that names no drawn row refuses silently and moves nothing. And §5.3 prices **ink** at zero but not **attention**, and the glance surface is where attention is the budget. Drawing the rank and the caret together only at widths wide enough for a third column is also refused, because the region's left edge would then depend on width. **What reopens it is [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72)'s week of real use**: readers who do not find the digits, or who reach for a number and get nothing.

**The caret moves through the window, and the window moves as little as it can.** The window holds still while the current file is inside it. When the file leaves, the window moves by exactly the overshoot. So scrolling from the start walks the caret down the rows first, and only then does the list move under it. **Two fixed positions were ruled first, and both were wrong** in opposite directions, for one reason: a caret on a constant row does not follow a file, it drags the window on every step. Ending the window on the current file showed the six files *before* the six the diff was drawing. Starting the window on it pinned the caret to the first row, so the list scrolled while the marker never moved. Minimal movement covers both. At the top, the caret is on the first row because the file is first. At the end, the pull-back rests the last file on the bottom row. *Both reported from use.*

**A click on a listed file puts the diff at it.** Readers try that gesture without being told, because the region draws the names of the things it maps. It is **not** selection, and B4 stands. Nothing is remembered, no row becomes special, no mode appears, and the next event means what it would mean with no click before it. The same argument already allowed dragging a scrollbar.

**The pointer is acknowledged when it arrives, not only when it acts.** What it is over is marked, on three surfaces and nowhere else: **a step button, a scrollbar, and a listed file**. **Nothing in the diff body is marked.** There, a drag shows where it is going as it goes, and `Regions::hover_at` answers `None` for every row. Cost is not the question. Motion already reaches this loop and already draws, so **the mark itself** adds no wake, no paint and no frame. It adds only an `Option` of a target and a style on cells that were being redrawn anyway. *§11.2 B10, ruled 2026-08-16, reversing the previous day's decline ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123)).*

**The build added one cost the ruling never priced: the focus step's own traffic.** Asking for `?1004h` makes `FocusGained` and `FocusLost` arrive on Unix and macOS, where they never did before. `run` draws once per drained batch, whether or not a wake in it produced an action, so each event costs a wake and a frame. `FocusLost` earns it, because clearing the mark is a visible change. **`FocusGained` does not.** It retires nothing, produces no action, and repaints a byte-identical screen. How often the window is tabbed back to bounds it. It is an external event, not a clock, so `Shell::patience` never consults it and the idle receive stays untimed. Windows has paid the same repaint since before this branch, because its console delivers `FOCUS_EVENT` records whether or not anyone asked. This is recorded, not fixed: the repaint per batch belongs to [#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154).

**A click is always brighter than a hover, and that rule decides which elements can carry a mark at all.** A gesture has to outrank a pointer that is only resting, or *you are doing this* looks the same as *you could*. On the bar there are three weights: `Theme::bar_track` at rest, `Theme::bar_hover` under a pointer, and `Theme::bar_active` under a press or a drag. The rule lives in the **ordering**, not in the middle weight. The pressed mark exists for the case where nothing else can answer: pressing *up* at the top of a diff moves no row. A hover that outranked it there would remove the only answer in the one case it exists for. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::a_gesture_is_always_brighter_than_a_pointer_at_rest` asserts this as a rule across every surface, not only at the element that forced it.

**The thumb and the track are one target.** A press anywhere on a track seeks, so a click acts on the whole column, and the thumb answers because it is the part that would move. `Theme::bar_hover` is its weight. **A listed file takes `Theme::path_hover`, which underlines.** Intensity belongs to recency for anything durable, and a mark that follows the pointer is not durable. So it keeps `Theme::bar_hover`'s colour instead of adding a fourth weight above the ladder. On `ansi`, where the foreground equals `Theme::path_cold`'s, the underline carries the mark. **Clearing the mark is a ladder, and this ruling is the list of its rungs.** Motion inside the pane clears exactly, because `?1003h` reports at cell granularity, so leaving one row arrives as entering the next. `FocusChange` asks for the event that says the window was left. It is the fourth step of `TAKEOVER`, and `terminal.rs::the_takeover_takes_every_step_there_is` holds it. Every terminal checked delivers it. tmux's `focus-events` defaults to off, so under a multiplexer that rung is missing and the screen cannot say so ([#188](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/188) is the unverified half). **Any repaint that moves the bars retires the mark**, because a mark is a claim about the screen it was resolved against. `input::repainted` does this between the layout and the paint that uses it. Correcting the field after drawing would fix the state but not the screen. `RULINGS.md`'s two B10 sections hold the survey and the trail.

**A hover mark is the quietest thing that is still visible in its region.** That pays for the gap the ladder leaves. When the pointer leaves the pane with the window still focused, on a tree nobody is writing to, the last target stays lit until the pointer comes back. This is accepted, not fixed. A stale mark at that weight costs a glance nothing and never competes with the caret, which means *the diff is in this file*.

**The mark is view state, never an action, and that keeps B4 intact.** The loop resolves the mark from `Regions` and draws it, as it draws a pressed step button. The mark is not a keymap entry, so no focus appears, and every binding means the same with or without a mark. `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::pointer_motion_over_a_laid_out_screen_is_still_no_action` holds that line: motion alone is still no action. That separates it from the drag B20 rules on.

**A drawn thumb is a published affordance, so both thumbs are draggable**, and a press anywhere on a track jumps there. The scrollbar column is tested before the region that contains it. Otherwise every drag would read as a wheel. **A track maps onto travel, not onto the whole.** The list's travel is its file count less its own height. The diff's travel is its total rows less a screenful. Mapping onto the whole leaves the last screenful of track dead: every fraction past the bound clamps to the same window, and the pointer reaches the bottom with the view still short of the end.

**Scroll position is `(file, offset within that file)`, never a row index.** So a frame that changes something above the viewport does not move the view. This is a correctness property, not an implementation detail. With a row index, an agent writing to a file earlier in the list would yank the reader's position on every keystroke it makes.

**A position past the end of the diff resolves to the last screenful, not the last row.** These are different places. Resolving to the last row draws one line of content over a blank screen, while the header still correctly counts the changed files, so one thing on screen contradicts the other two. A pager rests the final row at the *bottom* of the viewport, and so does this. Only `View::collect` resolves it, because `App::view` stores the resolved position back on every frame. So there is one rule in one place, not a clamp in the scroll path and another behind it. Two things reach it, and **only one of them is scrolling**. The other is the diff shrinking under a position that was reasonable when it was taken: `git reset --hard`, a branch switch, or an agent reverting its own work. The walk back reads only the files the screen is about to draw, so I4 is untouched. **A step down from a frame that drew the diff's last row holds**, as a pager does at its end, so the short pane that `G` and follow leave does not jump a screenful back on the first `j`. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session* ([#62](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/62)).

**`G` does something else on purpose.** `End` goes to the top of the last *file*. Cost is not the reason, since `Frame::height` adds up every file's height once a tick. The reason is that `g` and `G` are the two ends of the *file list*, the pair the header counts and the pinned region draws. A `G` that landed mid-file would make the two keys use different units.

**Follow mode, which is I5.** It has `less +F` semantics, and it is the toggle the README mockup already published. Follow is **on at startup**. **Any manual scroll disengages it.** **`f` re-engages it and jumps straight to the newest change**, without waiting for the next one. The footer shows `follow ▶` while it is engaged. Two boundaries are load bearing, because each one would make the mode quietly wrong instead of visibly broken. A **terminal resize does not disengage**: it moves no viewport and expresses no intent, and a monitor beside an agent is resized constantly. **`G` disengages and does not re-engage**, because "jump to the last changed file" and "resume following" are different intents that would otherwise share a key.

**The newest change is the file whose write landed last in the settled batch.** The batch is the coalesced tick. A monitor cannot find that file by looking. Running `stat` on every changed file breaches I9 at scale, as [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19) records, and I4 forbids reading files the frame does not draw. It does not need to look, because the filesystem event already carries the path. So following costs no read, no `stat` and no diff. When the named path is not in the diff (an index write, or an edit reverted before the tick landed), the view stays where it is, because there is no newest *change* to follow. **Follow lands on the change, not on the heading, when those are different places.** A file whose diff fits in one screenful keeps I5's promise by accident of size, because its heading and its change are in the same place. A file with a 76-line deletion under three one-line tweaks does not: the pane showed a filename and some unchanged context instead of what the agent just did. **Follow keeps the heading on the top row while the first changed line of the file's busiest hunk is already drawn from there. When it is not, follow puts that hunk's header on the top row.** The heading carries the path, the counts, the sigil and the heat strip, so follow gives it up only when that buys the change. A taller pane is moved off the heading less often. *Ruled 2026-08-22, reader, from a live pane ([#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257)).*

**Follow uses the busiest hunk, not the first.** The first is the obvious choice and cannot reach this case at all. A block is a heading, then a header plus lines for each hunk, so the first hunk's header is always row one, and landing on it moves the pane by one row. **Follow uses a hunk, not the heat strip's busiest slice.** The slice has the same intent in the wrong unit. A bucket names a working-tree line, a viewport needs a row, and a row falls inside a hunk. Ties keep the earlier hunk. A file with nothing to land on keeps its heading.

**The test checks the first changed *line* twice. Testing the header instead would miss a real case, not a rounding error.** A hunk opens with up to three unchanged lines. So a pane one row short of them draws the `@@` and none of the change, which is the reported symptom while the gate reports it handled. A landing therefore needs the change to be **not drawn from the heading and drawn from the landing**. The second half also sets `Body::split`'s floor: a one-row diff region cannot show a changed line from anywhere, and one bare `@@` is strictly less than the heading it would replace.

**A landing fills the pane. A jump does not.** A viewport reached by scrolling backs up instead of resting a short tail over blank rows. A viewport reached by a *jump* does not back up, because a jump claims which file belongs on the top row. A landing claims less: only that the change is **visible**, which any row that draws it satisfies. So a landing backs up. The change moves down the pane, not off it, and the rows below it are diff, not blank. This applies only to a landing that moved the viewport. A landing that kept the heading is an ordinary jump.

**Follow does not pick the *newest* hunk, and that is the case to reopen on.** The question is *what did it just do*, and the diff alone cannot answer it. The newest hunk is the one that differs from the previous tick's diff, which is a comparison with staleness rules of its own. The busiest hunk is never worse than the heading it replaces, and it is right in the reported shape. The case that would reopen this is the accumulating one, where a rewrite from ten minutes ago outvotes the tweak that just landed. The answer there is that comparison, not a third heuristic.

**A landing belongs to the file it was armed for, and to no other.** It names a row *inside a file*, while the viewport holds an index. Every advance renumbers the changed set: a file committed, an edit reverted, a branch switched. Ticks coalesce, and only the paint is shared. So an advance can land between the follow that armed a landing and the frame that resolves it. That includes a tick that names no path at all, such as a `.git/index` write. So the landing is applied only while the position still names the path the tick named. A refused landing is **settled**, not held.

**Following costs nothing, and its signature keeps it that way.** `App::follow` takes `&Frame`, so it cannot diff, cannot read and cannot `stat`. For the same reason it cannot find the row, because the loop advances, then follows, and only then draws. At the moment of the jump, the frame holds the previous tick's diff for the one file that just changed. The jump names the file. The walk that draws the file resolves the row once, from a diff it has already fetched. The gates are `tests/follow.rs::following_a_file_costs_no_diff_and_no_read` and `::landing_on_a_change_costs_no_extra_diff`.

**How the chrome fits, which is I6.** One rule decides the layout: **a thing made of items breaks, a thing made of characters marks its edge, and content is neither.**

The **hint bar is a list**. When the footer cannot hold both halves on one line, it takes a **second line** instead of shortening anything. The state moves to the upper line and the hints keep the bottom row, so narrowing a pane never moves the hints. The footer grows only while at least two body rows survive, only when there is a state worth moving, and only when a hint would fill the line. So widening costs the body one row once, where the shortest hint first fits. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session* ([#283](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/283)). A notice replaces the hints. It takes the whole line when there is one, but it **never causes it**. A notice is transient, and a footer that grew for one would jog the diff. Below the width where even a full line holds the bar, the bar drops **whole hints**, never part of one: `q quit` first, then `? keys`, leaving `f follow` last.

**The bar is three items**: `q quit · f follow · ? keys`. It is twenty-three columns at its widest. The four-item bar was thirty-eight. B12 is the reason it can be this short: the keymap is on a sheet, so a bar naming four gestures of sixteen showed an arbitrary quarter of a table that opens in full. **The bar holds three things**: the way out, the state that can be lost without anyone seeing, and the door to everything else. `? keys` ranks above `q quit`, because without the keymap nothing else can be found. It ranks below `f follow`, because a sheet nobody opened costs nothing, while a follow mode nobody can see costs the screen. **The arrows are not on the bar.** The sheet names them. `f follow` keeps the last rung because nobody guesses it, and everybody guesses an arrow key. *Ruled 2026-08-17, reader, from use ([#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80)).*

**There is no bonus rung.** The widest bar the pane owes decides the footer's height, and every rung is owed. So `HINT_BASELINE` is rung zero, and no hint can buy a body row by existing. **The state is two columns from the hints, not one.** Every other group on the row is two columns from the next, and at one column the state read as a fourth hint.

**Advice outlives instrumentation.** The two status readouts, `0.8ms frame` and the memory cell, sit left of the state as one **diagnostics** group. The footer drops the memory cell first and the frame cell second, so both are gone before any hint is. The hints *operate* the tool, and the state describes what the *tree* is doing. The readouts describe **`vigia` itself**, the least of the three things a narrow pane owes anyone. Memory goes before frame time, because the frame cell reports a budget someone can act on.

**Neither readout can change how many rows the footer takes.** This is a rule, not a side effect. `Footer::plan` decides the height from the state ladder alone. The diagnostics take only what is left after the hints have their widest fitting rung. Otherwise two things would jog the diff down a row and back for no visible reason. The frame cell **does not exist on the first paint**, because no frame has completed to have a p99. The memory cell does not exist on a platform with no cheap read.

**The footer's right-hand side carries three colours, taken from the picture.** `assets/preview.svg` draws `0.8ms` and `24MiB` in its cyan, the word `frame` in the same grey as the hints, and the follow marker in green. The shipped footer drew all of it in one grey. So the two numbers someone *checks* and the marker that says the mode is on looked like words someone *reads*. §5.1's rule that a published artifact answering a question is the answer applies to colour as it does to layout. The position `N/M` keeps its grey: the picture gives it no colour, and it is a place, not a measurement. Both styles are **reused, not named anew**, so this is a reading of the picture, not a palette decision. The cyan is the colour of every other structural mark, and the green is the diff's own added colour. The footer applies them as a **second pass over the cells**, not as separate placements, and this is load bearing. Each readout is part of a token that a ladder picks whole. Placing each colour separately would take that choice away from the ladders, and the footer's width arithmetic would have to know about colour. Tinting afterwards cannot move a column.

**The pass must terminate, and it must not reach past the diagnostics.** Both are rulings because both were once wrong. A walk that asks whether a cell *opens* a measurement and whether it *carries* one must consume the opening cell whatever it says. `>` answers yes and no, so on `>1s` the two questions spun against each other and the pane froze mid-frame. The pass looks for the marker only within the state's own columns, never the whole row. A notice is an error string that can carry a path, and `▶` is a legal filename character.

**Both cells have a constant width, whatever they say.** `0.8ms frame`, ` 12ms frame` and `999ms frame` are all eleven columns, with the number right-aligned inside them. So a value that changes between frames never shuffles what is beside it. Past the field, a sigil replaces the number: `>1s` for a frame and `>1GiB` for memory. The exact value at those sizes says nothing the sigil does not, and a number that grew a column would move the footer.

**The header never grows**, on either side. A worktree name is not a list and has nowhere to break, so it marks its edge like every other single token. The clause it leads is made of items, and it breaks by dropping a whole rung, not by taking a line. A second line cannot guarantee a fit either way, and it would spend a body row on a maybe.

**The header is laid out by subject, like the footer.** The left side holds two facts about the **tree**: the worktree name, then how much of that tree changed. The right side holds one more: how much changed in lines. Where there is no total to draw, the right side falls back to the one fact about **`vigia` itself**. The count reads `changed`, not `files`. `vigia · 3 files` would be a **worse** claim than the one it replaced, since the repository has more than three files in it. Beside the name of a tree, a bare noun describes the tree. `changed` makes the number a fact *about* the tree. It is also one rule where there were two, because `changed` is a participle with no plural to inflect.

**The ladder keeps its order, and only the side each rung drops from changes.** Widest first: `{worktree} · {N} changed` with the right-hand side, then the worktree name with it, then the worktree name alone. That is the ladder, not the whole list of screens, because the two sides degrade independently. The left side's facts drop rightmost first, and each one qualifies the one before it.

**The header shows where the pane stands, and every fact beside it describes that run.** `12 changed` under `since origin/main` counts what the branch has done, not what is unstaged. So the token and the number move together, or the header would describe two comparisons at once. The token sits between the worktree name and the count, because it qualifies the count. **Its rank in the ladder is not fixed.** When it reads `current`, it names the pane everyone already has, so a narrowing header drops it first and the count keeps its place. When it reads anything else, it is dropped **last**, because a count drawn without it reads as the working tree's, which is exactly what it is not. This amends #67, which ruled that the count sits next to the worktree name. Next to now means through this token or directly, and nothing else may come between them. *Ruled 2026-09-10, reader.*

**The left-hand side leads with the worktree, not the program.** This is the one open departure from `assets/preview.svg`, per §5.1's departure ledger. A title bar that names the program spends six of forty columns on the one thing everyone already knows. What looking does not tell you is *which tree*, and that decides whether two panes side by side mean anything. The header shows the worktree's own name **even when the path it was given does not contain one**. `vigia .` hands `gix` a workdir of `.`, which has no final component, so the header drew `.`. The path is now resolved before its last component is taken, for **display** only. Resolving it where the watch compares event paths would introduce `\\?\C:\` on Windows and `/private/var` on macOS ([#30](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/30)).

**A binary file is counted on the left and left out of the total**, so a run of twenty-eight whose total describes twenty-six says which files are missing from it. The binary count is drawn only when there are some, since `0 binary` spends a column on the ordinary case. It sits beside the count it qualifies, not beside the total. On the right, it would move the one figure that lines up against the rows below. *Ruled 2026-09-09, reader.*

**The right-hand side carries one thing, and the total outranks the word.** The word goes there only when there is nothing to count, and the picture, with changes to count, draws the total. The total is the one figure that looking at the rows cannot add up. **A total of nothing is not a total**: an empty tree draws the word, not `+0 -0`.
| Mode | What it means |
|---|---|
| `watching` | the watch is live, so the screen follows the tree |
| `not watching` | the watch never armed, or it ended, so this is a still picture |

**There are two words because of I1, not for minimalism.** §5.1 read `watching` as implying a settling state and an idle state as well. It implies neither, because both are *durations*, and this shell wakes only when something changes. `Watcher::next_tick` blocks until a burst has settled, so the shell is never awake **during** settling. "Idle" would need a wake that says nothing happened, which is the timer I1 forbids. A word for either state could appear and then never be replaced. **The word starts at `watching`, before the watch is armed.** Arming happens after first paint, so the watch does not see the shell's own setup reads. A separate word for those microseconds would flicker on every launch, to describe a state that always resolves the same way within one wake. A real arming failure arrives as its own wake and corrects the word. **The negative is `not watching`, not `stalled` and not `still`.** It is the mockup's word negated, so learning one teaches both. `stalled` reads as temporary when this is not, and `still` means both "motionless" and "continuing".

**The mode is state, and the reason for it is not.** When a watch ends, `not watching` goes on the header **and** its error goes on the footer as a notice. The header carries the durable fact: this diff is no longer live. The notice carries which failure caused it, which is not durable. **A dead watch is drawn loud and a live one stays quiet**: `watching` takes the header's dim grey, and `not watching` takes the footer's alert. A state nobody can catch at a glance has not been reported, and §5 makes colour half the differentiator.

**The footer has three voices, and neither their colour nor their motion is invented.** *Ruled 2026-09-04, reader*, reported from the pane. Every message on the footer was `alert` red, a clipboard receipt included, so the line said the same thing about a success and a failure. The kind of a message cannot be recovered from its text, and one call site produces two kinds, so the voice travels from the producer.

| Voice | What it is | Colour | How the colour travels | Takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Said | An act of the reader's, answering | `chrome` | left to right, like something being written | 550ms |
| Arrived | Unasked-for, nothing wrong | `note` | the whole line at once | 750ms |
| Alert | Something went wrong | `alert` | from both ends, closing | 450ms |

All three colours were already in the palette. This program uses `note` for things that are not diff content, and an announcement is one of those.

**The transition is a crossfade, and the glyphs never move.** A status bar is static, and its text is the only thing on it that changes. So an arrival is a *change of that text*, not an entrance. Revealing characters one by one was tried, and it was reported from the pane: it leaves the line unreadable while it runs, and a message must never be unreadable. So the letters are there from the first frame, and only the colour travels, eased at both ends, over a duration someone can watch. Speed carries the urgency, as on a phone. The warning is quickest because it has to be read now. The announcement is slowest because nobody asked for it.

**Where the colour depth leaves both ends the same colour, there is no transition at all**, and the message simply appears. A phone gives the same answer for reduced motion. It is also why the motion is not rebuilt on the glyph channel to survive: a transition nobody can see is better skipped than replaced by one that makes the text harder to read.

**A message replaces the one before it without cutting it off.** The outgoing message runs its departure first, and the new one begins when the line is free, so a change is always one thing fading into another. `NOTICE_LINGER` is 4.5 seconds, and it is the **whole** of a receipt's or a warning's time on the line. The departure comes out of it, not after it, so the slowest voice is 750ms in, three seconds settled, and 750ms out. **An announcement stays a minute**, `ARRIVED_LINGER`. *Ruled 2026-09-05, reader*: a receipt answers a gesture just made, so it finds the reader looking. The update notice arrives while the reader is looking at the other pane, and four and a half seconds was gone before anyone read it.

**A newer published version produces one footer notice, once, and nothing else.** *Ruled 2026-09-04, reader* ([#401](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/401)). One request goes out at startup, on a thread, so first paint never waits on it. When crates.io answers with a higher version, one notice names it, held for `ARRIVED_LINGER`. Every other outcome is **silence**: already current, offline, refused, slow, unparseable, or an architecture the TLS provider will not compile on. A network problem is not the reader's problem, and a monitor that nags gets closed. The check arms no clock of its own, which keeps I1 whole. It answers once and cannot repeat, and the only deadline is the one every notice already sets. `VIGIA_UPDATE=off` declines the check. A value the program does not understand is refused before it takes the terminal, not quietly ignored.

**The right-hand side outranks everything else wherever it fits at all**, so it is placed first. The file count summarises a body that is on screen and can be counted. The line total and whether the pane is still live cannot be recovered from anywhere else. **It is not, however, the last thing left on the row.** The two sides have independent budgets. The right side is all-or-nothing at its own width, while the name marks its edge at any width above zero. So on a live watch with nothing to count, the name has the row alone from 5 to 7 columns, the word has it alone from 8, a marked fragment of the name rejoins it at 10, and both are drawn whole from 14. With `not watching`, which is four columns wider, those widths are 5 to 11, 12, 14 and 18. **A total takes the same rungs at its own width.** Where it does not fit, the word takes the side back, so a pane too narrow to count still says whether it is live. **`crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_header_degrades_at_the_widths_the_spec_records` gates those numbers.** They were wrong here first, and a measurement quoted only in prose drifts from what it measured without anything failing.

**A file in a language this process has not met yet arrives plain and is coloured a moment later.** The text appears immediately, in the pane's ordinary few milliseconds. The colour follows when the grammar has compiled off the frame: a cold screenful measures 124ms under Rust and 695ms under Markdown. **This replaces a freeze, not colour.** The loop is single-threaded, and the pane stopped answering while the grammar compiled. This is I7's opening rule applied more widely, not a new rule. I7 already draws a process's first frame without colour, because a compile belongs behind a screen that has content on it. The same reasoning now covers the frame that meets a *grammar*, not only the frame that starts a process. **Only the hunks under that grammar are plain**, never the whole pane, and it happens once per grammar per session. **The common case should not flicker at all.** So the three languages the repository is mostly written in are compiled at launch, from the index. A pane is often opened on a clean tree *before* the agent writes, so a warm over the changed set has nothing to warm. What the repository is mostly made of is the best available guess at what the agent writes next. *Ruled 2026-08-21, session ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)).*

**The count is not drawn when it is zero**, the same way a position is not drawn when there is no diff to stand in. `0 changed` spends columns repeating what the empty state says below in words. **And there is no fourth fact.**

**The right-hand side is never cut.** It is drawn whole or dropped, which is stricter than the marking rule the rest of the header follows. `wat›` is a state nobody can read, and `+1204 -3›` is a number that is not the number. Unlike a path, neither has a part that identifies it.

**The empty state**, which is B3. The tool shows this screen most of the time. It is also the first screen anyone sees when they open the tool beside an agent that has not written anything yet. There, a blank pane and a hang look identical. The screen holds four facts. The header already carries two of them, so the body spends one line, not four.

The line reads `no unstaged changes`. Since B17, it names the other run when that run has work: `no unstaged changes · 3 staged`. That tells a clean tree from a fully staged one. When both runs are drawn and both are empty, it names both: `no staged or unstaged changes`. When a `hide` pattern emptied the run, the line reads `12 hidden` in place of all three, and the other run's count still sits beside it (§11.1). **It does not read `working tree clean`**, because that phrase is git's. Git means index against HEAD as well as tree against index. A worktree with every change staged draws zero files, so that phrase would say the tree is clean while `git status` says the opposite. `no unstaged changes` is exactly what zero files means here, untracked files included, because an untracked file is also unstaged.

**The branch is for orientation. It is not the comparison.** It lives on the header, not on this line ([#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158)). It changes nothing about what is diffed. The pane names it anyway, because otherwise two agents on two worktrees of one repository look identical on screen. §4 defers that multi-worktree case. It does not reject it. A detached HEAD names no branch. The line then drops the branch instead of inventing one, because `HEAD@abc123` would put a commit id in a monitor that shows no commits. The name costs one `.git/HEAD` read, taken only on frames that draw no diff. I4 holds because the thing read is the thing drawn, and the frame that draws it is the cheapest frame there is.

**A token that had to lose characters says so, in the direction it lost them.** `…` on the **left** means the beginning is gone. Only a file path uses it, because the end of a path names the file. `›` on the **right** means the token continues past the edge. The worktree name, a notice, a hunk header, a note and the empty-state line use it. A hunk header cut silently to `@@ -258,7 +25` reads as a different line number, and the mark prevents that.

**A blank row closes every file's block except the last.** The stream has one boundary per changed file and used to draw none of them. The boundary between the two **regions**, one level up, gets a rule of its own. **The blank trails a block instead of leading it.** Every jump here resolves to `Position { file, row: 0 }`, so row zero of a file must be its heading. A leading blank would sit above every heading a jump lands on. **There is no blank after the last file.** That is an exception to the tidy rule, because the rule about resting on the last **screenful** makes the bottom of the diff content. `crates/vigia/tests/scroll.rs::the_bottom_of_the_diff_is_content_rather_than_blank` gates it. **It is a blank row, not a rule**, because B11 ruled that the rule between the regions stays bare. A second rule inside the stream would mean something different on the same screen, and §5.3's furniture law would run it full-bleed. Space is the quietest boundary there is. *Ruled 2026-08-15, session ([#165](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/165)).* **The blank is counted, and that half had no gate.** `view::rows_of` and `view::span_of` are the counting twins that the row-exact scrollbar is scaled against, and the blank belongs to neither. Whether a blank follows a file depends on the file's **position**, and both functions are handed a file. `view::gap_rows` states that fact once. A caller that holds a position asks for a **block** instead of adding the term itself.

**A clipped diff line is marked. It is not a truncated label.** Content does not wrap by default, because a wrapped line moves every line below it and the shape of the screen stops meaning anything. Content cannot be elided either, because unlike a label no part of it is the identifying part. So an unwrapped line is clipped and marked `›`. This document reads I6's *truncated-to-useless labels* as covering labels, not content.

**A line wraps as far as it needs. The only ceiling is the pane.** Pressing `w` asks to see the line, so a mode that shows part of it is the defect this ruling was opened on, one rung smaller. **Wrapping reaches content rows and nothing else.** A file heading, a hunk header, a note and the empty-state line are labels, and they keep their own marks in both modes. *§11.2 B19, ruled 2026-08-26, reader, from the pane ([#272](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/272)).*

**A continuation row blanks its gutter and draws `↳` in its sigil column, in the row's own diff ink.** The blank line number is `bat --style=numbers`' own signal. It is the cheap half, because a real line always has a number. It is not enough alone, because `gutter_width` drops the gutter **entirely** below `MIN_TEXT_WIDTH`'s floor, and that narrow pane is the one wrapping exists for. At forty columns, without the arrow, a continuation with a repeated sigil and no number would read as a line of its own. **The continuation is indented to its own line's indent, capped at half the content width.** This is Neovim's `'breakindent'`. It applies [#164](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/164) one rung down and is not decoration. That ruling made a content row's origin uniform down the block, and an unindented continuation breaks the block shape of the nested code this tool is pointed at. The cap stops a deeply indented line from buying a second row with nothing on it. **The wash and the left bar cover both rows**, because a band that stopped at the first row would make a wrapped removal look as if it ended early.

**`w` wraps and unwraps, and it starts off.** It is a keystroke, not a setting, because B6 keeps this tool configured by gesture. It is off at launch for [#204](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/204)'s reason: every neighbour that wraps gets the whole terminal, and this tool is built for half of it. So the mode with a price is the one the reader asks for. **Wrapping does not change what the scrollbar counts.** B19 rules this: the bar counts the diff's rows, not the terminal's. **The trim at the bottom is a display offset and moves nothing stored.** That keeps the end of a wrapped diff a place a reader can scroll out of. It has one narrow cost. Where a wrapped last screenful spans two files, the first row drawn can belong to the file after the one the position names. So the caret in the list marks the reader's position, not the topmost drawn row.

**A press washes its row. A drag washes every row it crosses, within its own region. Releasing the button sends them.** The wash covers whole rows, because the copy is whole lines. A wash over part of a row would promise a substring that the send does not carry. **The copy is never the drawn row**, which is elided where I6 applies and clipped where a line runs past the pane. The copy is resolved against the row model, not read from the cells. So it carries a clipped line's tail, a wrapped line as one line, a heading's whole path and a hunk header's `@@`. **A note is the reader's own text, not a piece of the line it hangs under**, so its words and the agent's answer are each copied as one line. The frame and the status word around them are drawn, not written, and they send nothing. The copy goes by path, not by index, because a tick rebuilds the list mid-batch. **The payload comes from the frame, not the gesture**: the send carries the wash of the last painted frame. So the release's own coordinates do not extend the span. A write mid-drag does not clear the wash, because the span is re-resolved against every frame. **The wash lasts exactly as long as the gesture**, so nothing is remembered once the button is up. While the button is down, `Esc` cancels the gesture and sends nothing. That is `Esc`'s rung: under the sheet's and over quitting. A span with no text sends nothing, because an empty OSC 52 write clears what the clipboard held. **The copy takes the first route that carries it**: the machine's own clipboard tool, then `tmux load-buffer -w -`, then the escape. The escape goes to the backend, not to a cell, because it draws nothing. tmux is on the list because its `set-clipboard` has defaulted to `external` since tmux 2.6, and that setting discards what an application writes while letting tmux itself write. **The machine's own tool is skipped over `ssh`.** There it would succeed at setting a clipboard the reader cannot see, and that success would end the chain before the escape, which is the route that crosses back. Each route is fed on standard input, so nothing in a copy can be read as an argument. Each route gets one second before the next is tried, because the loop that carries a copy is the loop that paints. The footer says **sent**, not copied, because OSC 52 has no reply. A failed write is reported on the footer and does not end the pane. The footer word clears on a one-shot deadline that `Shell::patience` folds in beside the other three. It sits over a lasting warning and does not replace it. *§11.2 B20; the note's own text ruled 2026-09-08, reader, and its edges the same day, session.*

**A pointer on a content row's gutter turns the number into `✎` in the pointer's ink. A press there opens the note box under the line, and a note draws under its line.** The gutter is the number, the sigil and its gap, and every width draws it. Below the gutter's floor, the sigil and its gap are the target, and the sigil's cell takes the icon. The mark is B10's, with B10's ladder. Only a line row carries it. A heading, a hunk header, a note row and a row of the box are not targets. A continuation marks the line it continues. A press on the content columns still begins a selection, and the two gestures share no cell. **A drag from a gutter makes a note over a range.** The press washes its row in the note's line ink, and the drag washes every row it crosses inside the hunk the press landed in. When the button comes up, the box opens under the last row of the span. Its top edge reads `note · path:12-15`, or `path:-5 +5-6` where the span crosses sides, spelled as a hunk header spells them (reader). A press and release on one row is a range of one. `Esc` while the button is down cancels and opens nothing. Every line of an open note's range draws its number in the note's line ink, and the note hangs under the last line. *Ruled 2026-09-28, reader ([#540](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/540)); `Esc`'s cancel, session.*

**The box is the pane's one mode, bounded as B21 rules.** It opens at the content origin. Its top edge reads `note · path:line` and its bottom edge reads `Enter sends · Esc cancels`. The frame and both labels are in the note's frame ink. The text is in the chrome's own ink, and the caret is its cell reversed, so nothing in the box reads as a line of the diff. Its rows are display rows that the bar does not count. They wrap at the box's inner width by the note rows' own rule. The box shows up to four body rows and scrolls so the caret's row is always drawn. The anchor gives up its word first, then its head, the way a heading's path does. While the box is open, every key belongs to it. Enter writes one file and closes the box. Esc closes it and writes nothing. A blank box writes nothing. `Alt-Enter` is a newline, and the note rows break on it. A press outside the box closes it and does nothing else. A press inside it does nothing. The wheel and a resize pass through. Follow holds the viewport still under the box, so the agent's next write cannot carry the box off screen while the keys still belong to it.

**A press on a line that carries an open note reopens that note in the box**, and the note's rows stand aside. Enter rewrites the note as *open* and keeps its id and the agent's reply. Enter on an emptied box withdraws the note. So a line holds one open note hanging under it. If the agent resolved the note meanwhile, the resolve stands and the new text is written as a new note. The line under the box keeps the note's ink. The box arrives over the 550ms a receipt takes, because it answers a gesture and is watched while it runs. It evolves in through shade blocks out of its middle. Esc sweeps it away left to right over that same length, and the rows drop on the frame after. Enter closes it at once, and the note's rows arrive the way the agent's line does. A store with no home is reported at the press, and the press opens nothing. A write the store refuses is one footer alert, and the box stays with its text.

**A note is placed against the whole diff of the file the walk draws**, because a line one row under the fold is not gone, and the diff is already in hand. The box is placed by the same rule, so it follows a line that moves. A path that both runs hold takes one placement: in the run where its line resolves best, and in the unstaged run on a tie. The box goes with it.

**The note's rows are display rows that the bar does not count.** They are made beside a line's continuations and counted in the same unit, so the bottom clamp sees them. The reader's words are enclosed at the content origin. The frame is in the note's state ink. The body is in the chrome's own ink, wrapped at the last blank that fits inside the frame. The status word sits on the bottom edge, set in from that corner. The edge carries nothing else, so it looks the same whether or not the note is answered. The answer follows one `↳` in the enclosure's own left rule. Its text is in the body's column and the reply's ink, and it is otherwise unmarked except where it quotes code.

**What the answer quotes is drawn as code.** A fence is three or more backticks on a line of their own, closed by at least as many, so a longer fence holds a shorter one whole. The block drops the fences, because they are markup, not something the agent said. Its lines break at the column, not at the last blank, and each continuation is set in by the line's own indent. So a statement keeps its shape, where a sentence would be cut at a word. Its runs take the diff's own syntax classes. They use the grammar the fence names, where the dump holds one, and the anchored file's grammar where the fence names nothing. They are plain where neither applies, or where nothing has compiled that grammar yet, because a block is parsed on the frame the agent writes it and never because a frame drew it. A fence that names a language the dump lacks stays plain and does not borrow the file's grammar, because the agent just said the block is not in that language. A backticked run inside a sentence drops its backticks and takes the plain class alone, because a backticked word is as often a path or a flag as code. An indented block is words, because a continued list item has the same shape. B20 sends what was written, so the copy keeps every fence and backtick the pane did not draw. An answer always draws a row, because a note the agent answered has to show it. Without that row, a note whose fence held nothing would vanish from the pane. Below the width the enclosure's edge needs, the next rung is a `▎` at the content origin. The `▎` and the word are in the state's ink, and the body is in the chrome's dim. The word sits at the right edge, on its own row when the body leaves it no room.

**A pointer on a note's left side turns the cell under it into `✕` in the pointer's ink, and a press there takes the note back.** The left side is the one column at the content origin, on every row the note draws: the enclosure's corners and its left rule, the `▎` of the rung below it, and the `↳` the answer hangs from. The press does what an emptied box does, by a shorter road. So it reads the note back first, and it leaves alone a note the agent resolved between the frame and the press. It departs the way a withdrawal does. It begins no selection, so it shares no cell with B20.

**A note stays with its line.** A line that moved keeps its note under its new number, and the store is not rewritten. A line edited under its note draws the note dim with *changed*. A line gone from the diff draws its note under the heading with *gone*. A file out of the diff leaves its note adrift: drawn nowhere, and counted in the footer beside the position as `2 notes · 1 adrift`. The note is back under its line when the file returns. The count is the first fact a narrowing footer drops, and it never buys the footer a second line. A note with a body draws its line number in the note's line ink, bold. Bold holds where a palette has no colour, and it keeps a persisting mark brighter than a pointer resting on it. A note with no body draws the icon the same way, because nothing else marks the line. `c` hides and shows the rows, and the marks stay. The pane watches its own store directory, so a write there by another process is a wake and the next frame draws it. With nothing on disk to watch, the reader's first note arms the watch.

**How notes change on screen.** A word the agent moved crossfades in from the announcement's ink over 750ms. A line it wrote without closing the note evolves in over the same length, the way a note's own rows arrive. A resolve is a departure a minute long. The rows become the agent's line, arriving the same way. The line holds with no motion over it. Then the rows are swept away over 750ms, and the frame after drops them and closes up the diff. A resolved note found at startup departs the same way. Its file is removed on the frame that drops the rows. A refused removal is one alert, and the note stays off. A withdrawal is swept alone. So is a note whose file vanished with no press here. A resolve on a note drawn nowhere departs unseen, and the count follows. Every effect is armed by a wake or a press and retired at its own length, whether it is drawn or not. A store with no home, a write it refuses, a file it cannot read and a listing it cannot make are each one footer alert. The pane shows the alert when it changes, and the pane carries on. *§11.2 B21; where it was silent, the icon, the bold and the wrap are ruled 2026-09-05, the departure's shape 2026-09-06, the run a path in both runs takes 2026-09-07, its hold 2026-09-08 and the answer's quoted code 2026-09-12, session; the enclosure, the answer's ink and both movements ruled 2026-09-07, the box's length and the answer's column 2026-09-08 and the left side and its press 2026-09-09, reader, whose column, mark and read-back are the session's.*

**`vigia mcp` serves the store to the agent.** It speaks the `initialize` handshake, `2024-11-05` to `2025-11-25`. It echoes the client's revision, or answers with the latest. A `server/discover` probe is answered method-not-found, which tells a client on the newer revision to fall back. `notes` walks the diff on every call and places each note by the pane's rules. Each note comes with the line's current number, text and path, and three lines on each side: from the working tree on the new side and from the diff on the old side. It marks open notes *seen*. It skips resolved notes and leaves their files for the pane to remove once the pane has drawn them. `all` lists them, and it must be a boolean or it is refused. Reading `vigia://notes` gives the default listing. `resolve` and `reply` refuse an empty line, answer *no such note* without an error, and check just before the rename that the note is still there. That is as close as two unlocked processes get to never undoing a withdrawal. `reply` writes its line without closing the note. No home, no worktree and a torn file are each reported in one sentence, and the server stays up. A configuration file that does not parse leaves the server on the unstaged diff, with a line on stderr. B6 has the pane refuse in that case, but a server has no screen to refuse on. Every write to the store reaches the client as `list_changed`. The server creates the reader's state root at start, so the first note ever written is announced. *Ruled 2026-09-05 and 2026-09-08, session, where B21 was silent.*


**The branch is drawn on every frame.** B3 gave the branch to the empty state because nothing else on the pane named it. It answers *which line of work*, and a reader cannot reconstruct that from the body: the list says what changed, the diff says how, and neither says against what. **`branch_for`'s guard is gone.** The guard said *never touch a file the frame does not draw*, and it applied that rule to a fact: only the empty state named a branch. Drawing the branch always makes that fact false. The read is now one the frame **is** going to draw, which the rule permits. `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs` asserts the other direction: every frame reads it. *Ruled 2026-08-17, session ([#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158)).* **A clear column stands between the sigil and the line, and it does not ladder.** This is the fifth deliberate departure from `assets/preview.svg` that this document records. The mockup places the line number end-anchored, the sigil beside it, and every content origin a further cell in. **The column does not degrade, although every other spacing decision here does.** The sigil column **is the diff signal** at any depth, and on any palette that cannot wash the row. §5.1's tinted-row cell states this, and `crates/vigia/tests/colour.rs` gates it. A column that keeps that signal legible is part of the signal, not decoration beside it, so I6's *every cell is contested* does not reach it. **The column is uniform, not conditional on the line.** The gap only earns its column where a line has no leading whitespace of its own. But drawing it only for unindented lines would make a content row's origin depend on its *contents*. That is the intermittent layout this section has already refused twice. **The measured cost** is one column of every content row. It lands hardest at the forty columns I6 is named for, where the reference fixture goes from **one** clipped line to **three**. The eighty-column pane clips nothing either way. The hunk header, the file heading and both regions' chrome are untouched. *Ruled 2026-08-15, session, towards the picture ([#164](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/164)).*

**How a diff line is coloured.** I2b pays for this. Content is syntax highlighted, and the mockup already ruled how: added, removed and context lines all carry the same classes, the sigil is green or red, and the line number stays faint. Only content is highlighted. A file heading, a hunk header and a note are chrome. *Ruled 2026-07-30, session, following the picture literally per §5.1.*

Three constraints follow from this. None of them is a preference.

**The engine emits meanings, not colours.** `vigia-core` maps a syntax scope onto one of nine classes and stops there. The shell decides which colour a class gets. A core that emitted truecolour would settle the palette in the one place §6 says has no terminal in it. It would also pre-empt the 256-colour degradation path before anyone wrote that path.

**At sixteen colours the diff signal degrades to the sigil column *and* the left bar.** In the mockup, the row tint and the left bar of §5.1 separate an added line from a context line. Sixteen foreground-only colours cannot draw the **tint**. They can draw the **bar** ([#218](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/218)), because the bar is a background on a blank margin column with no text over it.

**A file type nothing recognises is not an error.** §6's five steps resolve the syntax. A file they do not resolve draws exactly as it did before highlighting existed. A monitor must not refuse a file because it has no colour for it.

**How recency is drawn.** This is I10, and §5.1 asked for it as two separate things. It is **one ladder with three rungs**, read from one store through one lookup. Each rung is an intensity on the file's own heading:

| Rung | What it means | How it draws |
|---|---|---|
| **pulse** | named by the most recent tick **and** holding ink in the newest samples | brightest |
| **live** | has a sample inside the 120-second window | plain |
| **cold** | nothing in the window, so nothing is tracked for it | faintest |

**The top rung is not a duration.** §5.1 reads the mockup's persisting mark as a decay. A decay measured against a wall clock has to be *seen* decaying, and that once meant a redraw that nothing schedules. The window now ages on a clock, and I1's licence reaches it. So the sample grid carries the ruling, not the invariant: the mark lives until a sample boundary, as the pulse paragraph above states.

**Cold is not a fourth rule.** Under I10, a path with nothing left in the window is dropped from the store entirely, so *cold* and *untracked* are the same state. The **first** frame of a session looks like this too. A worktree that was already dirty draws every row cold, because a monitor cannot know what happened before it started watching. A pane that invented a recency for those rows would light up rows nothing has touched. **Three rungs is a property of the store, not of the palette.** §5.1 and `theme.rs` both promised that the *real* gradient would arrive with [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)'s truecolour. Their reason was that sixteen foreground-only colours can only spend bold, plain and dim. The arithmetic is right, but the conclusion does not follow. #11 widens how many distinct colours a rung can be drawn *in*. `Recency` decides how many rungs there **are**. It has three variants, because the store can answer exactly three questions about a path: is it in the newest tick, is it anywhere in the window, or is it not tracked at all. A fourth rung would have to mean *how far through the window*. Nothing computes that today, and the table above makes it expensive to add honestly. The window is real time, the sampling is event-driven, and a fraction of the window read on a quiet tree ages without being redrawn. So #11 replaced bold, plain and dim with three chosen luminances, and stopped there. Widening the ladder is an I10 change with a budget attached, not a theme change.

This file has now recorded the same pattern twice: **a limit blamed on the rendering layer was a limit of the data behind it.** §5.2 caught the mirror image: a property that looked expensive turned out to be a by-product already computed. Before blaming the thing that draws for a limitation, check what the thing that supplies it can actually distinguish.

**The sparkline never draws a partial bucket or a squeezed strip.** Until 2026-08-20 it was treated as a thing made of items, so it **broke**: it dropped whole buckets, oldest first. **It is now a projection** ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)). A narrower rung sums adjacent source buckets and draws the whole window at a lower resolution. This is the heat strip clause below, applied to a second element. It is not a new rule.

**Why the old ruling was reversed.** A sparkline's items are slices of one **window**, in order, and the set of them is the claim. A strip that shows the newest half of a window, beside a band that draws all of it, is the same misdescription the heat strip clause refuses on the other axis. The claim was measured before it was believed, over forty seeded series in three work patterns. Dropping the oldest half costs **shape as well as span**: on a steady worktree it takes a twelve-bucket strip from 5.75 distinct drawn heights to 5.25. The number of drawn columns carries the shape, not the period each column covers. So the count became a rung, and every rung covers the window. The gate is `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_sparkline_reprojects_rather_than_dropping_buckets`. It uses a window written at both ends and quiet between them, the one shape where truncation and re-projection differ.

**The bound left by the retired ceiling is the window, not a period.** #161 ruled twelve a ceiling, because a drawn bucket had to stay coarser than a churn-band column. [#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232) retired that band's fixed period and the bound with it, and [#457](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/457) retired the band. Every rung still reads one store over one window, so what must agree is the **window they cover**. The sparkline covers all of it at every rung, and the same gate holds that.

**The re-projection also changes a denominator, not only a width.** A drawn bucket that sums `n` source buckets has to be measured against what `n` source buckets are worth. That is **not** the finest figure multiplied by `n`. `scale_of` averages the non-empty values, and grouping merges empty buckets into their neighbours. So multiplying is exact on a busy worktree and wrong by up to `n` on a quiet one. On a sparse fixture, the multiplied estimate moved a cell by as much as **four rungs of the eight-level ramp**. So `vigia_core::History` computes one figure per grouping in the walk it already makes, and `vigia_core::SPARK_GROUPS` is the ladder of groupings it computes.

**The heat strip is a fourth kind of thing, and the rule needed a third clause for it.** The rule *a thing made of items breaks, a thing made of characters marks its edge, and content is neither* covers a list, a token and a line. A heat strip is made of items and is **not a list**. Its items are slices of one file, in order, and the set of them *is* the claim. If the last six slices are dropped, the strip draws the first half of a file as if it were the whole file, and a reader concludes that the tail is untouched. Less detail is honest. A prefix presented as the whole is not.

So **a projection re-projects instead of dropping items.** A narrower rung sums adjacent slices and classifies the sums, which is exact at halves: twenty-four slices, then twelve, then six, then none. **The source resolution and the drawn count became two numbers on 2026-08-18** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)). `heat_at` groups `HEAT_BUCKETS / width`, so the source is the ceiling of this ladder. Doubling the source lets a pane wide enough for it draw a finer strip. **Twelve is now a rung, not a constant**, which keeps §5.1's ruling. `assets/preview.svg` says how many slices a pane draws **at the picture's own width**. Its comment records that width as 109 columns, and `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_pictured_width_still_draws_twelve_slices` turns that into a gate. **From here the projection bounds the ladder, not the layout.** Below a source of 48, a file shorter than the strip draws a solid change as dashes, because consecutive lines land on non-adjacent slices. So the 48-slice rung waits on [#230](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/230), not on a width. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs` gates the strip with a file changed at both ends and quiet in the middle, the one shape where truncation and re-projection differ. It reads **cell colours**, not symbols, because every slice draws the same block.

**The two strips scale against different things, on purpose.** A sparkline's height is measured against `scale_of`'s figure over every bucket **on screen**. Down a file list the question is which file is busiest. Scaling each row against its own maximum would draw every file at full height the moment it is the busiest it has ever been. That answers a question nobody asked and destroys the one comparison a file list is for. A heat slice's intensity is measured against the busiest slice **of its own file**. There the question is where in *this* file the work is, and the neighbouring rows have nothing to do with it. One compares down the list. The other compares across a row.

**A sparkline bucket with no writes draws a track, and a file with no churn is the all-empty case.** Drawing no strip at all saves nothing. The reserved-slot ruling earlier in this section takes the slot from the pane whether or not a row can fill it. The row is spent either way, so drawing nothing costs exactly what drawing a track costs. Replaced 2026-08-03 ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)). §5.1 carries the ruling and the reason the track is `_` and not the ramp's floor.

**The heat strip's empty case is a different case, and its opposite ruling stands.** `heat_at` draws nothing for a file with no line diff at all. A strip of pure track there would say *"this file is in the diff and I cannot tell you where"*. That is an **epistemic gap**: the row asserts the file changed, and the strip cannot place the change. A sparkline's empty state is a **fact** the store actually holds: nothing has been written since the watch opened. So the two elements draw opposite conclusions from input that looks the same, and both are honest. This distinction is why #78 is not an argument for changing the strip too.

**No glance element may take a heading below twelve columns of path.** The counters, the pulse, the strip and the sparkline are all placed from the right, against a floor the path keeps. The layout table decides which of them survives at a given width, in the order recorded above. It is not a per-row allocation. A row reduced to `M …` would stop naming its own file. That is the truncated-to-useless shape I6 forbids, reached by decoration instead of by narrowing.

**CLI.** The command takes one optional positional path, which defaults to the working directory, or the bare word `mcp`. That word starts §11.2 B21's server, not a pane. `./mcp` is still a directory to watch. The surface has **one flag, `--version` (and `-V`), which prints `vigia <version>` and exits successfully**.
