Every other argument that begins with `-` is refused with one line that says so. It is never taken as a path. So `vigia --colour=never` is told what the surface is, not that `--colour=never` is not a repository. **A second argument is refused after a path. After `mcp`, the second argument is one of two words**, `register` or `pending`. A hook runs those two, not a reader. Anything else is refused with one line naming both words, because a word dropped in silence leaves a hook that looks installed and a socket that nothing posts to. Arity is part of the surface, so the classifier takes the whole argument list, not one argument. `--help` stays B6's open question. The version is `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, so the string cannot drift from the manifest the release was cut from. The classification lives in the library, not in `main.rs`, because §7 makes the test suite the proof and a test cannot import a `main.rs`.

**Configuration is two files and one environment variable.** This is B6, ruled 2026-07-31, amended the same day, and amended again 2026-08-25 by [#306](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/306). The CLI gains nothing from either amendment. The files are a **palette** and a set of **view defaults**. They are separate for a mechanical reason, stated below.

**A palette is a preference, so it lives in a file.** `~/.config/vigia/theme` is read when nothing overrides it. The path is resolved from `HOME` or `USERPROFILE`. That is **one** rule for every platform: no XDG matrix, no `%APPDATA%` special case, no discovery crate. `VIGIA_THEME` still names a built-in or points at a file, and it still wins. A variable is how you say "not this time" without editing anything.

**A colour depth is not a preference. It is a property of the terminal you are in right now.** On one machine, for one user, in one afternoon, this pane is truecolour, that one is an `ssh` into something ancient, and the third is CI. A file gives all three the same answer and is wrong for two of them. So `VIGIA_COLOR` stays a variable, for the same reason `NO_COLOR` is one.

**A font is the same kind of fact as a colour depth, so `VIGIA_GLYPHS` is a second variable, not a second setting.** Added 2026-08-17 ([#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159)). It takes `block`, `braille`, `octant` or `auto`. It overrides a detection that reads the terminal's identity, the same way `VIGIA_COLOR` does. **It does not change B6's count, for B6's own reason.** B6 counts one environment variable of *configuration*, `VIGIA_THEME`. A variable that says what the terminal in front of you can draw configures nothing. The test is the one in the paragraph above, and a font passes it as a depth does: a file would give three panes on one machine the same answer and be wrong for two of them. A value that is not one of the four is **refused, not ignored**. This follows `VIGIA_COLOR`'s rule: a reader who set a variable and saw no effect cannot guess that it was discarded.

The amendment costs less than B6 assumed, because **the format and the parser already existed**. `VIGIA_THEME` has always been able to point at a theme file. The amendment adds a place to look, not a surface. It also removes a step: a preference set once now survives a new shell.

**The view toggles are a preference too, so they live in a file as well: `~/.config/vigia/config`.** Added 2026-08-25 ([#306](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/306)). B6 says why it is its own file. Ten keys exist: `follow`, `rail`, `single`, `overview`, `staged`, `wrap`, `notes`, `icons`, `links` and `persist`. Each takes `on` or `off`. The file uses the theme file's grammar: one key per line and `#` comments. The parser strips a byte order mark. An unknown key is **refused**, not dropped. Every error names its 1-based line. A missing file is not an error. An unreadable one is. The pane reads the file where it reads the palette, before it takes the terminal, so a file that does not parse is reported on a screen the reader can still see.


**`hide` keeps paths out of the pane entirely.** Added 2026-09-10 ([#495](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/495)), asked for from use. It takes one regular expression, applied at the status walk, not at render. So a hidden path is absent from the list, the diff, the counts and the churn history, and it never spends one of I10's 256 tracked paths. **The header says what is hidden**: `N hidden` sits beside the count whenever anything is hidden, as `N binary` does. A monitor that hides work without saying so is lying. *Ruled 2026-09-10, reader.* **A hidden path does not wake the watch either.** The watcher tests the pattern before its stat, so a file in a generated directory written all day costs a match and no walk. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session* ([#501](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/501)).

**The session settled what #495 left open.** The pattern is **searched** against each changed path, not anchored to it, so `hide = ^target/|\.lock$` works as written. `#` still opens a comment, so a pattern that needs one writes `[#]`. The count sits between the binary count and the staged count, so a narrowing header drops it second. It is the one fact on that row that the reader configured. **B3's empty state shows it too**, because the header drops the count before the body drops anything. The body reads `12 hidden` there, and the clean tree's own line is unchanged. **Both rows draw one figure, and it also covers the run the pane is not drawing.** A worktree whose only change is staged and hidden empties a walk that never saw the change. Without that run's figure, the pane would read as a clean tree. **`vigia mcp` does not get the pattern at all.** The server reports what the pane draws, and a server has no pane. *Ruled 2026-09-10, session.*

**No variable joins them**, so B6's count of one is unchanged. `r` and `s` already say *not this time* in one press each.

**`rail on` below 139 columns sets the request and draws nothing.** This is §11.2 B14 unchanged: a reader who configured a rail and opened a narrow pane gets one as soon as the pane widens.

**The monitor writes nothing of its own.** It writes nothing into the worktree and nothing into `.git`. Into the state directory it writes only what §11.2 B21 opens: what the reader asked for, on the reader's gesture, and nothing else ever. The gate below snapshots that directory too. It still asserts zero writes across the same drive, and `crates/vigia/tests/writes.rs::one_gesture_writes_exactly_one_file` is its sibling. **Into the configuration file it writes only what §11.2 B22 opens**: that the reader turned remembering on, a toggle they pressed, and nothing else ever. Both are gestures, not housekeeping, and that is the whole licence. **What B7 rests on is untouched.** §6's settle margin and §10's bullet argue from a monitor never writing *into the worktree*, and neither file is in one.

B7 was ruled 2026-08-05. It is stated here because two rulings depended on it before it was written down. §6 makes the settle margin a table lookup, not a measurement, because measuring would need a probe write. §10's settle-margin bullet rests the whole 2-second constant on *"a monitor never writes, so it only ever sees the gaps its user's tools happened to leave"*. Both arguments held only because `vigia` happened to open no file for writing, and nobody had decided that. A cache, a lock file or a crash dump added in good faith would have made two recorded rulings wrong, and nothing would have said so.

**The rule is about the filesystem. It deliberately does not cover what the process emits.** Bytes sent to the terminal this program draws into are a different surface with different costs. Reading this rule as covering them would decide questions it has no business deciding. [#120](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/120) asks about OSC 8 links on paths and a yank key over OSC 52. Both are pure emission, and this rule does not settle them. The two surfaces meet in one place: neither may become a *reason* to write. A clipboard fallback that needs a temp file, or a link that caches a resolved path to disk, falls under this rule again. **Both were ruled 2026-08-15 and both were declined, as §11.2 B8.** Neither decline rests on this rule, as this paragraph requires.

**How a writing monitor breaks, for anyone revisiting B7.** A log inside the worktree is not just untidy. It is a **self-sustaining wake loop**: the write is a filesystem event, the event is a tick, the tick is a frame, and the frame writes again, at frame rate, for as long as the process lives. `vigia-core`'s event filter decides how visible that is. Of the four places a write can land, it hides three. A path *outside* the worktree fails `strip_prefix` and is dropped. A path inside it and **gitignored** is dropped by the exclude rules. A path inside `.git` is dropped unless its file name is `index`, the one file there that changes a diff. A path inside the worktree and **not** ignored is accepted. So a writing monitor would need a guard, and the guard is not a convention about where the file goes. It is the worktree root, resolved before the file is opened, and a refusal. The refusal is a behaviour, and it is one of the five B7 counts.

**Instruments go in the harness instead.** Declining one here is not the same as having none. The harness is where every other measurement of this product already lives. The soak is `vigia::run` with the terminal removed (§7). `VIGIA_BUDGET_SLACK` is the precedent for a variable that the tests read and the binary does not, so a measurement switch costs the product surface nothing. The reader gets the footer's own readouts, which are §5.1's business.

`crates/vigia/tests/writes.rs` gates this. It covers every entry under a fixture root, with `.git` included and the root itself stamped. It runs across a first paint, six reader actions, a second tick and the grammar warmer. The warmer is **joined**, not detached, because a write that lands after the comparison would pass the gate by racing it. One point was checked, not assumed: `gix`'s status walk is never asked to write the index (`write_index` is called nowhere in `crates/`), and the gate confirms this empirically. So a monitor that reads a working tree many times a second leaves it byte for byte as it found it. Both directions are tested, per §7: a sibling gate writes a file and asserts that the comparison **reports** it, because a comparator that cannot say "something moved" is untested.

### How colour degrades

`SPEC.md` §5 makes shape and colour the whole differentiator. A palette that assumes 24-bit colour silently stops working on terminals that lack it. §10 named that problem and left it open. The answer here is **two axes, not a fallback chain**.

**The palette decides what can be drawn. The depth decides how finely it can be expressed.** An element appears only when both allow it, and the two can disagree. `ansi` refuses a row wash at *every* depth, because a wash must assume a background and `ansi` assumes none. `dark` draws a wash wherever the depth can express it.

Four palettes ship. **Since [#325](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/325), the default comes from the terminal's own answer** (§11.2 B18). At startup, before the event reader exists, the shell sends an OSC 11 query and waits at most 150ms. If the background answers, the shell picks `dark` or `light`. **`ansi` is the fallback**, unchanged from before there was a choice. Every colour in it is one of the sixteen *names*, so it resolves to the terminal's own scheme, and `vigia` matches the pane beside it instead of clashing with it. It is the only palette that is correct on a background nothing has detected. Since #325 that means a terminal that gave no answer: over ssh, under a multiplexer that swallows the query, or on Windows, where no query is sent. `dark` is `assets/preview.svg` read out of the file, not approximated. `light` is the same design re-picked at light-background luminance, with every ramp reversed, because on white the dark end has the contrast. `system` is `ansi` plus the diff washes. The washes are mixed from the terminal's own background toward its own green and red, which the shell queries only when `system` is named ([#333](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/333)). With no answer, `system` is `ansi`.

The depth ladder, and what each rung loses:

| Rung | Foreground | Background |
|---|---|---|
| truecolour | 24-bit, as authored | as authored |
| 256 | quantised to the xterm palette | **dropped** |
| 16 | nearest of the sixteen names | dropped |
| none | dropped | dropped |

**No track draws in the pane's own colour at any depth.** At sixteen colours `dark` draws its tracks `DarkGray` and `light` draws them `Gray`. *Ruled 2026-09-30, session* ([#98](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/98)).

**A background needs 24-bit and a foreground does not.** This asymmetry is the one point here worth arguing. §5.1 records that a quantised background is a solid block, not a tint. The darkest available green behind a line of code is a slab, and a slab destroys the syntax colours on top of it. That is worse than no tint. So the row wash needs the top rung, and text colour goes all the way down to sixteen. Below the top rung, the diff signal narrows back to the sigil column, as §11.1 recorded before #11.

**That boundary sat at 256 for a phase. A real screen moved it.** The cube has six levels per axis, and its darkest two are 0 and 95, so a *subtle* colour has nowhere to land. `#1b3d29` quantises to `#005f00` and `#45222a` to `#5f0000`: saturated primaries at roughly two and a half times the authored luminance. Over one hunk this reads as a strong tint. Over a **newly added file, where every row is an addition**, it is a screenful of flat green, and §5's claim is that colour carries signal.

**The grey ramp is not the escape.** It is much nearer in distance than the cube. The quantiser would send a desaturated wash there if its chroma gate did not stop it. But `#1b3d29` and `#45222a` average to the *same* grey. An added row and a removed row would then be one colour, which §5 forbids at any rung that has colour at all. There is no honest wash below 24-bit. A reader whose terminal draws more than detection can prove sets `VIGIA_COLOR`. Inside `tmux`, the multiplexer must also pass 24-bit through instead of approximating it.

Detection is a precedence chain, and the first answer wins: `VIGIA_COLOR`, then `NO_COLOR`, then `TERM=dumb`, then `COLORTERM` claiming 24-bit, then **`TERM_PROGRAM`**, then **`WT_SESSION`**, which Windows Terminal exports into WSL as well and which is not read inside tmux or screen, then `TERM` promising 24-bit, then `TERM` containing `256color`, then **Windows**, then sixteen.

**A terminal that names itself ranks above `TERM`. That order was learned, not designed.** Git Bash and MSYS export `TERM=xterm-256color`, so a chain that read `TERM` first sent the most common shell for this repo to 256. At 256 a subtle colour has nowhere to land. The xterm cube's darkest axis levels are 0 and 95 with nothing between, so a row wash of `#1b3d29` quantises to `#005f00`, a saturated primary instead of a tint. A terminal that names itself is better evidence than a variable that describes a terminfo entry.

**That ruling was written for `WT_SESSION`, and `TERM_PROGRAM` applies it more widely.** On a Unix, a chain with two positive signals lost both of them in the setup §1 describes as the point of the tool. `COLORTERM` is the only convention for claiming 24-bit, and **nothing propagates it**. `ssh` forwards only `TERM`, and a multiplexer replaces `TERM` with its own entry. `tmux` at its default `default-terminal screen` is the ordinary case, and `screen` contains neither `256color` nor anything else the chain read. So an agent in one pane with `vigia` in the pane beside it landed on sixteen. Sixteen drops backgrounds by the rule above, so `dark` coloured every foreground and washed no row. From outside, that looks like the palette being ignored, not like a wrong rung. At sixteen, `#58a6ff` resolves to `LightCyan`.

`TERM_PROGRAM` survives all of this, because it names the process that owns the screen, not the entry the innermost layer chose to advertise. **`Apple_Terminal` is the one entry in that table that is not truecolour**, which is why the table answers with a rung and not a yes. Terminal.app does not draw 24-bit and never did. It rounds the sequences to its own palette. Claiming truecolour there is the over-claim this ladder's floor exists to avoid. An under-claim only looks flatter than it had to.

**`TERM` recognises two more promises than it did, both in terminfo's own vocabulary.** The `-direct` suffix is the database's name for direct colour. A few terminals ship an entry named after themselves (`xterm-kitty`, `xterm-ghostty`, `alacritty`, `wezterm`, `foot`, `contour`, `rio`). None of them contains `256color`, and none ever had a 16-colour era. A terminal that only *usually* has truecolour is not on that list. It belongs behind `COLORTERM` or `VIGIA_COLOR`, because the list records terminals someone checked, not a claim about the ones nobody has.

**Windows detects truecolour. 256 was not a safe default, only a different wrong one.** That default protected against consoles older than Windows 10 1703, which stopped being a supported target years ago. A reader on something older sets `VIGIA_COLOR`. The override exists for that case.

**`TERM` only ever promotes.** On a Unix it is the last signal, not the whole answer. On Windows it describes a world where sixteen was the floor, so reading it as a demotion would remove colour from consoles that have it. An entry that promises neither rung changes nothing and leaves the floor where it is. The floor is sixteen because that answer is never actively wrong.

**Modifiers survive every rung, including none.** `NO_COLOR` asks for no *colour*, and bold is not colour. On a monochrome terminal, bold is the only distinction left.

**Quantising happens once, at startup.** The palette is walked once and the result is stored. The frame path draws with values already fitted to this terminal, so I9 never sees the cost.

**A wider palette does not buy a wider ramp.** Mapping 24-bit colours onto sixteen entries cannot keep nine syntax classes distinct: `#ffa657` and `#e3b341` are both yellow, which is arithmetic, not a choice. `ansi` is the answer for a sixteen-colour terminal, and it hand-picks all nine. The diff signal must **never** collapse at any rung that has colour at all, because §5 spends it on colour. That rule has its own gate.

**A theme that does not parse is reported before the screen is taken.** B5 states this rule for a path that is not a repository, and it holds here for the same reason: an error painted inside a TUI that then hands the terminal back is an error nobody sees. An unknown key is **refused, not ignored**, and the error names the line it was on. A silently dropped key makes a theme that does nothing, and nothing on the screen tells the reader the key was discarded.

**A path that is not a repository exits before the screen is taken.** This is the first half of B5. `Worktree::discover` and the opening walk both run before `Session::enter`, so the failure reaches a terminal that can still be read. An error painted inside a TUI that then hands the terminal back is an error nobody sees.


**The first signal from outside exits the way the quit key does: the terminal is restored, nothing is printed, and the exit status is 0.** Nothing failed, so there is nothing to report. A message printed after the terminal is restored would land on a shell prompt the sender was not watching. **The first ask deliberately does not re-raise.** A parent's `wait` reports *exited 0*, not *killed by SIGTERM*, because the program was asked to stop and it stopped. If a script ever needs to tell "quit" from "killed" on the first ask, this is the line to change. This is a choice about exiting cleanly, not a limit of a dependency. `signal-hook`'s `low_level::emulate_default_handler` would re-raise if used here. It is in a crate §6 already names, and using it would not change the unique-crate count on any of the three tier-1 targets.


**The second signal takes the default disposition: it kills the process and restores nothing.** This half keeps the first half safe. Arming a handler removes a guarantee the sender had before. An unarmed `vigia` died on one `kill`. An armed one turns that `kill` into a wake for its loop, and a loop that is wedged for any reason swallows every later `kill`. That process could then be stopped only by `SIGKILL`, and `SIGKILL` leaves exactly the broken terminal this invariant exists to prevent. So the graceful path needs a floor under it. The consequence is stated openly: **on the second ask the terminal is not restored**, `wait` reports killed by that signal, and the I8 row lists it as a by-choice exclusion. *Ruled 2026-08-08, session ([#24](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/24)).*
### 11.2 Undecided


A question lands here with a recommendation marked **(proposed)**. It is not settled until ruled on. A recommendation must not contradict §3. If one does, §3 wins and the recommendation is wrong.


**A ruled item keeps its number here and moves its behaviour to §11.1.** Code in `crates/**` cites these numbers, so retiring one would silently repoint those citations. A question ruled **no** has no behaviour to describe. For it, §11.1 carries a pointer and the reasoning stays here. B10 needs both halves at once, because it was ruled out and then in. A reversal owes the reader the reasoning it overturned, not only the answer it reached.


**B1 — What happens to follow mode when the reader scrolls.** *Ruled 2026-07-30, session: the proposal stands. See §11.1.* Follow uses `less +F` semantics: on at startup, off on any manual scroll, back on with `f`. Disengaging on scroll is the only rule that never fights a reader mid-read. A dedicated key is better than overloading `G`/`End`, because "jump to the last file" and "resume following" are different intents.


**B2 — Which file wins when several change at once.** *Ruled 2026-07-30, session: the proposal stands. See §11.1.* Follow goes to the file whose write landed last in the settled batch, and §5's pulse marks the others. This reads "newest" literally, and it is stable, not heuristic. It is affordable only because the filesystem event names the path. Deriving it would mean `stat`-ing every changed file, which is [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19)'s breach.


**B3 — The empty state.** *Ruled 2026-07-31, session: the proposal stands, with its wording corrected. See §11.1.* The tool shows zero changes most of the time. It is the first screen anyone sees beside an agent that has not written yet, and there a blank pane looks the same as a hang. The correction is to the wording. The proposal said "no changes" and the shell said `working tree clean`. Both are looser than the diff underneath, which compares the working tree against the **index**.


**B4 — Is the file list navigable, and is it a region?** *Ruled 2026-08-01, session ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66)): the list is a region, and it is not navigable. See §11.1.* Both halves are ruled, because the question as written asked only one. *Navigable* depends on *is it a region at all*, and the proposal's "one continuous scroll" answered the second without asking it.


**Not navigable** stands as proposed: no selection, no focus, no second mode. Selection implies focus, focus implies a mode, and modes are reviewer-class (§2). **A region** is ruled the other way, towards the picture. That choice has a reason. Four of §5's five glance elements ride a file heading. In a single stream they are visible only at scroll position zero, so the glance surface would disappear exactly while the pane is being read. `assets/preview.svg` has drawn two regions since before the question existed. The picture got a smaller thing wrong, and it was corrected there: an automatic, unbounded changed set cannot have a summary block for *every* file. So the region caps its height, scrolls, and shows that with a bar. The cell-for-cell gaps that remain are recorded in §5.1.


**B5 — Not a git repository, and submodules.** The two halves are separate because only one of them is decided.


**Not a repository: ruled 2026-07-31, session, and it had already shipped. See §11.1.** [#40](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/40) found it while B3 was being ruled. This is the drift that §11's warning box describes. `take-next`'s pre-flight cannot see it, because it compares invariant tokens, not behaviour.


**Submodules: still open.** *(proposed)* Out of v1. A submodule is shown as an opaque directory, and the pane says so. Recursing into submodules would cost the incremental guarantees of I2a.


**B6 — CLI surface and configuration.** *Ruled 2026-07-31, session, together with theming, which it had said it was waiting for; amended six times since, most recently 2026-09-11 by B22. See §11.1.* The CLI stays at one positional path. It gains no flags **that configure it**. Configuration is **two files and one environment variable**: a palette, a set of view defaults, and `VIGIA_THEME`.


The count covers *configuration* only, and the amendments depend on that. `VIGIA_COLOR` and `VIGIA_GLYPHS` are not counted. A palette is a preference about you, so it belongs in a file. A colour depth or a font's glyph coverage is a property of the terminal you are in right now, and it really does differ between two panes on one machine. `--version` and `-V` are not counted either. They print one line and exit before a terminal is taken, so they cannot change a frame or leave any state. The test is not whether an argument begins with `-`. The test is whether the process behaves differently *after* it starts drawing.


**`--help` is untouched and stays open.** Help text describes a surface, so it grows with the surface and must be kept true. A version string is generated from the manifest and cannot drift. Any other argument that begins with `-` is still refused with one line. `crates/vigia/tests/cli.rs::an_option_that_is_not_a_version_query_is_still_refused` holds this.


**The view defaults are the second file, `~/.config/vigia/config`** ([#306](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/306), 2026-08-25). So there are two files, and still one variable. A reader asked for it from the pane after pressing `r` or `s` every time the pane opened.

**Ten keys, and which gestures are excluded on purpose is a rule rather than a list.** Five keys are the gestures sheet's `view` section with follow taken out: `rail`, `single`, `overview`, `staged` and `wrap`. Because the five are derived from the sheet, `staged` landed with B17, not after it, and `overview` landed with its own state. The sixth is B21's `notes`, which `c` reaches from the sheet's `notes` section. B18's `icons` and `links` reach no key at all. **It answers nothing [#50](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/50) asks.** That row decides which way the toggle *ships*. The shipped default is unchanged and off. The file only lets a reader who has already decided stop saying so every session. The keys have one thing in common: each is pure preference, and every combination is a legitimate pane. **The rule is B22's**: a setting is remembered when it is a boolean about how the pane behaves. Where the pane is looking is not a setting. So `b` is out because it is a state, not for the reason it used to give. **Follow is in.** Its I5 exclusion was overruled 2026-09-11 by the reader, and `REVOCATIONS.md` keeps what it said. *Ruled 2026-09-09, reader, amended 2026-09-10, session, and again 2026-09-11 by B22.*

**`hide` is the file's one valued setting** ([#495](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/495), 2026-09-10), and neither count moves for it. A gesture is for what changes while you watch. A file is for what was true before you opened the pane. B22 keeps `hide` off the menu for the same reason. §11.1 says what it does.


**The branch not taken: put the keys in the theme file and add no second path.** This is the smaller change. It is recorded because it will be raised again. It is refused because of a defect, not taste. `VIGIA_THEME` is resolved before the theme file is opened, and a built-in name wins outright. So `VIGIA_THEME=dark` never reads that file. Under that design, a reader who names a palette for one session would have the file's view keys discarded in silence. The two files share one format, one discovery rule and one error path. They do not share a subject.

**Amended 2026-09-05 by B21: the surface gains its second word, `vigia mcp`, and the count above does not move.** A server takes no terminal and has no frame to change, which is the test this ruling sets for `--version`. `mcp`'s own words (`register`, `pending`) belong to the server, not the pane.


**B7 — Does the monitor write an observation log?** *Ruled 2026-08-05, session: no. See §11.1.* ([#117](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/117).) The proposal was `VIGIA_OBSERVE=<path>`, off by default. It appended per-tick aggregates to a file outside the worktree, so that the week of real use [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) needs would leave numbers instead of memories. It is B6's pressure, arriving with a candidate behind it.


**The case for adopting it is not weak, and it is recorded because it will be raised again.** Every deferred cost on the shelf is a function of the changed-file count over time. Four shelf entries are ranked today on judgement, not on numbers. This document's own method says a claim without a measurement is a wish, and that argues for instruments. The nearest precedent went the other way: refusing the footer's memory readout on a similar economy argument was overruled in [#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41), correctly.


**It was declined on four points that were checked, not argued.**


1. **It does not answer the question the week exists for.** Of #72's five items, *"any disagreement with `git diff`"* needs an oracle, and *"what was reached for and not found"* needs a person. [#50](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/50) asks the second, and #50 is the row #72 exists to unblock. An aggregate of frame times and file counts reaches neither.

2. **The cheap half is not `vigia`'s to measure.** How many files are dirty at once is a property of the worktree, so `git status --porcelain` counted on a timer already measures it. I1 constrains this program, not a shell loop beside it. The highest-value number needs no product code, and that decided it.

3. **It is neither of B6's two kinds of setting.** Widening the surface is the one answer to B6's pressure that B6's rationale rules out.

4. **The cost is more than one variable.** A write target needs a state directory *created*, where the theme file only needs one *read*. It needs a policy for an unwritable path or a full disk, because a monitor that dies of its own logging is worse than one with no log. It needs a growth policy over the days I3 makes a claim about: §10's hour ran 73,265 frames, so a 64-byte line is 4.47 MiB an hour and 107 MiB across I3's 24-hour window, which needs rotation or a cap. It needs a flush inside I9's budget. It needs to refuse any path under the worktree, for the wake-loop reason §11.1 gives.


**What declining costs, and what reopens it.** The changed-file histogram drops from frame resolution to the sampler's one second. That is adequate only because the question is how many files are dirty at once, which is a state that persists, and not how spiky arrivals are. *"Was it ever over 16 ms"* stays unanswerable, because `Samples` is a 128-frame ring and a breach rolls off it. Follow-transition counts are visible to nothing else, and nobody has asked for them. Daily use asking for the first of these reopens it.


**A footer readout of the maximum frame time ever seen was refused on a recorded principle.** It is the principle that [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s deferred total and the pulse's elapsed-time decay failed on. A number that only rises says *"something was slow once, hours ago"* forever, which is a stale claim that looks live. The pulse escaped by being redefined against event identity. A maximum has no such escape.


**B8 and the clipboard write arrived as one question, and they are two.** ([#120](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/120), ruled 2026-08-15, session.) That issue offered one property as the gate for both: *what a terminal that supports neither sees is nothing different*. That is true of OSC 8 and false of OSC 52. A terminal that ignores OSC 8 draws the same cells. A terminal that ignores OSC 52 leaves the old contents in the clipboard, and nothing can say so. **Two affordances whose failures point in opposite directions do not share a ruling.** Both were declined, and neither decline rested on B7's writes-nothing rule. Both were later reopened separately, on unrelated evidence.


**B8 — OSC 8 hyperlinks on drawn paths.** *Ruled 2026-08-15, session: no. Overruled 2026-08-25, reader, inside B18's vocabulary ruling; shipped as [#326](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/326).* Listed paths and headings are `file://` links. They are on by default and turned off with the `links` key. `REVOCATIONS.md` keeps what the decline said. `render::put_linked` pays the two real costs. First, an escape written through `Painter::put` is dropped, because `ratatui`'s `set_stringn` filters out graphemes that contain a control character. Second, a linked path measures 46 columns to `unicode-width`, which every width measurement in the shell reads. So the sequence is folded into the first cell after `put` has run, and the columns that cell then claims are overwritten with what the terminal will show. There is still one drawing route, and the width ladder still reads the true width.


**The clipboard is state this program does not own.** It is answered, not overridden: one bounded string, on a gesture the sheet names. The two that did not are in `REVOCATIONS.md`, both about mechanism, not principle. The sequence is written by hand, not taken from `crossterm`'s `osc52` feature. That feature is `dep:base64`, and using it would declare a crate this workspace does not.


**B10 — a hover highlight on what the pointer is over.** *Ruled 2026-08-15, session: no. Reversed 2026-08-16, session: yes. See §11.1.* ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123); the build is [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186), filed separately so the ruling did not wait behind an implementation.) The body stays here because a reader of a reversal needs to see how the conclusion survived both reasons it was given.


**Both reasons were absences that stopped being true. `RULINGS.md`'s two B10 sections are the only copy of the trail.** First, I1 was already paid: `?1003h` in `crossterm`'s mouse bundle wakes and paints the loop on every cell the pointer crosses, and the bundle cannot be narrowed portably. Second, *nothing would ever tell it to turn off* described one line not yet written in this repository: `EnableFocusChange`, one step in `TAKEOVER`. Inside the pane, cell-granular motion reports leaving row 5 as entering row 6. What remains is a multiplexer default, not a terminal gap. tmux's `focus-events` is off by default, so under §1's two panes the rung that clears a mark when the pointer leaves the window is absent until someone turns it on. For that reader, the residual case is ordinary, not exotic.


**So clearing the mark is a ladder, not a wall, and the residual rung is accepted.** §11.1 states the three rungs. The residual costs one mark left on the last target the pointer crossed. It pays for itself with a constraint: **a hover mark is the quietest thing that is still visible in its region.** A mark that quiet costs a glance nothing when it is stale. It corrects itself at the only moment that matters, because the mark exists for a pointer coming back.


**Here the pressed mark and the hover mark are one question.** B10 as written did not forbid [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166)'s lit step button. The clearing reason does not reach a gesture with an observable end. The confinement rule does not reach the scrollbar, because §11.1 rules that a button is chrome. The line that holds is mechanical. **A press ends** with an `Up`, and `Held::ends` covers a lost one, so its mark needs no ladder. **A hover has no end at all**, only a change of position. The difference is in what the program can observe.


**What B10 did reach is a hover mark on a list row, and that rule is amended, not dropped.** It said that facts about `vigia` itself are confined to the chrome. The amendment: **a mark about an input device is licensed inside a body region, on a surface the reader's pointer can act on**, bounded by the quietness rule. That keeps what the original protected, which is that the body is where the worktree is read. It also ends a confinement that would refuse acknowledgment on the one region where a reader aims at a one-row target. **It says *the pointer*, not *a click*, on purpose.** [#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177) is a drag in the diff body. A licence keyed to a click would first need someone to answer whether a press that begins a drag is a click. Hover's own scope is narrower than the licence and is stated in §11.1, not derived from it. So widening the licence does not quietly widen the feature.


**The losing branch is recorded first, because it will be raised again.** On an idle monitor the pointer is more often parked than aimed. A reader typing in the agent's pane leaves the mouse wherever it was, and a lit row then means only *the mouse happens to be here*. This is unevidenced either way, and labelled so. §2 says interaction is rare or never, and says nothing about where a resting pointer sits. Every mouse defect §11.1 records from use describes somebody **aiming**. The case for the mark is that acknowledgment before action is most of what "snappy" means with a pointer, and that it costs an `Option` and a style.


**B11 — a speaking rule: the diff's heading embedded in the border line between the regions.** *Ruled 2026-08-15, session: no, and on what the heading *is* rather than on when it is there.* ([#124](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/124).) The proposal puts the path in the rule, `─ src/engine/watch.rs ● ────`, and costs it as a relocation that gains one body row. **A heading here is not a line of text.** It is a `Painter::file_row` that draws the kind letter, the path, the pulse, the heat strip, the sparkline and the counts, through the one drawer §11.1 rules for both regions. Folding that into a border line has two branches, and each spends something already ruled. **Drop the glance elements**, and the pinned occurrence stops being what a list row is. **Keep them**, and the rule becomes a full glance row with dashes at the ends, which is not a rule. Neither branch is a relocation.


**If adopted, the row it reclaims is real, and it is there more often than the decline first said.** `App::jump_to` lands every jump on the file's heading, and follow is on before anything is touched (I5). So the row is there at startup, after every jump and on a followed tick. It is absent only after a relative scroll. [#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257) narrows the followed-tick clause: on a tall diff being actively written, follow lands inside a file whose busiest change is not drawn from its heading. **On a monitor being watched rather than read, the saving is available most of the time.** That is the strongest form of the case for adopting. In the frames between, the row under the rule is content. A rule that spoke would then pin a heading that is no longer otherwise on screen. That is a sticky heading, which is new state, not a moved row.


**B4 already answered stickiness the other way, by construction.** The pinned list exists because *the heading scrolls away, so you lose track of which file you are in*. `Body::split` makes that airtight: it sets `rule: list > 0`, so **the rule and the list are coextensive**. No state has a rule and no list, so no state has the question a speaking rule would answer. **This ruling does not rest on counting carriers.** An earlier argument said the caret and the footer's `N/M` already carry the current file. That proves too much. §11.1 allows restatement where it makes a map, not a second opinion, and the three carry three different facts: a path, a row, an ordinal.


**#125 does not reopen it.** That proposal turns the list into a left rail: the same two regions, rearranged. It says outright *"Not the borders — #124 owns the chrome question."* A left rail has no horizontal rule at all, so #125 does not reopen B11. It **dissolves** it. What can reopen this is [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) measuring the quantity the ruling turns on: how much of a real session is spent at a file boundary, and how much scrolled inside one. A reopening must answer the structural objection first.


**Declining costs the picture nothing.** `assets/preview.svg` draws the rule and the heading as two separate rows, so the artifact and the shell already agree. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::the_rule_separates_the_regions_and_spans_the_pane` fails if the rule ever speaks. It asserts that the rule row is the rule glyph repeated across the pane at 40, 64 and 120 columns, so a title in the line fails on the first character.

**B12 — where the keymap lives once it outgrows the hint bar: a gestures sheet drawn over the pane.** *Ruled 2026-08-17, session: yes, on a ground that is structural rather than spatial. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#167](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/167); the build is [#206](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/206).) `?` toggles a centred, bordered sheet of the keymap, composited over cells that are already drawn.


*It buys space* is the weaker half of the case. The stronger half is that **an overlay is the only form of cheatsheet that cannot move content**. §11.1 already rules that a transient thing must not move content: the footer refuses to grow for a notice, so a file that vanishes between being named and being read cannot jog the diff. Every answer that lives in the footer breaks that rule from the other side. More items need more width, more width brings the second footer line at more pane widths, and the second line takes a row off the diff. That route is **full**, which [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121) measured at eighty columns.


**The key is `?`, and the reference class agrees.** `btop`, `bottom`, `rtop`, `htop` and `less` all open help on `?` or F1. `?` is unbound here, so nothing is overloaded. **`h` is refused.** It is a vi *motion* everywhere else, and this pane has no horizontal scroll to give it.


**The sheet is not a mode, and that reconciles it with B4.** No key changes meaning while it is up: `q` still quits, `j` still scrolls the diff behind it, and a digit still jumps. B4 refused selection and focus because keys that mean different things depending on where focus sits are the defect. Here that is impossible by construction. The sheet is a third thing the pane can be **drawing**, not a third thing it can **be**.


**Its cost is that it covers the content this tool exists to show.** The answer is not that the cost is small. It is that the cost is **held by the reader**: a sheet is asked for and dismissed in the same second, while chrome that takes rows takes them from everybody on every frame.


**It is centred, not full pane**, so the header, the footer and the edges of the diff stay visible, and a reader can see the tool is still alive behind it. A full-pane sheet hides the state a monitor exists for.


**It is retained state, not a one-frame draw, and this is the constraint most likely to be missed.** The pane wakes on filesystem events, so an agent's write redraws the frame underneath the sheet. A sheet that lasted one frame would be dismissed at random by somebody else's build. It uses `r`'s mechanism: a private `App` field, a `Chrome` field the drawers read, and an `Action` the keymap resolves to. **It snaps** in both directions, because it answers a key and §5.3 has an act land whole.


**It degrades on two axes, and both floors live in the layout, not in the painter.** Width picks the spelling for the whole table. Height drops the mouse group before the keyboard group, then keyboard rows in their own drop order, leaving `a`, `f` and `?` last. **That order is a constant** ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)), not the table's own order. Dropping *from the top down* would have kept `q` and dropped `f` and `a`. The gestures nobody guesses outlive the reflexive ones, which is also why `f follow` is the hint bar's last rung. The floors belong to the body's split, for the reason [#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158) already paid for: a width floor that lived only in the drawer made a narrow pane reserve four rows for a region the painter then declined to draw. **Below its floor, `?` still toggles and nothing is drawn**, as `r` behaves on a pane too narrow for a rail. A key whose effect is sometimes invisible is better than one that is sometimes not a key.


**I1 is untouched.** A reader pressing `?` is not idle, so I1's *0 wakeups while idle* does not reach this in either direction, and §5.3 prices ink at zero. `crates/vigia/tests/budgets.rs::a_frame_under_the_sheet_holds_the_frame_budget` times a frame with the sheet up, on the pane where the two-column rung arrives. It asserts that the rung is the one it is named for, so it cannot quietly become a second copy of the gate above it. `a_frame_under_the_roomy_sheet_holds_the_frame_budget` sits beside it since [#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285), because the widest rung and the largest rung stopped being the same rung. A full-screen terminal takes the roomy rung, a short one falls to the two-column rung, and the roomy rung has the larger cell count.


**One row per gesture, with aliases spelled inside the keys cell.** The README's table already has that shape. It answers half of [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80)'s question, whether an item can express a relationship and not only a key: here it can, because here there is room. **The width is the ruling, and the height moves with the table.** A gesture costs the sheet a row and nothing else, so the row counts are not written here. §11.1 states the current figures, and `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs` holds them.


The wide spelling, at eighty columns:


```
┌─ gestures ──────────────────────────────────────── ✕ ┐
│ j  k  ↓  ↑              scroll a row                 │
│ Space  PgDn  PgUp       page                         │
│ d  u                    half a page                  │
│ g  Home  /  G  End      first / last changed file    │
│ n  →  /  p  ←           next / previous changed file │
│ 1  to  6                jump to that row of the list │
│ J  K  Shift+↑  Shift+↓  scroll the pinned file list  │
│ f                       follow the newest change     │
│ r                       show or hide the left rail   │
│ s                       one file, or the whole diff  │
│ a                       show or hide staged changes  │
│ w                       wrap a long line, or clip it │
│ c                       show or hide the note rows   │
│ Enter  Esc              send the note, or cancel     │
│ ?  Esc                  this sheet                   │
│ q  Ctrl+C  Ctrl+D       quit                         │
│ mouse ────────────────────────────────────────────── │
│ wheel                   scroll what you point at     │
│ drag a scrollbar        move that region             │
│ click a track           send that region there       │
│ click  ▲ ▼              one row, and repeats held    │
│ click a listed file     jump the diff to it          │
│ drag the diff           copy those rows              │
│ click a line number     open a note there            │
│ click  ✕                close the sheet              │
│ just point              it marks itself              │
│ Shift+drag              the terminal selects text    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```


**And the tight spelling at forty columns**, where one rung of the ladder is shown instead of described. The alias cells have dropped from the right, so `q  Esc  Ctrl+C  Ctrl+D` is `q`. The arrows survive on `j  k`, because they are the alias a reader is likeliest to try. **The mouse group fits here.** That took two shortened verbs ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286)), and it put the whole table inside the width I6 is named for.


```
┌─ gestures ────────────────────── ✕ ┐
│ j  k  ↓  ↑     scroll a row        │
│ Space  PgDn    page                │
│ d  u           half a page         │
│ g  /  G        first / last file   │
│ n  /  p        next / prev file    │
│ 1  to  6       jump to a list row  │
│ J  K           scroll the list     │
│ f              follow the newest   │
│ r              the left rail       │
│ s              one file only       │
│ a              staged changes      │
│ w              wrap long lines     │
│ c              the note rows       │
│ Enter  Esc     send the note       │
│ ?  Esc         this sheet          │
│ q              quit                │
│ mouse ──────────────────────────── │
│ wheel          what you point at   │
│ drag a bar     move that region    │
│ click a track  send it there       │
│ click  ▲ ▼     a row, held repeats │
│ click a file   jump the diff to it │
│ drag the diff  copy rows           │
│ click number   open a note         │
│ click  ✕       close the sheet     │
│ just point     it marks itself     │
│ Shift+drag     select text         │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```


**Both boxes are re-rendered from the shipped drawer, not kept by hand** ([#272](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/272)). The fields are derived from the content of the rows a rung draws, not chosen. A picture in this document is evidence, and §5.1 exists to stop evidence from lagging what it shows.


**The close control is the pane's first, and it inherits a vocabulary instead of inventing one.** [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166) settled that a button is a glyph in the chrome's own weight, told apart from what it sits on by shape, not colour. B10 settled that a click is always brighter than a hover. `✕` sits outside CP437, exactly as `▲` and `▼` do.


**Once the sheet exists, the hint bar no longer carries the keymap. It points at it.** That re-scoped [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80) to three items, and it made [#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147) cheaper to answer in the readouts' favour.


**B13 — how a pane too small for the whole table reaches the rest of it: the sheet pages.** *Ruled 2026-08-24, session: `?` advances, and the last page closes it. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286).) Before this, the height axis **dropped** gestures and nothing on screen said so. It was measured on `main` at 0.25.0 over every width from 20 to 140 against every height from 3 to 40. A 40 column pane reached **11 of 16** gestures at every height, and a pane of 8 rows reached 4. A help sheet that silently omits help is worse than a tight one, because the reader cannot tell it happened.


**`?` keeps exactly one meaning, and that is why paging wins.** Every route to a *scrolling* sheet breaks B12's reconciliation with B4. `j`/`k` scroll the diff and `Space`, `d` and `u` page it, so taking one of them back is the mode B12 refused. The only route that takes no key is the wheel. A mouse-only answer does not reach a keyboard reader on the narrow pane, where they are most likely inside `tmux` or over `ssh` with no mouse reporting ([#188](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/188)). A scrolling sheet would also need its own bar, which is furniture inside furniture. **Paging costs one key that was already the sheet's.** On every pane whose sheet is one page, `?` is the toggle it has always been.


**The third candidate is kept, not declined.** #286 offered *say so only*: a last row naming how many gestures the pane has no room for. On its own it does not meet the gate, because the gestures stay out of reach. As the page counter in the title bar, it is the honest half and costs no content row. A say-so on its own row would cost a row of the gestures it apologises for.


**What is promised, and where.** At forty columns and up, every gesture the sheet binds is reachable at every height at which the sheet draws. Forty is I6's own width. Below it, the mouse group has no spelling that fits.


**What it costs.** A pane under thirty columns now draws no sheet at all, where it used to draw four gestures of sixteen. The counter sits in the title bar. Every rung reserves the counter's widest spelling so it can never run into the close control, and that floor comes to thirty columns. A sheet that cannot say how much of the table it hides is the state this ruling ends. So drawing no sheet is the same answer `r` gives on a pane too narrow for a rail.


Every page of a pane is one box, with the unused tail blank inside the frame. That is how a paged surface looks:


```
┌─ gestures  24-25 of 25 ───────── ✕ ┐
│ just point     it marks itself     │
│ Shift+drag     select text         │
│                                    │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```


**B14 — whether the left rail arrives on its own or is asked for.** *Ruled 2026-08-24, session: it is asked for, on `r`, and the default is the strip above the diff at every width. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295).) [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) shipped the rail arriving on its own at 134 columns. For a reader that meant one number: at 133 the diff plans against 129 columns, and at 134 against 60. Widening a pane past a threshold nobody chose more than halved the thing this tool exists to show. **The derivation of 134 is not reversed or changed.** Both regions read one glance ladder, so splitting a pane costs each half what the whole had. The derivation now decides only where the gesture can be honoured.


**It is not a mode**, so B4's refusal does not reach it, as with B12's sheet. No key changes meaning while the rail is up, and the list is still not navigable. It is only somewhere else. B6 keeps it a keystroke, not a flag. Below 139 it changes nothing drawn and eats no gesture. The request survives a pane too narrow to honour it, so narrowing and widening again brings the rail back without asking again.


**The branch not taken: leave it automatic and add an opt-out.** It was rejected because it puts the cost on the wrong reader. The reader who has not thought about the rail is the one whose diff drops from 129 columns to 60. The gestures sheet is the discovery path either way. Since the sheet teaches the gesture whichever way it points, the better default is the one that changes nothing.


**`r` is a fourth gesture a reader cannot guess, beside `a`, `f` and `?`, and `SHEET_KEEP` keeps three**, so one of the four must drop first. `r` is given up at rank eight of `DROP_ORDER`. **The reason first written for that was false, and it is corrected here.** The rank that would drop it needs a width below thirty. Below thirty no sheet is drawn, so the reorder is **unreachable on every pane that draws**. It is a defensive ordering. `sheet_tables` asserts that the keep-set is `a`, `f` and `?`. The untouched order would have dropped `f`. If a future rung ever reaches that depth, `r` is the right one to lose, because it is the only one of the four that needs 134 columns.


**B15 — whether the left and right arrows move between changed files.** *Ruled 2026-08-24, session: yes, as aliases of `n` and `p`. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#296](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/296).) The capability already existed, so this is a naming fix. `Action::File` already moved the caret, and a reader who reached for an arrow got `Shift+↑`'s list scroll instead. Vertical keys move inside the diff, and horizontal keys move between files. One action has one arm, so the two directions cannot drift.


**What it spends, since the arrow pair is scarce.** The one feature that would want these keys back is a horizontal pan. §11.1 declined that as *"a key and a mode this spec does not name"*. B19's wrap toggle needs no arrows, so nothing open is blocked. If a pan is ever ruled in, it takes `h` and `l`, or it takes these back. This sentence records that, so the choice is not a hidden reservation.


**The sheet names the arrows at the wide spelling only, and that is arithmetic.** The tight keyboard key field is eleven columns and the arrowed cell is thirteen. Carrying the arrows there would cost panes of 35 and 36 columns their twelve gestures. Losing gestures to an *alias* for two columns of pane is the wrong trade. `q  Esc  Ctrl+C  Ctrl+D` already drops its aliases at the tight spelling.


**B16 — whether the diff can be pinned to one file, and what every other key means while it is.** *Ruled 2026-08-25, session: it can, on `s`, off by default, and every key keeps its meaning over a smaller subject. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#297](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/297).) It was asked for from the pane. An agent works inside one file for minutes at a time, and a continuous diff makes the reader hold two positions at once: where they are in the file and where they are in the changeset. Pinning removes the second. It is `f`'s sibling one step over. Follow decides which file the pane goes to. Pinning decides how much of the rest of the tree the reader's own scrolling reaches once it is there.


**It is not a mode.** No key changes meaning while it is on. The list is still not navigable: it is the same map, addressing a narrowed diff. Four questions needed a ruling, and one sentence answers all four: *one subject, narrowed*.


**`g` and `G` are the first and last row of this file.** The alternative keeps the changeset and jumps out of the pinned file. Then two keys would mean something different while the pin is up, which this ruling says does not happen. **`G` reaching a real last row, not a heading, is affordable here and nowhere else.** That looks like an inconsistency, so the reason is stated. Unpinned, finding the diff's last row means adding up every file's height, which is the read I4 forbids. Pinned, the subject is already diffed and the row is free.


**Follow still moves between files.** `f` and an automatic follow are explicit requests to be moved. The pin only limits what a reader's own scrolling reaches. So pinning is not a manual scroll, and it does not disengage follow. The two together are the gesture's best case, not an edge of it.


**The heading of the pinned file draws, as the block's first row.** It carries the pulse, the heat strip, the sparkline and the counts. A pane that dropped it as redundant would lose the glance elements on exactly the screen built to watch one file. **A sticky heading is refused, not declined.** It costs the diff a permanent row. It gives a second answer to a question the caret and the bold path already answer. It is also a larger claim than a gesture that only narrows what the walk reaches. If a reader turns out to miss the heading when it scrolls away, the question reopens as its own ruling, not as an amendment to this one.


**The scrollbar measures this file, and so does a drag on it.** Both read the pinned file's span, and neither counts the changed set. A bar drawn from one total and dragged against another agrees at both ends of the track and nowhere between, so a gate that asserts only the ends passes against it. The row-exact bar taught that, so the gate walks the middle.


**Toggling settles the position; it does not restore it.** From a screen already inside one file, a pin and an unpin is identity. From a screen that straddles two files it cannot be. The pin rests the pinned file's last row on the bottom, as a pager does, where the reader was usually already looking. **The resting is the walk's own back-up for a short screen. The pin allows it as a term in the guard, not as a flag it writes.** Left to whatever placed the position, the resting held for a reader who scrolled there and not for one who dragged the bar. Written as a flag, it leaked past the pin and moved a reader who jumped onto a short tail and toggled twice. A term has neither failure. The gesture promises that every pair of presses after the first is identity, so it is a toggle, not a ratchet.


**Two gestures the pane answered and the sheet did not name, ruled 2026-08-25, session** ([#288](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/288)). `README.md`'s Mouse table taught the **hover mark**, and `MOUSE` did not carry it. The sheet's own **close control** was named in neither. That was worse, because `✕` is a clickable control this element draws, and the only way to learn that was to rest a pointer on it. Both are rows of `MOUSE` now.


**A configuration file carrying the pin as a default was asked for on the same issue and is not ruled here.** It is a B6 question, with its own issue ([#306](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/306)), its own ruling and its own change.


**B17 — nothing shows staged files, so an agent that stages its own work empties the pane.** *Ruled 2026-08-25, reader: `a` draws the staged run beside the unstaged one, as two labelled runs with the staged rows marked, off by default. Shipped the same day. See §11.1 and §5.3.* ([#313](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/313).) Reported from the pane. §11.1's opening contract excluded staged content *by construction*. So a worktree with everything staged had no changes by this tool's definition, and plenty by `git`'s. B3's empty state said `no unstaged changes`, which was true of a clean tree and of a fully staged one alike.


**A union, not a swap. The reader ruled this; it was not derived.** Swapping which comparison is drawn costs one walk, one diff and no new channel, and it was the plan. It was rejected because neither view alone answers *what has the agent done since the last commit*, and a monitor beside an agent exists for that question. Both runs are drawn, unstaged first, and **a path changed on both sides appears in each**. They are two diffs of two different pairs of bytes. One `MM` row would show a third thing that belongs to neither.


**`a`, for *all***. `i` for *index* was the proposal while the toggle was expected to swap, and it adds.


**`a` walks on the keypress. No other key on this map does.** The other view toggles rearrange rows the frame already holds. This one changes what the frame *contains*, and `Frame::advance` otherwise runs only on a tick. Without the walk, pressing `a` kept the old frame until something was written. I1 is untouched, because this is work on a wake the reader caused, not a clock.


**The cost was measured before it was allowed to decide anything.** The second walk is `HEAD^{tree}` against the index. It reads no file, and it allocates one change per path and nothing per line. It costs **430µs against 2.15ms** for the index-worktree walk beside it on a 200-file fixture. A staged diff compares two object ids, so it spends no syscall. Its cached answer needs no fingerprint and no settle margin, because two unchanged ids name the bytes outright.


**It also fixed an older watch defect.** The `.git` arm admitted only `index`. `git reset --soft` writes `refs/heads/<branch>` and not `index`, so the staged run compared against a commit that was no longer `HEAD`. `HEAD`, `refs/heads/**` and `packed-refs` are watched now. `refs/remotes`, `refs/tags`, the reflogs and the object database still wake nothing, so a `git fetch` costs no frame. **An `index` write wakes only when an entry moved**: its path, object or mode, or its stage, assume-valid, intent-to-add or skip-worktree flag. A rewrite that moves none of them wakes nothing, as when an IDE refreshes stat data or a timed `git status` runs. *Ruled 2026-09-28, reader ([#538](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/538)).* **These files are watched wherever the git dir is.** A linked worktree, a submodule or a separate `GIT_DIR` keeps the git dir outside the tree. A linked worktree's common dir wakes only on its refs, because the other worktrees' `index` and `HEAD` are there too. *Ruled 2026-09-29, reader ([#548](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/548)).*


**The list names the runs and every row is marked. Each half does a job the other cannot.** The list names the runs, because a heading is worth a row in the map. Every row carries its own gutter mark, because the stream is a continuous scroll and a heading scrolled out of view says nothing. A separator in the *stream* was in the plan and is refused. Every row in the stream counts in the height arithmetic that `span_of`, `block_of`, `gap_rows` and `Frame::height` share. The diff's scrollbar, the scroll clamp and I4's counting bound are all computed from that arithmetic. A label row there would change the scroll model, only to repeat what the mark already says.


**B18 — what a monitor-class pane is allowed to draw in 2026.** *Ruled 2026-08-25, reader: the vocabulary expands to everything the research priced, ruled at the checkpoint rather than by rows. The builds are [#320](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/320) through [#326](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/326).* ([#318](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/318).) The evidence is `docs/research/318-drawing-vocabulary.md`: four surveys read from source, font sweeps, rendering probes on this machine, the tool's own mode matrix captured on a chaotic fixture, and vision boards mocked in a real terminal with the real font. The reader chose against the boards, and chose all of it: the delta-formula diff washes with word-level emphasis and a two-tone gutter, gradient ramps on the glance elements, the segmented chrome, the spliced sheet title and opt-in file icons, octant and sextant rungs decided by a terminal table, the terminal-derived adaptive theme with live dark/light, and OSC 8 links on paths. The theme foundation comes first, because every other row takes its hues from it.


**Three qualifiers come with the yes, in the reader's own words.** Every hue is a theme key, and nothing hardcodes a colour the theme cannot move. The default theme is the showcase, because the out-of-the-box look is the one that was ruled for. Everything is documented, and each key and rung lands with the ruling that introduced it.


**What was re-checked and held.** The green/red role rule reaches the sparkline: its gradient ships in the cyan family, because green two rows down means *added*. `Ansi16` as the default depth holds, and the matrix strengthened it. `COLORTERM` is a trustworthy positive signal, and its absence proves nothing. Windows Terminal is the canonical false negative. So an under-claiming default behind a strong positive override is the right shape. I1 reaches none of it: the adaptive theme's colour-scheme subscription is an event, not a poll, and every gradient is a lookup in a precomputed table.


**What stays parked. Parked is not declined.** Styled and coloured underlines are supported by every modern terminal, and no fact in this pane wants the channel yet. §5.3 reserves underline for the pointer, but that is a fact about the *plain modifier* as it is used today. The style axis is unclaimed. Claiming it needs a ruling that names the role first.


**Mode 2026 stays a progressive enhancement.** VTE recognises the mode and implements nothing, and VTE-based Ptyxis is now the default terminal of Fedora and Ubuntu. Nothing in the slate assumes synchronized output. Anything that adopts it detects it first.


**B19 — a long line has no route to its tail, and the ruling that clipped it named an alternative it also refused.** *Ruled 2026-08-26, reader: `w` wraps content rows as far as they go, off on launch. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.* ([#272](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/272).) Reported from the pane. §11.1 clipped a content row and marked it `›`. The next sentence refused the alternative as *"a horizontal pan, which is a key and a mode this spec does not name"*. Each half is defensible alone. Together they left a pane where **the tail of a long line cannot be reached at all**: not by wrapping, not by panning, and not by the terminal's own selection, because the takeover holds the mouse ([#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177)). It hurts most where I6 is aimed: the reference fixture goes from one clipped line to three at forty columns, and clips nothing at eighty.


**Half of the original reason expired and half did not.** *No part of a line is the identifying part* still holds. *A wrapped line moves every line below it* is about a reflow nobody asked for, and a gesture is a reader asking.


**The field was read on 2026-08-22, and `RULINGS.md` records it tool by tool**, because any reopening starts from it. The pane bounds the frame. Wrapping stops when the screen is full, so the work is one screenful however long the line is. A line taller than the region is marked, because scrolling steps over it whole.


**What the scrollbar counts does not change. This is the ruling most likely to be argued again.** Wrapping splits one quantity into two. A **logical row** is a row of the diff's own model. A **display row** is a row of the terminal. Before wrapping they were one number. The bar keeps counting **logical** rows: `Frame::height`, `view::rows_of`, `view::span_of`, `view::hunk_span`, `view::diff_rows`, `view::block_rows` and `view::Position` are untouched. **The thumb's height follows the screenful, and travel and position count the topmost row *drawn*.** *Amended 2026-09-03, reader.* **The clamp at the diff's last screenful gives rows back as a display offset and stores nothing**, so the end of a wrapped diff stays a place a reader can scroll out of.


**The alternative is refused on a fact, not on cost.** A display-row total is O(content) where today it is O(spans). It is also **circular**. `render::gutter_width` sizes the gutter from the largest line number *on screen*, so the text width moves as a reader scrolls. A wrapped height depends on the text width. The total depends on the wrapped heights. The drawn rows depend on the total. No order evaluates all four. So the unit that survives is the diff's own, which is also the one a reader can name: *how far through the changed lines am I*. Display rows exist **inside the viewport only**, bounded by the region, so I4 stays exactly where it was.


**Parked, not declined: word-boundary breaking** (Neovim's `'linebreak'`, `ov`'s `Alt+w`) and a cap a reader can move. Both refine a mode that is new, and neither is worth ruling on before anyone reads a wrapped pane for a week.


**The terminal's own selection is documented, not assumed.** One modifier was read from each vendor and named in `README.md` and in the sheet's mouse group, because a reader had no way to know it. It does not answer [#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177): it selects what was drawn, elided marks and all. **B20 answers it.**


**B20 — the shell drawing its own selection over the diff.** *Ruled 2026-08-29, reader: no. Withdrawn the same day, reader. Ruled 2026-09-02, reader: yes, whole rows in and whole lines out. Ruled 2026-09-03, reader: the gesture ends on the clipboard and the key goes. See §11.1.* ([#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177), from use; the build is [#380](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/380), and the release is [#386](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/386).) `REVOCATIONS.md` keeps the decline verbatim. The decline was true of the cells and never of the program, and §11.1 draws the copy from the row model instead. **So the span is rows.** Rows reach what the terminal's own bypass cannot, and the bypass still does half a line well. The cell-exact branch is refused on a fact: `render::gutter_width` sizes the gutter from the largest line number **on screen**, so no column has a stable byte behind it. **B4 does not reach this.** The wash lasts as long as the button is down, so nothing is remembered once the gesture ends. No key changes meaning, and there is no `y` on the map. **The key reached one thing a pointer could not**: a whole path at the forty columns I6 is named for. A drag over a file heading now reaches it there, gated by `crates/vigia/tests/select.rs::a_heading_row_sends_the_path_and_not_the_drawn_label`.


**B21 — a note on a diff line reaches the agent over MCP.** *Asked 2026-09-04, reader, from use. Ruled 2026-09-05, session: yes, as asked, the box with its bounded mode. See §11.1.* ([#414](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/414); the builds are [#415](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/415) to [#421](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/421).) A pointer on the gutter of a content row turns the line number into an icon. A click there opens a bordered box under the line and pushes the diff down. The reader types one note there for the agent in the other pane. The agent reads the note over MCP, anchored to the file and line, and marks it *seen*. It resolves the note with a line that the reader watches arrive before the note leaves. `btop` carries a signal menu: a monitor may hold one bounded act on the thing it watches without becoming a reviewer. Here the thing beyond the diff is the agent, and the act is one sentence to it. The pane is a mailbox and calls no model, so §4's non-goal stands. A pane with no notes draws today's frame.


**The box is the pane's first mode, and its bounds are the ruling.** B4's reason is a focus that persists over the diff. The box exists only while a hand is in it. It opens on a click and never from the keyboard. It is the frontmost drawn thing. Esc closes it. A click anywhere else closes it and sends nothing. The wheel still scrolls behind it, and no key outside it changes meaning. Enter sends, and empty text sends nothing. Alt+Enter is a newline where the terminal reports it, and v1 is one paragraph. The editor is `ratatui-textarea`, named in §6. It arrives and leaves through `tachyonfx` under §5.3's licence, armed by the click and done when done.


**The anchor is `(path, side, line number, the line's text)` for each end of a range inside one hunk, its first line and its last.** A note on one line has one such anchor. Every frame resolves it against the diff of every file the walk draws, and of the file's other run when it has one. When a number no longer carries its text, the pane looks for the text within a few rows, so an edit above does not orphan the note. §11.1 carries what each outcome draws. A range draws what its last line draws. It is also *changed* when its first line's text no longer matches. If its first line is not found, it shrinks to its last. No state loses a note. A line holds one open note hanging under it, and ranges can overlap. A stored note with no first line reads as a range of one. *Ranges ruled 2026-09-28, reader ([#540](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/540)).*


**The store is one directory per worktree under the reader's state directory**: `$XDG_STATE_HOME/vigia/` or `~/.local/state/vigia/`, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\vigia\state\` on Windows. It is keyed by a hash of the canonical workdir `Worktree::discover` resolves. `vigia-core` computes the hash, so the pane and the server cannot derive two keys for one tree. Each note is one file, written by temp-and-rename on Enter, before any effect runs or any socket is posted to. A status change rewrites the file the same way. No file is ever open in both processes at once. The store is not in the worktree, because of §11.1's wake-loop mechanism. It is not in `.git`, whose byte-for-byte claim is worth keeping. N panes on N projects never see one another's store, and two panes on one worktree share it. The pane watches its own store directory: an event source, never a timer. The unsent draft lives in memory.


**`vigia mcp` is the CLI's second word: a stdio JSON-RPC server hand-rolled over `serde_json`, which is already here.** It has seven methods: `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, `resources/list` and `resources/read`. It also sends `notifications/resources/list_changed` when the store changes. The tools are `notes`, `resolve` and `reply`. `notes` marks each note it lists *seen*. Each listed note carries its range, `first_line` and `first_text` beside `line` and `text`, and the text of every line in the range. `resolve` needs a line, because the departure shows that line. There is one resource, `vigia://notes`. The server takes its worktree from `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, which Claude Code sets for a stdio server under either scope from v2.1.238, read 2026-09-07. If that is not set, the server falls back to the working directory. A project-scoped server is given the project as its working directory, and a user-scoped one is not. Every other client is served by that fallback. A client that starts the server elsewhere points it at the tree with its own `cwd` setting. The handshake's instructions name the worktree served, or say why there is none, so an agent reads which store it has before its first call. *Ruled 2026-09-29, reader ([#450](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/450)).* `rmcp` 3.2.0 was refused on a measurement that `RULINGS.md` holds: **28 crates** that this lock file lacks, `tokio` among them, in a program with no runtime. So switching later is a decision, not a discovery.


**Enter also reaches the running session when one is registered.** Claude Code exports `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_SOCKET` and `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN` to hooks before `SessionStart` runs. A `SessionStart` hook runs `vigia mcp register`. It records that session's id, socket and token in a registry beside the store. The registry is keyed the same way and sits outside the directory the pane watches, so a session starting is no wake. `SessionEnd` clears it. On Enter, after the store write, the pane opens each registered socket on a thread of its own. It sends `{"type":"auth","token":"..."}` first, then one `{"type":"user","message":{"content":"..."}}` frame carrying the note, its anchor, three lines of context and the id to resolve it by. Then it closes. A message to an idle session starts a turn. This is on with nothing to enable from v2.1.224, and from v2.1.234 on native Windows, read 2026-09-05. The frame's shape is documented nowhere, and it was probed before it was built, 2026-09-06. The frame names the session it was registered for, and the peer checks it, so a registration left behind delivers nowhere. The frame carries no reply address, since `vigia` binds no inbox. The way back is `resolve` and `reply`. Every registration takes one message, and a refusal forgets none. **Nothing is written back**, so the footer says **sent** when a socket took the line, never that it arrived.
