It says **noted** whenever nothing live took it: none registered, none took it, or a fifth post while four are in flight. *Ruled 2026-09-29, reader ([#450](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/450)).* The pane is not the session's child and nothing comes back. So the pane cannot see whether the session acted on the note, held it behind a permission prompt or dropped it. A note on a removed line carries its anchor alone. Without a socket, the note stays in the store. A `UserPromptSubmit` hook running `vigia mcp pending` carries into the next prompt what each note waits on: a read, a resolve, or the reader. Channels stay a preview and are not taken.


**Invariants and gates.** I1: the store watch is an event source, and every effect ends inside its duration. I3: the soak types nothing and asserts zero files, and pruning is gated over a store of resolved files. I4: one hash probe per drawn row, and the store is read per wake, never per frame. I5: a pane with no notes draws today's frame, which the snapshots already gate. I6: the icon, the rows and the box at forty columns inside `legibility.rs`'s sweep. I9: a screen full of notes with the box open, against the 16ms budget, from a warm p50 of 5.17ms on 2026-09-03. §11.1's writes rule is amended with its two gates. §10's residue gains `✎` and `▎`, with CP437 stand-ins recorded beside them and no ladder yet, since nothing detects that loss today.


**Also ruled with the feature**: the box over mark-only. The state directory over `.git`. Hand-rolled over `rmcp`. Rows drawn and `c` to hide them. Enter sends. Enter also posts to a registered socket. `✎`, `▎` and `↳`. The departure holds the agent's line for a beat. The prune after the departure. The draft in memory. An adrift note withdrawn through the agent or from its line once the file returns, and no footer gesture until use asks for one. A note on an edited line stays until resolved.


**B22 — whether the pane remembers a toggle, and where it writes it.** *Asked 2026-09-10, reader, from use. Ruled 2026-09-11, reader: yes, and the config file stops being a thing you have to know exists, open and edit. See §11.1.* ([#494](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/494); the builds are [#511](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/511) and [#512](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/512).) `m` opens a **config menu** over the pane, and one row under a rule turns remembering on. The objection was never the write. It was the history a reader cannot see, and the menu makes that history visible. **Remembering is opt-in and off by default**, so a setting stays a starting point for anyone who never opens the menu. **There is one store, and it is the file B6 already names.** A second store would be invisible state again. A wholesale rewrite would destroy the reader's comments, so the pane replaces only the lines it owns. A remembered toggle *becomes* a configured one when it is written, so nothing tells the two apart.


**A setting is remembered when it is a boolean about how the pane behaves. Where the pane is looking is not a setting.** The rule can be checked: the file's keys are `bool`, `hide` is a value, and standing is its own type. **`f` is in, and B6's I5 exclusion is overruled.** `REVOCATIONS.md` keeps what it said. A *file* that turned follow off would configure away an invariant for a reader who never asked. Remembering continues what this reader pressed, with follow explicitly on, and I5's own words are *correct with zero interaction*. The file goes to **ten keys**: `follow` and `persist` join the eight. **Six the design left open are ruled 2026-09-11, session.** A short pane **scrolls** with the caret. `?` pages the sheet, which has no caret. This menu has one, and B13's reason against dropping rows applies to a settings menu even more. **One overlay at a time**, because both are the frontmost drawn thing. **Nothing moves the box**: it is placed in the pane, not in the body. **Remembering defaults off**, and **a failed write is one alert that turns it off**. The toggle itself still applies. **The menu says nothing about `hide`**: the header already counts what the pattern kept out.
